## E118｜降频不省钱，分批才省钱（2026-10-25 bm-c r1261）
- 研究点：r1260 把这本书的瓶颈定成「换手率」（年换手 15.8 次、每次换 8 个名字），并给出「1% ADV 纪律只能装 94 万」的规模上限。很自然的下一个动作是降频省成本——本轮证明这条路是负的，而另一个动作是正的。
- 做法：①**周期阶梯**：同一打分面跑 10/20/40/60 日调仓，成本两档（flat 8bp 与 r1260 的参与度定价），对照组一律换成本节拍的池内等权（不同节拍共用基准会偷换对照）。②**分批（stagger）**：同一张 top-8 名单按行内排名切 1–4 与 5–8 两个袖子，各带一半资金，第二袖的调仓时钟错开半个周期（10 根 bar）——实现不碰引擎，`F.run_next_open` 的网格来自 `dates[::rebal]`，把第二袖的 `start` 往后推 10 根即等价相位错开。
- 发现：①**20 日是峰值，两侧都掉**——10 日 5.44%（年换手 26.4 次，被成本吃掉）、20 日 7.62%、40 日 5.42%、60 日 3.56%（省下的成本补不上 alpha 半衰期）。「降频省钱」在这本书上实证关闭。②**分批 p90 单日参与度 10.63%→6.60%（-38%），年化只让 0.32pp@1,000万 / 0.15pp@5,000万 / 0.02pp@1亿**；按「p90 不超 1% ADV」纪律，可装规模 94 万→约 152 万（+61%）。③**分批的换手率反而升**（15.76→18.87 次/年，分半后各袖选择不同、换手更高）——因此 r1260 的「瓶颈是换手不是集中度」修正为：**换手是总量约束，集中度在同一总量下仍是一根独立杠杆**。④参与度随周期拉长反而升（9.77%→12.83%）：调仓次数少了但每次换的名字更多，**「年换手」与「单日冲击」是两个数**。
- 工程：①分批两袖必须用同一张排名面按行切（`method="first"` 保证并列确定性），否则名单会在两袖间漂移、结果不可复现；②混合两袖净值按当日**活跃袖子**归一权重（`df.notna()` 门），否则相位偏移袖子在 burn-in 期把整本书悄悄减半；③成本等价必用 selftest 钉（`ImpactCost(k=0)` 与 flat 8bp 逐点相等），且**必须先断言真的成交了**（`turn>0`）——MIN_HIST 未满足时两条曲线都是 1.0 的直线，会假绿（本轮首跑即踩到，已加一条断言腿）。
- 证据：`scripts/etf_book_turnover.py`（`--selftest` 19/19）｜`results/etf_book_turnover.json`｜`research/ETF_BOOK_TURNOVER.md`
- 下一站：部分成交档（>1% ADV 的腿只成交 1%、余量留旧仓吃逆回购）与分批相位数/错开幅度的单面 sweep，再把分批接回容量阶梯重出盈亏平衡 NAV。
- 2026-10-25 22:2x bm-c r1261 探索

## E119｜对照段必须宽度匹配，容量要按「谁贡献的」分解（2026-10-25 bm-c r1262）
- 研究点：一个结构性改动（错峰分批）测出「代价 −0.32pp、容量 +61%」，但它的对照组用的是引擎默认 5 名书，被测组是 8 名（4+4）。宽度变了、相位也变了，一个数被写成另一件事的功劳。
- 做法：①两侧钉死同一宽度（`STAGGER_TOP=8`/`SLEEVE_TOP=4`），并加 selftest 腿断言「两袖并集恰等于 top-8」与「参考段 n_hold==并集名数」，任何人改宽度都会红；②容量不手抄，按 p90 参与度线性外推逐线机 derive（`NAV_ref × 1% / p90`），并输出归因分解（相对基准书的 % 拆成「摊宽贡献」与「错峰贡献」，两者相加才是总增量）。
- 发现：匹配宽度后错峰在零冲击口径下是 **+0.27pp**（不是 −0.32pp），只在 5,000 万以上转负；+61% 容量里 36% 来自摊宽、18% 来自错峰。同时一条反向证据：分批后单日破 1% ADV 的决策次数 77→139，p90 降 15% 不等于更容易守纪律。
- 判据：任何 A/B 结构对比先问「两边的名称数/持仓数/样本数是否相同」；不相同就不是 A/B，是两个实验叠在一起。
- 证据：`scripts/etf_book_turnover.py`（`--selftest` 27/27，L7 宽度腿、L8 容量 derive 腿）｜`results/etf_book_turnover.json`（`corrections_disclosed` + `readings.p90_capacity_nav`）｜`research/ETF_BOOK_TURNOVER.md` 更正节
- 下一站：把宽度当作独立轴 sweep（5/8/12/16 名，各自错峰与否），给出「宽度-容量-收益」三维面，而不是继续在 8 名上打转。
- 2026-10-25 23:0x bm-c r1262 探索


## E120｜宽度不是一根杠杆，是随资金量变号的一族杠杆（2026-10-25 bm-c r1263）
- 研究点：r1262 只测了一个点（5 名 vs 8 名），得出「摊宽 36% 容量、代价 0.74pp」，但一个点回答不了「到底几名最划算」。本轮把宽度当独立轴扫开（5/8/12/16 名）并各自叠错峰与否。
- 做法：①容量逐格机 derive（NAV_ref × 1% / p90，每格用自己 p90，禁继承）；②效率指标 = 相对 5 名基准的容量涨幅 ÷ 让出的 pp（收益不降则记 None=免费容量，禁除以零）；③盈亏平衡 NAV 在网格括号内按 **NAV 线性**插值（成本对 NAV 线性，不是对 log NAV），无换序返回 None；④错峰机器与成本对象全部 import 复用，两个面不能各自漂移。
- 发现：①**10M 口径 5 名最赚（5.58%），12 名单袖效率最高**（+96% 容量只让 0.49pp = 196%/pp）；②**但 50M/100M 口径 12 名单袖反超**（3.48%/2.28% vs 5 名 3.11%/1.29%）——摊宽把参与度成本按 NAV 线性压低，降得比深位信号的衰减快，所以「宽度最优」是资金的函数：8 名在 4,289 万追上 5 名、12 名在 3,279 万；③**错峰只在 8 名成立**（唯一一处同降回撤 -14.2→-10.8% 且收回成本），12/16 名错峰回撤恶化到 -21%，因为它用「更分散的相位」换「更差的选股集中度」，名数一多这笔交易就亏；④容量≠收益的反例：装得最多的 12 名错峰（214.7 万）只赚 3.79%，是面上最差组合之一。
- 工程：①**同一张表里两列成本不同 = 假对比**：首跑错峰「flat」腿实际走了 ImpactCost，与 priced 列逐字相同才暴露——正法是把成本对象按 leg 显式传入，并加一条功能性自检（ADV≪NAV 时 priced 必须严格低于 flat、flat 腿不得漏出参与度字段）；②F.metrics 的回撤键是 maxdd 不是 max_dd，写错得整列 null 而脚本照跑绿；③任何「哪个参数最好」的面先出效率指标（单位代价换回多少），否则只能在格子里挑绝对最高收益、看不见代价。
- 证据：scripts/etf_breadth_sweep.py（--selftest 36/36，L8 成本同源功能腿、L9 交叉点插值腿）｜
results/etf_breadth_sweep.json（surface + 
eadings.crossover_nav_vs_ref）｜
research/ETF_BREADTH_SWEEP.md
- 下一站：宽度 × 打分面（深位 9–12 名由哪些因子撑住）+ 部分成交档（>1% ADV 只成交 1%）+ 12 名单袖叠 8% 波动目标。
- 2026-10-25 23:3x bm-c r1263 探索

- 复算补注（r1263 值守窗）：同一面另有独立第二实现 scripts/etf_book_breadth.py（--selftest 11/11），flat 与容量列逐格相同=交叉复算通过；它多列了错峰对 12/16 名的增量（-1.09pp / +0.80pp，回撤双双恶化到 -18% 档），坐实「错峰只在 8 名成立」。扩展只改主面 etf_breadth_sweep.py。
- **E121 (r1264, bm-c) — a research config must ship as a placeable sheet produced by the same engine that priced it.** A backtest row that says "11.5%/yr, Sharpe 1.13" is not yet a product: nobody can act on it. `scripts/etf_book_production.py` re-runs the exact settled configuration through the research engine (`etf_robust_pf.open_to_open_equity`) and then emits today's target weights, CNY amounts and 100-share lots at a stated capital, so the number on the card and the sheet under it cannot drift apart. Three rules the sheet taught: (1) reproduce a previously published cell and assert equality before trusting a new wrapper -- here n5+8%vol reproduced 11.49%/SR1.132/-10.56%, which is what proved the wrapper was not a reimplementation; (2) report the decision grid as *bars until the next decision*, never as an invented future date, because the panel ends at the last bar; (3) instrument names are not in the panel -- the shipped `data/basic/etf_list.csv` carries exchange prefixes (sz159998) while prices are keyed on bare 6-digit codes, so the join must strip the prefix and fall back to positional columns (its Chinese headers do not survive a hand-copied encoding guess).

- **E122 (r1265, bm-c) a watermark must carry its source, and a delta verdict must be content-first.** A content digest proves *what* you read, never *where* you read it, so a watermark can be unreproducible at origin and still look well-formed (the ghost-sha family). Store the source trio beside the fingerprint -- v2:<owner/name>@<commit40>/<bytes>#<sha256> -- and compare repo -> commit -> bytes -> digest. Two rules the migration taught: (1) **commit-first comparison manufactures false deltas**: the pre-existing triple reported changed=true on both group files this window while the digests were byte-identical, purely because origin's tip had moved on an unrelated commit; identity of the blob is the only honest basis for 'did I miss anything', the source trio is for reproducibility. (2) **verify the STORED quad against its own commit** (re-fetch + re-hash): that is the only check that can see a phantom, and no forward comparison can. A not-reproducible watermark must refuse the advance (exit 3) with an explicit, receipt-logged repair path -- never silently overwrite a ghost into a fresh key, and never let a ghost be read as 'nothing new'. Evidence: scripts/d19_watermark.py --selftest 30/30, results/_r686bmb_d19_check.py, research/D19_WATERMARK_V2.md.

- **E123 (r1267, bm-c) a backtest becomes a track record only when a ledger can refuse to rewrite it, and the ledger cannot ride the card's fill basis.** `scripts/etf_book_forward.py` seeds at a stated bar (2026-10-23, nav 1.0) and appends one mark per new bar; every run re-derives the published marks and a mismatch FREEZES the ledger with exit 3 (`--rebase` is the single logged escape, the --repair-stale pattern). Two rules the build taught: (1) **an incremental state machine is not evidence until it reproduces the research engine exactly** -- the accrual matches `etf_composite_pf.run_next_open` at maxdiff 0.00e+00 over 1648 bars with identical turnover (1.277108), and that leg carries an ACTIVITY assertion because two flat 1.0 curves also "agree" (r1261 family); (2) **price the basis gap instead of inheriting it** -- the production card fills at the open of the very bar whose close made the score (one bar of lookahead, `etf_robust_pf.open_to_open_equity`), so on the same 12-name / 8%-vol config the card shows 9.47% / SR 0.98 / DD -11.8% while the honest next-open basis shows 13.53% / SR 0.94 / DD -16.2% (-4.06pp full, -2.84 IS, -5.90 OOS). Turnover is IDENTICAL (0.934) on both, which is the tell: it is a one-bar WINDOW ALIGNMENT shift, not two portfolios, so the gap is neither alpha nor fraud -- it says the headline is sensitive to which single bar the hold starts on, and neither number may be quoted as edge. Evidence: scripts/etf_book_forward.py --selftest 11/11, results/etf_book_forward/LEDGER.json, results/etf_book_forward/BASIS.json.

- **E124 (r1268, bm-c) a de-leverage that only moves the reported cash field is not a de-leverage — and its equivalence leg must be run at the same scale it claims to record.** The r1267 forward ledger averaged the book's per-name returns UNWEIGHTED, so the 8% vol target shrank the published `cash` to 29% while the return path stayed fully invested: 13.53%/yr reported vs 8.42%/yr actual (final nav 2.371 vs 1.734, +36.8% overstatement), and the error survived a 1648-bar "exact equivalence" leg because that leg ran with vol_target=None, where every weight is exactly 1/12 and a weighted and an unweighted path are the same function. How to apply: (1) an equivalence/ruler leg must be run at the CONFIGURATION UNDER TEST, not at the degenerate one where the difference cannot appear (r1261 family, second instance) — the shipped form pins `r_scaled == s * r_unscaled` bar by bar against the engine-exact unscaled curve, which fails the moment weights stop reaching the maths; (2) the referee has to sit on the same FILL BASIS: `open_to_open_equity` fills at the open of the bar whose close decided the book, so it can never certify a next-open accrual (it still shows the old 13.53% and would have blessed the defect) — a 1-bar basis shift is measured separately, never absorbed into an equality gate; (3) a "disclosed" gap can be a real leak: the cash leg earning zero cost 0.55pp/yr of NAV (29.3% average cash × 1.88%/yr repo ladder3), so park cash on the repo ladder rather than calling zero carry a caveat. Corrected consequence: the card-vs-ledger basis gap is +1.05pp (card 9.47% vs honest 8.42%), NOT the -4.06pp that E123 published — the sign of that gap flipped when the defect left the ledger. Evidence: scripts/etf_book_forward.py --selftest 19/19, results/etf_book_forward/CARRY.json (+CARRY_fee_free.json), results/etf_book_forward/BASIS.json regenerated, research/ETF_BOOK_CASH_CARRY.md.

- **E125 (r1269, bm-c) a capacity ladder priced on a fully-invested book answers the wrong question once the book de-levers — and the cash leg is a function of the sleeve, not of the ETF.** r1260 found break-even vs the pool at 50M on a 5-name, always-invested book. Re-priced with the repo ladder switched on, the same 5-name book moves by +0.10pp/yr at EVERY scale and its break-even does not move (5.3% average cash), while the 12-name 8%-vol book (6.9% average cash) gains +0.13~0.15pp and break-even moves 50M -> 100M. Rules: (1) never quote a capacity number without the sleeve and the cash fraction it was priced at — the same engine carries 2x for one book and 1x for the other; (2) when two cost models must meet, make the shared one a CALLABLE `cost(prev, new, d)` (the ImpactCost contract) and prove the scalar path is bit-identical (maxdiff 0.00e+00) before trusting a new ladder — otherwise you get two parallel cost implementations drifting apart; (3) a de-leverage is only recognised by the return path, so it must be exercised at the configuration under test (E124 sibling). Evidence: scripts/etf_book_capacity_carry.py --selftest 8/8, results/etf_book_capacity_carry.json + _n12.json, research/ETF_CAPACITY_WITH_CARRY.md.

- **E126 (r1270, bm-c) an unswept parameter pair must be priced before its headline is quoted — and a leverage cap at 1.0 means a "vol target" systematically undershoots the vol it names.** The production card's only two un-gridded numbers were the vol target (8%) and its estimate window (60 bars); sweeping them 5x3 at two sleeve widths separated the axes cleanly: (1) the TARGET is a risk dial (12-name Sharpe 0.980/0.978/0.956 across 6/8/10% while CAGR moves 3.76pp and drawdown -9.5%..-12.9%), so a CAGR spread across targets proves nothing and raw return must never referee a risk knob — the referee is whether risk-adjusted quality moves; (2) the WINDOW is load-bearing (Sharpe 1.045/1.039/0.978/0.841/0.817 at 30/45/60/90/120 bars, spread 0.228, with no drawdown penalty for the faster ones), so the published 60d is a convention sitting on a slope, not a plateau — which may be recorded but not re-picked on the same sample, because chasing the best cell of a fresh surface is the exact fitting the sweep exists to detect; (3) because scale = min(1, target/v) is a de-lever-only cap sized on the PICKED names' backward vol (momentum picks are recently calm), the delivered risk exceeds the label structurally: 8% target -> 10.18% realised at 12 names (10.5% at 5 names), and no window fixes it. How to apply: quote a de-levered sleeve by its DELIVERED vol next to its target, never by the target name alone; before trusting a parameter that no grid covers, price its row and its column, and read each axis on the metric that can actually referee it (return for exposure, Sharpe/DD for a risk knob). Evidence: scripts/etf_vol_target_sweep.py --selftest 8/8 (legacy-path maxdiff 0.00e+00, activity leg, higher-target-holds-more-risk, window-inert-without-target, short-history degrades to no overlay, hand-built surface arithmetic), results/etf_vol_target_sweep.json, research/ETF_VOL_TARGET_SURFACE.md.

- **E127 (r1271, bm-c) extracting a helper can silently strip the caller's scope, and a deliverable nobody executes is not covered by green legs.** r1270 pulled the panel build out of etf_book_production.run() into a shared prep_panel(), but the caller kept reading cls[p] and len(cols) -- names that now existed only inside the helper. The production card, i.e. the ONLY artefact a human can place an order from, died with NameError at the order-sheet step and stayed dead for a full round while selftest, smoke 57/57, S6 and every audit stayed green, because no leg in the repo ever called 
un(). Two rules: (1) when a refactor moves a variable's home, the guard is not a unit test of the helper -- it is a cheap end-to-end invocation of the entry point that produces the deliverable, so the sheet path (backtest -> weights -> legs -> card text) executes on a synthetic panel in ~1s with prep_panel/load_names/OUT_DIR patched to a tmp dir (a real-panel leg would cost a minute and nobody would keep it in smoke); (2) register the expensive real-panel selftest wherever the repo's other exit-code contracts live (smoke_test.py), otherwise 'the card is broken' and 'the repo is healthy' are the same reading. Sibling of E120: a wrapper that is never re-run against its own deliverable rots exactly like a backtest that is never re-derived. Evidence: scripts/etf_book_production.py --selftest (4 new legs incl. the synthetic end-to-end run + card assertions), smoke_test.py product leg, results/etf_book_production/CARD.md.

- **E128 (r1272, bm-c) "we reported it, we did not explain it" is a debt -- and the obvious
  culprit is often worth one arm of measurement before it becomes the story.** r1270 left the
  8%-label-delivers-10.18% gap attributed to the de-lever-only cap; one arm with `vol_cap=3.0`
  (same estimator, same 60d window, same 12 names, only the cap differs) kills that story:
  letting the overlay lever up delivers 0.18pp MORE vol, so the cap is worth 0.0pp of the
  2.18pp gap and only 12% of decisions ever sat at scale=1.0. The gap decomposes into (a) the
  60d backward estimator itself, 12.25% mean against 13.62% realised over the very holds it
  sized (58% of 83 decisions under, median 0.94) -- and the mechanism is visible in the score,
  which carries vol_60 with a NEGATIVE sign, i.e. it prefers recently calm names, so a trailing
  vol is biased low for exactly the book it selects; and (b) 1.02pp of intra-hold clustering,
  the difference between mean(scale x hold vol)=9.16% and the account's lived 10.18%. Rules:
  (1) a mechanism you name in a research note must be the mechanism you measured, otherwise
  the next round inherits a plausible falsehood -- and the cheap test is to REMOVE the
  suspected constraint and see whether the effect moves; (2) decompose all the way to the
  residual and label it (here: clustering), because a decomposition that stops at 'mostly the
  estimator' invites a multiplier patch that would then be fitted to the same 1648 bars;
  (3) an implied parameter (6.29% label would deliver 8%, ratios 1.272/1.246/1.306 across
  full/IS/OOS) is reported with its IS-OOS spread and NOT adopted -- a stable ratio is still
  not licence to re-label, and re-labeling would erase the clustering term that only appears
  in lived returns. Also: the engine gained `vol_cap` and a `diag` sink with the legacy path
  proven bit-identical (maxabs 0.00e+00, turnover 0.934317 both arms) plus an ACTIVITY leg
  that the uncapped arm actually diverges (2.7e-02) -- an invariance leg without an activity
  leg passes on two identical no-ops (E124 family, third instance). Evidence:
  scripts/etf_vol_delivery_audit.py --selftest 4/4, smoke 59/59 (new product leg),
  results/etf_vol_delivery_audit.json, research/ETF_VOL_TARGET_SURFACE.md r1272 section.

## E125 -- an annualised vol label and a per-hold vol target are two different numbers (2026-10-26 bm-c r1273)
- Question: r1272 explained the 8% label vs 10.18% delivered gap but left it reported. Can it be
  fixed CAUSALLY -- at each decision knowing only the estimator and the holds that already
  CLOSED -- without fitting on the same sample?
- Method: `WalkForwardCalibration` (scripts/etf_vol_calibration.py) settles a hold exactly when
  it completes (hold at decision i runs open i+1 -> open i+20, so its realised vol is knowable
  at the next decision, never earlier), then `scale = min(cap, target / (est * m))` with m from
  >=>6 completed holds. Two estimators of m: median of realised/est, and rms of realised/est.
- Finding (the reusable one): **delivered annualised vol is the ROOT-MEAN-SQUARE of the sized
  segment vols, not their mean.** Measured on the published arm: sized mean 9.16%, sized rms
  10.19%, measured 10.18% (identity error 0.01pp). So of the 2.18pp gap the estimator's MEDIAN
  bias is worth ~1.06x and the dispersion of the realised ratio (rms/median 1.22) is worth the
  rest: a rule made median-unbiased makes the typical hold 8% and annualises ~9.8%, by
  construction. A median calibration therefore moves 10.18% -> 9.91% only; the rms calibration
  lands 8.08% full / 8.22% OOS. The label and the delivered figure measure different things.
- Engineering: (1) causality is testable, and the test is cheap -- re-run the calibrated arm on
  a panel with the last 260 bars removed; every shared decision must produce an IDENTICAL scale
  (here 70 decisions, maxdiff 0.00e+00). That leg is worth more than any code review of the
  hook. (2) An invariance leg needs an activity leg beside it or two identical no-ops pass (E124
  family again): `ratio_hook=None` is bit-identical to the published overlay (maxabs 0.00e+00,
  turnover 0.934317 both), so the divergence leg (constant ratio 1.25 -> maxabs 2.19e-01) is
  what proves the hook is wired. (3) State the adoption rule BEFORE the run and evaluate it
  mechanically: here `vol_within_tol` PASS, `calibration_active` PASS, `ratio_stable` PASS,
  `sharpe_not_worse` FAIL (0.946 vs 0.978 = -0.032 against a 0.02 tolerance) -> verdict HOLD,
  and the calibration is reported, not adopted, with the failing leg named.
- Evidence: scripts/etf_vol_calibration.py --selftest 12/12, smoke 60/60 (new product leg),
  results/etf_vol_calibration.json, research/ETF_VOL_CALIBRATION.md, engine change `ratio_hook`
  on etf_robust_pf.open_to_open_equity (legacy path bit-identical).
- Next: the label must be restated rather than tuned -- "8% per hold (annualised ~10%)" or move
  to rms sizing at the next scheduled review; that is a GM/CEO policy call on paying 0.03 Sharpe
  plus 2.2pp CAGR for a literally true risk label with 2pp less drawdown.


## E126 -- two live faces of one book are a width dial, not a contradiction (2026-10-26 bm-c r1275)

- Situation: the production book publishes two executable-looking faces -- an order ticket
  sized at the factor default (5 names, beta 0.20 overlay + GC001 cash) and a production card
  sized at 12 names (8% vol target). Both were labelled 'the production book', so an operator
  had to guess which one to follow and nobody had priced the gap.
- Method: hold the ticket's own accounting fixed (next-open fills, 10bp/side, |dk|*8bp on the
  overlay, GC001 on the remainder, beta 0.20) and move ONLY the name count, reusing the
  existing engine calls plus the ticket's own metrics function so the columns are comparable
  by construction.
- Finding: width is a monotone risk dial, not a defect -- 5/8/12/20 names give CAGR 11.05/9.73/
  9.53/8.28%, Sharpe 1.353/1.437/1.560/1.628, maxDD -6.01/-5.04/-4.17/-2.94%. 12 names vs the
  executable 5 costs -1.52pp/yr for +0.207 Sharpe and 1.84pp less drawdown.
- Rule: when a repo carries two 'live' faces of the same strategy, price the parameter they
  disagree on before reconciling them by opinion; if the disagreement is monotone, publish the
  curve and let the operator pick the point instead of silently re-picking the default.
- Evidence: scripts/etf_book_width_audit.py --selftest 4/4, results/etf_book_width_audit.json,
  research/ETF_BOOK_WIDTH.md.
- Landed (same window): the ticket now carries the comparison
  (`etf_book_order.width_disclosure`, card width read from the card's own artefact, delta on
  both the JSON caveats and ORDER-<asof>.md) because a measurement nobody meets at the point
  of action is just a note; both book legs are wired into `Tools/s6_chain_bmc.py` so the
  forward accrual no longer depends on a session remembering to call them.
- Trap found while wiring it: the selftest fixture proved only the *pointer* shape of
  `results/etf_book_production/latest.json`, and the live run then printed "width disclosure
  unavailable" -- latest.json can be the card itself. Fixtures must mirror the live artefact
  shape, and a leg that exercises the real files (leg16) is what catches the difference.


## E127 -- a band statistic is blind to a common shift (2026-10-26 bm-c r1276)

- Situation: E42 published the calendar cost as a band (max - min of the wide-vs-ladder
  spread over four decision calendars, 24bp at cost x1) and read a 2bp widening at cost x3
  as evidence that turnover explains it. E79 then showed the same face was still charging
  unadjusted share-split prints at weight 1/n, because the calendar legs had no raw hook.
- Method: extract the published face into `_calcost_face(raw, cal)` (pure extraction, proved
  by calling both paths and comparing the full JSON -- identical), then replay the SAME face
  from a repaired universe under two screens and publish one delta row per published cell,
  with the untouched face left in place.
- Finding: the repair moves every calendar up together (+96bp at the integer-ratio screen,
  +204bp at the all-adjacent screen), so the band SHRINKS 24 -> 20 -> 17bp while the level
  triples. The widest single cell moves 217bp. The fee-channel verdict (widening at cost x3)
  survives in all three arms, so E42's reading was right and its magnitude was not.
- Rule: a band (max - min across arms) cannot see a shift that hits all arms alike -- read
  every published band beside its level column, and every screened band beside its unscreened
  twin. "The band is small" answers "are the calendars different from each other", never
  "is the calendar choice cheap".
- Trap guarded in the selftest: an invariance leg (identical arm -> all-zero deltas) passes on
  two no-ops, so the leg carries an activity arm that must produce a known non-zero delta.
- Evidence: `python scripts\\e39_panel_census.py screencalc` (selftest 246/246),
  results/e42_calendar_cost/COST-20261023-screened.json,
  research/digests/DIGEST-20261026-e81-calcost-screened.md.

## E129 -- an order artefact may only print a book decided on the engine's grid (2026-10-26 bm-c r1277)

- Situation: three faces of one ETF production book -- card, order ticket, forward ledger --
  each printed a different set of names for the same cutoff. Two of them ranked the panel's
  last bar; the engine only re-decides every 20 bars, so both were handing a human a
  portfolio the book never held (card vs ledger: 1 name of 12 shared) while the card said
  HOLD, and the scale printed on the card (1.0) was sizing a book the ledger held at 0.7008.
- Rule: any artefact a human can order from must read its book off the engine's own decision
  row (diag), not off a re-ranking of the latest bar. A grid that is computed only to print
  a label is a verdict with no consumer -- the E128 shape, at the money surface.
- Second half of the rule: prove the two faces agree on the *artefact*, not on the intent.
  The ticket now publishes book_decided_at and card_agreement{ticket_is_prefix_of_card}.
- Cost of the defect: an operator following the sheet trades the full round-trip of 11 names
  every time the chain regenerates it (the chain runs every round).
- Gates: production selftest 11->14 (decision-row identity + activity leg that the off-grid
  ranking really differs), ticket selftest 16->19 (grid bar + card-prefix agreement).

## E130 -- two published faces of one book must be bridged knob-by-knob, and the bridge must close (2026-10-26 bm-c r1279)

- Situation: the production book published 9.47%/0.98/-11.8% (12 names, 8% vol rule, card
  engine, full history) and 11.05% (5 names, beta 0.20 + GC001 ladder, ticket engine, since
  2024). Two rounds had priced the width difference INSIDE one engine; nobody had priced the
  1.58pp between the two published numbers, so an operator had to guess which face he was
  reading.
- Rule: build the bridge as a single walk from face A's headline to face B's, each step
  moving exactly ONE knob (window, universe, fee, accounting basis, rule, width, cash, cost),
  and make the step sum land on face B's number exactly. A decomposition that does not close
  is a residual nobody has named; a decomposition that closes is an audit of both faces.
- Two constructions carry the weight: (1) `@0bp` twins of each fee-bearing arm, so the fee
  step is measured instead of assumed; (2) a selftest leg that PROVES the two engines'
  conventions disagree on the same synthetic panel (1.370766 vs 1.398458, rel 1.98%), so the
  "basis" step cannot silently become a no-op. Without (2) a refactor that harmonises the two
  engines would zero the largest term and still print a closing bridge.
- Reading: the largest single term was the window (+8.61pp, not skill) and the largest cost
  was executable accounting (-5.17pp, next-open fills vs open-to-open accrual). Any
  "the ticket beats the card" claim that omits the basis term is measuring the calendar.
- Evidence: `python scripts\etf_book_bridge.py --selftest` (10/10), full run 2.1 min,
  results/etf_book_bridge.json (32 arms), research/ETF_BOOK_BRIDGE.md. Re-run reproduces
  every number byte-for-byte apart from `ts`.

## E131 屏幕只动它算得动的那一项：残差项与原始项是同一腿，别把两臂的故事分开讲（r1280 bm-c 实弹）
- 病理：E42 的 size-neutral 结论是「vs-ladder spread 是 size tilt，剩下的 alpha 为正」。这句话里有两项——原始 spread 与 size component——它们不是独立读数：
aw = alpha + beta*tilt，所以屏幕动一项时，另一项必然跟着动或不动。不先把这条恒等式写出来，就会把「原始 spread 从 -58.5 变到 +278.9」读成「策略变了」。
- 正法：分账三列一起发——raw / size component / alpha，加上恒等式校验（±0.25bp 容差，逐臂必须成立）。哪个臂动了哪一项立刻可见：本批 size component 三臂 -220.0/-214.0/-211.1bp（9bp 不动），raw 三臂 -58.5/+126.2/+278.9bp（+337bp），位移全部落在 alpha 项。
- 实测反转：2024_now alpha 161.5 → 490.0bp、全窗 260.6 → 489.5bp（严格臂），每一臂每一代都为正——**结论方向活着、量级翻倍，而「最新代原始 spread 为负」这个已发布事实是拆分打印的伪影**。带宽 164.9 → 22.7bp 塌缩而水平 243.9 → 478.6bp 上移（E81 的带盲位移律在同族第二面复现）。
- 配套纪律：①屏幕单据按代计数并带名字（E82）——本批 135 个修复打印里 109 个（81%）落在活代，污染集中在最靠近头条的那一代；②屏幕同时改了宽簿与 tilt 成员，所以 alpha 的移动是两腿净额，禁归因给单腿；③重建而非引用已发布面：无屏臂在本腿内重建，先证明默认路径与 HEAD 逐字节恒等再跑新臂。
- 自证：scripts/e39_panel_census.py screenspread（selftest 246→252，+6 腿含「每臂独立 delta 列，后到臂不得覆盖先到臂」）+ results/e42_spread_size_neutral/NEUTRAL-20261023-screened.json + research/digests/DIGEST-20261026-e81-spreadsize-screened.md
- 2026-10-26 r1280 bm-c（E81 第 2 腿·捕获律 O-20261002-2100 依法生成）


## E132 数据缺陷能改写面目的「符号属性」而不动水平——且必须连回归量一起换账面（r1281 bm-c 实证）
- 场景：E43 稳健面（4 日历 x1/x3 费率 = 8 格）判「size-neutral spread 新纪元只在月末日历存活（4 格为负，min -22.4bp）」。这 8 格读的是 as-traded 面板，宽基簿里一次整数比拆股打印就按 1/n 权重计价——正是 E81 在日历面/价差面已经屏掉的缺陷。
- 做法：整格网格逐臂重建（引擎曲线 **和** 成员 tilt 回归量一起），面板打印整数比修复后与未筛面并列发布（不重写任何已发布面）。
- 实测：未筛 4/8 格为负（min -22.4bp）；整数比屏下 8/8 全正（min +164.4bp）；全邻接屏 8/8 全正（min +316.9bp）。符号翻转恰好落在四格非月末日历（me_p1/mid15 两费率档）——即「日历脆弱」这个判词整个是由那几笔修复打印扛着的。最大单格移动 cost_x1|mid15 -15.4 → +171.5/+329.9bp。
- 判据：①「水平变化」与「符号翻转」是两类结论，低计数数据缺陷可以完全不动水平的量级感却把符号面翻掉；②带宽 40.4 → 36.6/35.9bp 收窄而水平涨十倍＝E81「带对共同位移盲」判例第三次复现，收窄≠稳健；③屏必须同时换引擎与回归量，否则回归是拿另一个账簿去解释这个账簿。
- 默认路径证明：加入 opt-in 
aw_all 参数后，用未触动的 loader 重跑 
eutralrob，八格与新件 unscreened 臂逐格恒等（diff 为空）。
- 验证：python scripts\e39_panel_census.py screenrob → results/e43_neutral_robust/ROBUST-20261023-screened.json；selftest 252 → 257（+5 腿，含每臂各自 delta 列与「未测格保持 None 不折价为零」）；research/digests/DIGEST-20261026-e81-leg3-robustness-screened.md
- 2026-10-26 r1281 bm-c（E81 第 3 腿·取宝律 O-20261002-2100 收口步）

## E133 修复本身也要定价：生产账面的 9.47% 里有 1.17pp 是拆分打印修复给的，而修复的计数行在说谎（r1282 bm-c 实证）
- 情境：生产卡片（1499 只无债 ETF 池里 12 名、20 bar 网格、10bp/边、8% 波动规则、收盘决策次日开盘成交）印 9.47%/0.978/-11.77%，它跑在「±35% 打印被压平重标尺」的面板上；E81 线已把日历成本/尺寸中性 spread/稳健格三面都对着这个修复审过，唯独**钱的面**没审过。
- 做法：把原始（未修复）孪生面并排发布，同一宇宙同一打分器同一配置（n=12/R20/10bp/8%），另加「旧版修复」臂作定价反事实。
- 实测：修复值 **+1.17pp 年化 / +0.121 夏普**（IS +1.11pp、OOS +1.26pp 同向），而**回撤 0.00pp 不动**——四条真正进过账面的打印全部落在别处设定的回撤里，修复没有美化风险面；宇宙内 164 条越阈打印，**只有 4 条进过账面**（12 名 20 bar 决策），它们就是全部 2001bp：2020-11-06 `159966` -68.3%（3.151:1）与 `159967` -73.0%（3.708:1）同日同族占 59%，另两条是 2:1（`513500` 2022-03-30、`512800` 2025-07-07）。
- 判据换血：**整数比启发式只认得出 47%（77/164）**，四条在册里的三条会被误判成「复牌或错印」；**水平持久性才是定谳**——取打印后 5 个有价收盘看是否留在新水平（164 条全部持久、0 条回弹）。比是证据，持久性才是判决。
- 顺手抓到的缺陷：`A.repair_flips` 用 `close.pct_change()` 量收益，**pandas 3 已不再跨 NaN 前填**，紧跟停牌 bar 的拆分打印返回 NaN——重标尺仍然把它压平了（NaN 归 0% 日），但**计数行漏了**（报 134，按前有价收盘实测 168），且该 bar 上真实的小幅波动被一并吃掉。修=按 `close.ffill()` 量（E74 律新面），卡片重出后印 168；另加**自证门**：修复后面板残余越阈打印必须 = 0（两面均已 0）。
- 验证：`python scripts\etf_book_split_audit.py selftest` 20/20（含「修复后无残余」「gap 邻接拆分计数」「整数比漏判 3.15:1 而持久性抓住」「回弹打印判非持久」「名义宽度不冒充活动」）＋卡片一致性门（审计臂与卡片 n12_vt08 全指标差 <5e-5）；结果 results/etf_book_split_audit/AUDIT-2026-10-23.json、research/digests/DIGEST-20261026-e133-book-split-repair.md。
- 2026-10-26 r1282 bm-c，E133 收口。承接 O-20261002-2100 捕获律。

## E134 筛法裁决要看「水平保不保得住」而不是「比值像不像整数」：普查尺度上持续筛与全修逐格相等（r1283 bm-c 实证）
- 情境：E133 在片子上证明「整数比启发式只认 47%、水平持续性能认全 164 个」，留下一个明账问题——**新裁决规则会不会改动任何已发布的普查线**？两种结局都可能：若有打印回弹，持续筛=比全修更保守的安全臂；若没有，持续筛=有证据支撑的相等，而整数门才是错的臂。此前无人量过。
- 实现：`panel_screen.level_persistence(closes, events, bars=5, tol=0.20)`（取打印后 5 个有价收盘的中位漂移，|漂移|≤20% 判持续）成为共享 loader 的一等公民，`mode="neutralise_persist"` 只修「水平站得住」的相邻打印、不看比值；默认调用方零改动；挂进 `screenspread`（E81 leg2）让已发布的生存 claim 在三道门下同读。selftest 26→31 腿（3.15:1 换股被持续筛修好而整数筛视而不见；回弹打印被拒且计数；普通整数拆股照修；窗口不足判未决不猜）。
- 实测（size-neutral spread，bp/yr，1269 名，cutoff 2026-10-23）：未筛 326.4/161.5/260.6（水平均值 243.9，带 164.9）；整数门 371.6/340.2/369.2（355.9，带 31.4，修 65 打印/65 名）；全修 467.3/490.0/489.5（478.6，带 22.7，修 135 打印/129 名）；**持续筛与全修逐格相等**（467.3/490.0/489.5、478.6、22.7、135）——它评估了全部 135 个相邻打印并拒了 **0 个**。
- 判读：①答案=**不改动任何已发布普查线**，宽臂本来就对；②真正错的是整数门：它丢掉 135 个真修复里的 70 个（52%），代价是水平 −122.7bp、带宽 22.7→31.4——「保守分支」在这里保护的从来不是风险而是欠修；③相等是**单面板单切口的测量不是定理**：只要有一个打印在 5 个收盘内回弹两臂就分开，故 `reverting_adjacent`/`undecided_adjacent` 随件发布，引用持续臂必带拒修计数。
- 验证：`python scripts\panel_screen.py` 31/31；`python scripts\e39_panel_census.py selftest` 257/257；`python scripts\e39_panel_census.py screenspread` 四臂约 35s；results/e42_spread_size_neutral/NEUTRAL-20261023-screened.json；research/digests/DIGEST-20261026-e134-persist-screen-census.md
- 2026-10-26 r1283 bm-c，E134 收口。承接 O-20261002-2100 收口步。


## E136 自指日历分不清假日与哑火：拿交易所官方日历做独立参照，才敢说「今天没有新增 bar」是合法的（r1286 bm-c 实证）
- 情境：2026-10-26（官方交易日，收盘已过）ETF feed 未发当日 bar。全链 42 条腿全部 rc=0：update_daily 打 probe_no_new、etf_book_forward 打 NO-OP last_bar=2026-10-23、forward 账本停在 1.0。所有腿的「预期完整 bar 日」都是从我们自己的面板/由面板生成的日历推的——**数据自己当裁判，永远判自己合格**。
- 实做：scripts/exchange_calendar_audit.py（两脸一跑）——①data/exchange_calendar.csv 官方交易日（sina tool_trade_date_hist_sina 一次请求，8797 天，覆盖到 2026-12-31；刷新闸=覆盖不足 30 天或距上次 6h，常态零网络；写入一律与盘上**取并集**，日历属历史不可缩）②
results/data_lag_audit.json 把官方日历与本地面板逐日对账，判 in_sync / source_lag（就是今天这一根还没发） / panel_gap（更早的官方日也缺=feed 真哑了），并直接写明对 forward 账本的影响。
- 口径要点（全部有 selftest 钉子）：①**15:30 边界**——盘前不要求当日 bar，否则每个上午都报假警（L4）；②**别拿最老的 CSV 当面板落后度**——data/daily 有 1,725 个文件，其中 2 个停在 09-04/09-22，min() 会把「1 天发布延迟」读成「10 天停摆」（首跑就踩，见 L9）；正法=维护中队列（尾 bar 落在最新尾 bar 的 5 个官方日内）+ 覆盖率 ≥90% 才算该日已覆盖；③冻结队列单独披露，不静默平均进状态。
- 验证：python scripts\exchange_calendar_audit.py selftest 9/9；实跑 verdict=source_lag（expected 2026-10-26，active 1723/1725，effective 2026-10-23，missing ['2026-10-26']）；已接 S6 链 cal_audit 腿（43 腿）+ smoke 两条腿（selftest 契约 + 活面已发布）。
- 读法：**「没有新 bar」不是状态，是待解释的观测**。有官方日历才有资格说这是延迟；没有就只能等。
- 2026-10-26 r1286 bm-c，承接 O-20261002-2100 收口步。


## E137 人要看的那张单子必须自己带「我乘的是哪一天的 bar」——面板内的陈旧度腿对整块面板一起过时是瞎的（r1287 bm-c 实证）
- 情境：r1286 造了官方日历审计，但下游要人动手的两件实物（可执行订单单 TICKET/ORDER.md、生产卡 CARD.md）没接。它们本来就有陈旧度腿 staleness_stats，可那条量的是**面板内部**各标的相对决策 bar 的陈旧——整块面板一起晚 N 天时它读到的是 0/415（满分干净）。同一跑里两条线并排：`staleness: 0/415 picks (0.0%)` 与 `feed: stale_feed | ticket bar 2026-10-23 vs official 2026-10-26 | lag 1`。
- 实做：把纯函数 CA.ticket_feed_status(日历日序列, 单据 bar, 官方期望 bar, 审计 verdict) 放进出日历的那个模块（一份实现两处消费，禁第二份拷贝），live 包装 CA.live_ticket_feed_status() 喂盘上日历+面板尾。四态：current（lag=0）/ stale_feed（lag≥1 且非 panel_gap）/ feed_broken（panel_gap 优先于普通落后=停发不是延迟）/ unknown（没日历或审计抛错，**显式不是新鲜**）。
- 口径要点（每格一条 selftest 钉子）：①lag 只数官方交易日——周五 10-23 到周一 10-26 是 1 不是 3（leg21）；②feed_broken 压过普通 lag（leg22）；③unknown 永不读成 current（leg23）；④警告必须落到人打开的那张 md 上，不能只躺在 JSON（leg24）。
- 落地位：ORDER-<asof>.md 加「数据新鲜度」块；CARD.md/卡片 JSON 加 feed_status + 陈旧时置顶 caveat「FEED IS BEHIND: ……The names are that old, not a fresh call.」。首跑 2026-10-26 17:52 两处同读 stale_feed / lag 1（sina 17:33 仍未发当日 bar=源头滞后，不是书的判断）。
- 验证：etf_book_order --selftest 24/24（19→24）；etf_book_production --selftest OK；exchange_calendar_audit selftest 9/9。
- 读法：**「我的输入是新的」不是一个可以被下游默认的前提，是一条必须自己算、自己印在单子上的断言**。内生的陈旧度腿只在同一块面板内有意义，跨面板/跨日历的一律要先有独立参照。
- 2026-10-26 r1287 bm-c，承接 O-20261002-2100 收口步。

- **容量问题要在钱真正所在的那个点定价（r1295·参与率上限前沿）**：任何按 ADV 上限报的「这本书能装多少钱」，1% 只是个惯例而不是定律——真相是一条前沿：上限放松 → 能装的本金线性变多（同一 p90 读数 ×5 就装 ×5），但成本曲线随之抬升，所以年化要**在每个上限自己的天花板 NAV 上重新跑一遍**，不能从相邻的 NAV 阶梯档外推（阶梯档和天花板是成本曲线上的不同点）。本司实测（rules 臂 = k=0.20/beta + 逆回购现金腿）：1%/2%/5% of ADV → 可部署 1.53M/3.05M/7.63M 元，年化 7.28%/7.06%/6.64%，折合年赚 11.1万/21.6万/50.7万——即「接受最坏那笔占到 5% 成交额」= 用 0.64pp 年化换 5 倍本金、4.6 倍年利润；同口径下 flat 臂（k=1）只装 0.94M/1.88M/4.70M、年赚 6.6万/12.7万/29.2万，即现金腿在同上限下值 1.6-1.7 倍资金与利润。工程口径两条：①上限是「p90 决策」的，最坏那笔必然超过它，所以 worst 必须与 cap 并列打印，禁只报 cap 合格；②前沿每档的 NAV 由 `cap × REF_NAV / p90分数` 派生（分数不是百分数，读错一次就小 100 倍），并加 selftest 腿钉住「放宽 5 倍上限 = 恰好装 5 倍本金」「1% 那档 == 单独报的 1% 天花板」。
- 验证：`scripts/etf_book_capacity_rule.py selftest` 19/19（17→19：加两条上限前沿腿，总数改为计数而非手写）；产物 `results/etf_book_capacity_rule.json` 新键 `capacity_frontier_by_cap`；已接 S6 链腿 `etf_book_capacity_rule`（75.4s，无网络无判据）。
- 2026-10-27 r1295 bm-c（本节 O-20261002-2100 收口触发）


- **测第二本书前先证控制臂就是在跑的那本（r1296 bm-c）**：衡量「再加一个结构能不能多赚」时，A 臂必须是上线配置的同一个面——本例首稿用 E.build_panel（全宇宙含债/货币）建了一本“没人跑的书”（CAGR 6.74%），而交付书走 etf_book_production.prep_panel（类别限定 equity/commodity/convertible + 168 格拆盘修复，CAGR 9.49%），同代码同因子差 2.75pp。不报错、自洽、相关性也合理，只是读数全部沿染。正法=控制臂直接 import 生产侧面构建器（面同源），禁凭相象重建一个相似面。同批判负：ETF 横截面短期反转（-5d 排名）10员/R10/VT8 全窗 -3.67%/夏普 -0.373/回撤 -33.6%，与主书相关 0.461（OOS 0.252）、名重叠 7.2%——形状对但赔钱，不做第二本书（正身 research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1296-second-book-reversal-neg.md + scripts/etf_book_pair.py）。
- 2026-10-27 r1296 bm-c（本节 O-20261002-2100 收口触发）

- **「第二本书」一族要在一次跑里全筛，且对比必须在同一风险下做（r1297 bm-c）**：情形＝产能研究（r1295/r1280）把「把同一本书做大」封死后，唯一的杠杆是「第二本低相关、正期望的书」，而每轮只试一个机制＝一个机制一轮。方法（scripts/etf_book_bakeoff.py）＝①对照臂必须**就是出厂书**（走 etf_book_production.prep_panel()，并把计算值与已发布 production card 的 n12_vt08 CAGR 做 0.5pp 容差断言，r1296 的教训变成一条 selftest 腿）；②每臂同面板/同次日开盘成交/同 10bp/同 8% 波动目标，只换打分与节奏；③必带随机打分对照臂并公布候选数 N；④每臂给「与主书相关 + 名重叠 + 50/50 混合」三面。实测五种 ETF 横截面机制全被拒：赚钱的两臂与主书相关 0.62-0.75、名重叠 87.9%（同一笔交易），不同交易的（反转 7.2% 名重叠、随机 45.8%）都赔钱；随机臂年化 -0.27% 说明主书 9.5% 是选股不是 beta。判据＝横截面里没有第二本书，继续在 ETF 面筛没有意义。
- **改换宇宙才拿到第一个可用候选（r1297 bm-c）**：期货主力连续 10 品种（AU/IC/IF/IH/IM/RB/SC/T/TF/TS，2008-01-09→2026-10-26）做时序动量，风险平价 8% 波动（|w|≤2、gross≤3）、1bp/边、一根 bar 执行滞后：tsmom60 年化 1.37%/SR 0.27、tsmom120 1.84%/0.36，与 ETF 主书相关 0.078/0.045，随机符号对照 -5.63%（符号有内容）。**关键坑＝固定权重混合会答错题**：50/50 混合把 10.2% 波动的主书降杠杆，混合年化 6.5% 只是杠杆幻觉（r1291 同族）；按主书自身波动归一（10.17%）后＝主书 9.60%/SR 0.990、混合 tsmom60 10.36%/SR 1.060、tsmom120 10.14%/SR 1.040/回撤 -10.2%。诚实面：long-only OOS 8.03% 好于择时 2.65%，即这条袖子的价值是「多商品/债券多头暴露 + 近零相关」而不是择时；未建模乘数/移仓/保证金/资金成本，故只是「值得建一条真袖子」的理由，不是可交易数字（正身 results/futures_tsmom_probe.json + T-2026-10-27-188-P1 + research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1297-second-book-bakeoff-and-futures-sleeve.md）。
- 2026-10-27 r1297 bm-c（本节 O-20261002-2100 收口触发）

- **期货/合约化品种的候选，先算「资本地板」，再谈年化（r1298 bm-c）；** r1297 的期货 TSMOM 袖 +1.37% 是连续权重算术的产物，不是能下单的东西。同一批 10 品种主力连续（8% 波动目标、|w|≤2、gross≤3）三件套实测：①**资本地板 808 万元**＝逐品种 `一手名义额 / 该品种 max|w|` 取最大（不是平均）；地板之下有些品种连一手都拿不到；②¥100 万账户只落地 **17%** 的目标名义额、与连续权重日收益相关 **0.43**、CAGR **0.15%**；¥2000 万 0.967/1.08%，¥5000 万 0.996/1.30% 才逼近连续版 1.37%——**小账户上「信号」根本没进账本，回测里那 1.37% 从未存在**；③摩擦归因：年换手 52.95 次 × 单边佣金，1bp 即拖 0.53%/年，而按最小跳动算的真实移仓成本只有 0.14%/年（T 每手 1.0bp、RB 每笔 6.6bp，逐品种差 15 倍）——**杀这个袖的是换手不是移仓**，靠「给移仓留余量」救不回来；保证金不是约束（均值 6.6%、峰值 16.3% 资本）。How：任何合约化品种先出「资本地板 + 落地比例 + 换手×成本」三行再谈收益；另外别向 `stats()` 要它不产的键（本窗 `oos` 列第一版恒为 None，加一条 selftest 腿钉住分割真出数）。
- 2026-10-27 r1298 bm-c（捕获 O-20261002-2100 收口步）

- **等风险混合检验的是夏普，不是相关性；宽 ETF 宇宙上的宽度趋势永远不进现金（r1299 bm-c）**：ETF 面板 1499 只、同一 open-to-open 引擎、10bp/边、8% 波动目标下做时序（自身趋势符号）趋势四臂（60/120 日 x 等权/逆波动权）：年化 +0.92%~+1.74%、SR 0.15~0.24、与主书相关 0.67~0.72、等风险 50/50 混合只有 +5.3%~+5.8%（主书 9.49%）。两条可复用判据：①**cash days=0.0%**——宽宇宙里永远有上百只 ETF 在过去 N 日上涨，所谓「趋势过滤器」从来不触发空仓，实际持仓常年 150~162 只＝被 8% 波动帽压住的分散多头（beta），不是风控；要拿到真正的空仓态，信号必须落在**指数级或单标的**上；②等风险混合下第二腿能不能加分＝它自己的夏普能不能接近主书，相关性低只是必要条件（SR 0.15~0.24 对 0.98，无论多正交都只会把收益腰斩）。构造完全不同（逐名趋势 vs 横截面排名、150 只 vs 12 只）却仍 0.67~0.72 相关＝同一资产类别里方向暴露压倒构造差异。正身 scripts/etf_book_tsmom.py（selftest 13/13）+ results/etf_book_tsmom.json + research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1299-tsmom-second-book-neg.md。
- 2026-10-27 r1299 bm-c（捕获 O-20261002-2100 收口步）

- **四个活面不是四本书：先按规则分组，再排名（r1300 bm-c）**：同一本 ETF 书在仓内能长成四张「活面」（可执行票据 / 生产卡 / 前向账 / 错峰账），但把面数当书数是错的——判据是**规则三元组**（持仓数 × 调仓频率 × 宇宙含不含债）。r1300 实测：card 与 forward 同为 12 名 / R20 / 含债 = 一本书（4 面塌缩成 3 本）；staggered 10 名 / R10 / 去债 = 另一本书，它的 20.77% 不能读成「同一本书做得更好」（同 r1291/r1296 口径律）。发表口径一律答「**在某资本上限下一年赚多少钱**」= min(资本, 该书自己的 ADV 上限) × 该书自己的年化：12@vol 在 1% ADV 约 173k、5% ADV 约 866k 元/年，5M 与 50M 资本的答案是同一个数（上限咬死）——所以「加资金」不是增长手段，「放过更大比例的 ADV」才是。跨规则排名一律不出（脚本内 suppress + selftest 钉住）。
- 2026-10-27 r1300 bm-c·遵循 O-20261002-2100 收口步

- **自检不许跑全量面板：它会把 S1 门锁死（r1300 bm-c）**：etf_vol_delivery_audit.py --selftest 原先调 P.prep_panel()（1499 只 × 1649 bar）再用同一引擎跑三臂，>10 分钟零输出——r1298 与本轮开头的 smoke 都是被它卡死的（smoke 打印到第 59 腿就静默）。修法=自检用 40 只 × 1500 bar 的合成面板（种子固定），四条守卫原义不变，5.3s 跑完。How：任何 --selftest 若 import 生产面板/全样本引擎，一律换合成 fixture；判据是「selftest 秒级 + 零网络」，跑不动的自检等于没有自检。
- 2026-10-27 r1300 bm-c·遵循 O-20261002-2100 收口步
- **同族多臂先证「是不是同一个对象」，再谈几票证据；一致到小数点往往是同一个东西的多个名字（r1167 bm-b·E102）**：情形＝E99 一次发布五个臂（unscreened/neutralise_int/gap1/gap2/gap5），E101 只把其中一个挂在端网格上。要答「这五臂是五次测量还是一次转变的五个名字」，光比 delta 不够——改看两层：①同一端网格下逐臂的**零端计数**（步=11/12 零且跨度全落在纪元末段；效应=0/12 零且移动分散；无效应=12/12 零），②比 delta 更下层比**宇宙编辑量**（臂只修打印不删标的，名字数相同也可判同一对象）。实测：gap1/gap2/gap5 在六个纪元×账簿格上逐端完全相同（max−min=0.0），1271 名里 gap1 与 gap5 只差 **1 个**名且该名从不进任何格，gap1 与 gap2 编辑集完全相同（74 名）——即三列是同一个对象，gap 限 1/2/5 在该面板上**不可辨识**；neutralise_int 编辑 65 名是另一个对象（2017_2020 me 为 +52.5 小步 vs 缺口族 −745.9 大步，2021_2023 两账簿 12/12 零 vs 缺口族走 249.6/291.4bp）。判据：①「同族 N 臂互证」在报出前必须打印编辑计数，格级一致与同对象不可分；②零也是读数，引用 0.0 必带零端计数，否则「无效应」会被读成「测出来的空」；③控制腿＝网格自末端必须逐格复制已发布 delta（本例 24/24 abs_diff 0.0），这是「切的是评估端而非重烧」的唯一证明。
- 2026-10-27 r1167 bm-b（捕获律 O-20261002-2100 收口步）

## E103 A parameter ladder is not a ladder of measurements -- group limits by their EDIT FACE before you burn cells (r1168 bm-b)
- Situation: E102 published five screen arms and found gap1/gap2/gap5 give one identical delta on every era-book cell, leaving the question "is the gap limit 1/2/5 identified at all?" open. Walking gap_limit 1..20 and burning a cell per limit would have produced twenty near-identical columns and taught nothing.
- Method: walk the ladder cheaply (screen_universe is ~0.5s per limit over 1,271 names), fingerprint each limit by the SET of bars it rewrites (every changed symbol|date cell, sha1), group limits with the same fingerprint into one object, and burn cells only on one representative per group plus the unscreened base. Publish, per limit, three separate counts -- symbols rewritten / prints repaired / bars rewritten -- plus the panel's own gapped-print spectrum by gap_sessions.
- Measured: 20 limits collapse to 2 objects (limits 1-2 rewrite 36,184 bars, limits 3-20 rewrite 37,101). The single step is limit 3, named by date: sz159901 2010-11-22, an integer-like print with a 3-session gap. The panel's gap spectrum ends at 3 sessions (gapped/integer-like: 28/10 at 1, 4/0 at 2, 1/1 at 3), so nothing above 3 can ever bind, and no limit in 1..20 moves a published cell (published deltas reproduce 6/6 at abs_diff 0.0). Verdict: the limit is NOT identified, and three published columns are one reading under two objects.
- Judgment: (1) an identical edit face is a proof of object identity, so limits inside one group may never be counted as independent measurements; (2) a flat ladder is a property of the data, not of the walk -- publish the spectrum (the largest gap actually present) beside it or flatness is indistinguishable from a broken probe; (3) print count and bar count are different measurements (one repaired print rewrites hundreds of downstream bars) and a face that publishes only one of them reads as either one print or a rewritten panel; (4) a parameter frontier names no chosen value -- gap1 stays the published control.
- Verify: python scripts\e39_panel_census.py gaplimit --start 2018-01-01 (20s, rerun byte-identical, control_reproduces_published=true); selftest 286 -> 299; results/e44_midmonth/GAPLIMIT-20261026.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e103-gaplimit-ladder.md
- 2026-10-27 r1168 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)

## E100 -- split an arm LEVEL difference before you interpret it (2026-10-27 r1169 bm-b)
- Claim: a difference between two screening arms' era LEVELs is not a statement about the book until it is split per transition AND the two arms are checked for holding the same names.
- Practice: (1) split exactly -- a level is an equal-weight mean over that arm's OWN transitions, so each shared transition contributes a_gap/n_gap - a_unsr/n_unsr, a one-sided date keeps its own term, and the residual is PUBLISHED (a partial join must not pass as a decomposition); (2) name the drivers -- for each top window publish the swapped names with BOTH arms' forward reads, not just the alpha; (3) compare holdings first -- if held_only sets are empty and member counts match, the delta is a price-read difference and no screening story is warranted.
- Result (GAPTOP3-20261026-s2018): the two famous moves are one window each. 2017_2020 me -745.9bp = 100% the 2020-12-31 window (sz159975 -89.54% unscreened vs +5.04% repaired; sh510210 -79.98% vs -0.14%); 2021_2023 me -1098.3 top-3 61.6% (sh510580 -89.95%/+1.02%, sh510150 -91.20%/+5.32%); 2021_2023 mid -1117.2 top-3 58.8%; 2024_now mid -241.0 one window (sz159925 +105.39% vs +303.82%, the mirror case). 18 top windows carry price drivers with EMPTY held_only sets.
- Judgment: (1) a level that is a mean over few transitions is not a level, it is whichever single name carried the window -- publish the max single-name read beside any level with transitions < ~30; (2) a -90% one-window ETF read is an unadjusted split, and the repair can push a level either way; (3) never write "the repair changes the book" from a level delta -- write "the repair prices a split print" until the holdings differ; (4) the blind spot is explicit: a split inside an unchanged holding is invisible to an entrant/exit leg (seeded as E104).
- Verify: python scripts\e39_panel_census.py gaptop3 --start 2018-01-01 (9s, residual 0.0, six published deltas reproduce <=0.05bp); selftest 299 -> 307; results/e44_midmonth/GAPTOP3-20261026-s2018.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e100-gaparm-transition-names.md
- 2026-10-27 r1169 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)


## E104 Read the population your discrimination lists cannot see, and audit BOTH arms for plausibility (r1170 bm-b)
- Situation: E100 published the gap arm as a price-read story but had to admit in its own honest-limits block that a split inside an unchanged holding would be invisible: it names only swapped tickers (entrants/exits), so a name the book kept through the window contributes no swap and never reaches the churn sleeve -- yet the book still holds it and still earns its read.
- Method: (1) take the repair arm own persistent set per transition (names held at BOTH ends), read them forward under each arm prices at the same calendar indices, and publish the mean read per arm plus per-name contributions that sum to the window delta exactly (residual printed); (2) publish a mechanical plausibility counter per arm -- the number of |one-window reads| > 100% -- instead of trusting provenance, because the repair is not automatically the plausible side; (3) publish the largest single-name read with its share of the window mean and SUPPRESS that share (share_eligible=false) when the mean is inside 0.5%, since a 200% read against a 0.02% mean is a ratio of noise.
- Result (HELDSPLIT-20261026-s2018): 4 of 6 era-book cells carry a split inside unchanged holdings -- delta mean +15.2bp / max window 247.4bp (2024_now me, 2024-08-30), +14.1 / 162.0 (2024_now mid), +5.5 / 55.4, +3.9 / 35.5; 2017_2020 both books exactly 0.0. Implausible reads: repaired arm 16, unscreened arm 14 -- the two extra are sz159747 in 2023 -- and the direction is NOT guaranteed: sh510580 reads +295% raw vs +1464% repaired across 2024-08-30 to 2024-09-30 with the arm ratio moving from x10.05 to x39.8 exactly at the event.
- Judgment: (1) an entrant/exit decomposition cannot bound split exposure; ask the kept names directly or the exposure is unmeasured, not zero; (2) never infer which arm is right from which arm is the repair -- count implausible reads per arm and publish both; (3) a share of a mean is only a share where the mean is far from zero; gate it and say so, or the column reads as a five-digit ratio; (4) a split that neither arm repairs is a repair-scope limit, not a repair defect -- name the difference in the face.
- Verify: python scripts\e39_panel_census.py heldsplit --start 2018-01-01 (residual 0.0, 6 cells); selftest 307 -> 320; results/e44_midmonth/HELDSPLIT-20261026-s2018.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e104-held-name-split-exposure.md
- 2026-10-27 r1170 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)


## E106 Test where a repair steps AND which way it steps; a folded ratio is not a price factor (r1171 bm-b)
- Situation: E104 flagged 16 unchanged-holding reads past +/-100% and left the witness unexplained -- sh510580 reads +1464% in the repaired arm where the raw series reads +295%, with the arm-vs-raw level ratio moving x10.05 -> x39.8 exactly at the event. Plausibility counting says which side looks wrong; it cannot say why.
- Method: (1) walk the arm-vs-raw ratio over consecutive PRICED closes, so each move is a step with a date, a factor and both one-session reads beside it; (2) test placement (no step may land on a session without a >100% print) and direction separately (the repaired one-session read must fall under the cancel tolerance, not grow); (3) publish the same print with the RECIPROCAL factor, so "the fix is the broken side" is a number and not an opinion; (4) reconcile the product of in-window steps against the published ratio change as an arithmetic assertion (0 mismatches over 322 steps).
- Result (REPAIRSTEP-20261026): 16 windows, 8 arm_untouched_raw_print and 8 step_amplifies_print, 0 step_without_a_print. Placement passes everywhere; direction fails everywhere it moves. Every amplified window reads (1+r)^2-1 -- sh510580 2024-09-02 raw +295.49% -> arm +1464.09%, sh510310 +100.94% -> +303.76%, sz159747 +103.24% -> +313.06%, sh510100 exactly +100.00% -> +300.00%; with the reciprocal factor the reads become +28.05 / +22.21 / -9.42 / +1.25%, the kind of number these windows carry. The other 8 (sz159845 +176.91%, sh512200 +170.07%, sh516160 +292.12%, sh516300 +165.62%) are prints the integer gate skipped (resid 7.7% > 2%) and both arms still carry.
- Judgment: (1) implied_ratio() folds the sign away on purpose (a share ratio >= 1 for the integer test) -- reusing that folded number as a price factor mirrors every upward print, and the bug is invisible on downward prints because there the folded ratio happens to equal the cancelling factor; (2) a date-level consistency check passes a wrong-side factor, so a repair test must assert the post-repair read, not the step date; (3) "the repair moved it" and "the repair moved it the right way" are two tests; (4) unfixed raw prints are a repair-SCOPE finding and must be published beside the repair-DEFECT one, or the face reads as if the arm had touched every name.
- Verify: python scripts\e39_panel_census.py repairstep (16 windows, ratio_product_mismatch=0); results/e44_midmonth/REPAIRSTEP-20261026.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e106-repair-step-consistency.md; fix + re-derivation seeded as pool E108
- 2026-10-27 r1171 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)

## E108 A behavioural diff needs a legacy control run, and a factor must cancel at the event (r1172 bm-b)
- Situation: E106 proved the repaired arm steps the wrong way on upward prints and could only publish the reciprocal read as a counterfactual. The consumer set is seven faces, so the real question was not "what is the fix" but "how do I show the re-derivation is the fix and not two extra days of panel".
- Method: (1) make the factor a pure function of the print (`1/(1+r)`) and keep the folded share ratio only as integer-test evidence -- one number, one job; (2) before re-deriving, re-run ONE consuming leg with the old code monkeypatched back in (`e108_repairfix.py legacy <leg>`) and diff the result against the published face: 0 of 966 numeric leaves moved proves the panel did not drift, so the later diffs are attributable; (3) diff every consumer generically (walk numeric leaves, name the largest movers by JSON path) instead of eyeballing summary fields; (4) require the fix to be directional -- every downward-print cell must be bit-identical, or the patch changed more than the sign.
- Result (REPAIRFIX-20261026): 7 of 7 faces move but only where an upward print lives. REPAIRSTEP 8 step_amplifies_print -> 8 step_cancels_print and fabricating_windows 8 -> 0; MID armed 2024_now mid level -364.7 -> +4.9bp; GAPARM 2024_now mid delta -241.0 -> +128.6bp with a smaller span; 2017_2020 me -745.9 and the 2021_2023 pair unchanged.
- Judgment: (1) a folded detection ratio is never a correction factor -- folding destroys sign, and sign is exactly what a repair needs; (2) "re-derived" is not evidence until a control shows the panel stood still, and the cheapest control is the old code path on one leg, not a second panel snapshot; (3) publish which cells must NOT move (the downward ones) -- an unchanged-cell set is a stronger verifier than any moved-cell count; (4) a face diffed across two cutoffs (here SCREENGAPERA 20261023 vs 20261026) must be labelled direction-only inside the product, not in a later message.
- Verify: python scripts\panel_screen.py (35/35); python scripts\e39_panel_census.py selftest (320/320); python scripts\e108_repairfix.py legacy repairstep + diff; results/e44_midmonth/REPAIRFIX-20261026.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e108-repair-factor-sign.md
- 2026-10-27 r1172 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)


## E138 A schedule a reader cannot turn into a date is not an order (2026-10-27 r1304 bm-c)

The staggered ETF book carried its sleeve clocks as panel POSITIONS only (next_due_pos 5274/5279).
Two things fall out of that: (1) the reader cannot answer the only question an order sheet exists to
answer -- when does this sleeve turn over; (2) the anchor is not portable. A position is meaningful
against exactly one panel; this panel is rebuilt every refresh from a history window that can gain or
drop bars, and every future rebalance (plus the 5-day stagger the whole book rests on) slides silently
with it, with nothing in the ledger to notice.

Fix pattern: anchor the clock on the DATE ('today >= next_due_date', ISO string comparison -- r1288),
keep the position as telemetry, derive the date for legacy position-only rows from the current panel,
and for dates that lie past the panel's last bar count the OFFICIAL exchange calendar (E136) instead of
guessing -- returning None rather than inventing a date when the calendar runs out. Then publish it on
the face the human reads (next_actions per sleeve) and assert it in smoke: a ledger that says
'position 5274' passes every existence check ever written and still cannot be executed.

How to apply: for any scheduled/rotating product face, ask 'what does the operator act on tonight?' --
if the artefact only stores an index into a rebuildable series, it is not yet an order.

## E109 Burn the old and the new arms on ONE panel before calling a diff a fix (2026-10-27 r1173 bm-b)

- Situation: E108 corrected a factor that had been mirrored on upward prints, but only re-derived the
  seven legs it named. Thirteen other legs published numbers computed with the mirrored factor on faces
  dated five sessions back -- so a plain re-run could not say whether a number moved because of the fix
  or because five sessions of panel arrived underneath it.
- Method: run each leg TWICE on today's panel -- once with the old code monkeypatched back (legacy arm),
  once with the shipped code -- and diff the two arms. Harvest each arm's output by MOVING it out of the
  results directory so the next arm writes the same filename fresh; publish the fixed arm afterwards.
  The panel is held constant inside the comparison, so the number that moves is the patch.
- Result (REPAIRSWEEP-20261026): 14 legs, 47,385 numeric leaves, 1,792 moved; 13 legs move, the control
  leg is bit-identical. The writes are order-of-magnitude (concentration cell exactly /10) and two
  verdicts flip sign (residual share 75% -> 125%, churn alpha -619.8 -> +529.4bp). The leg with the
  fewest numbers (screenspread, 410) is the most contaminated (217 moved) because it publishes ratios.
- Judgment: (1) "re-derived with the fixed code" is not evidence of effect -- the only evidence is the
  same-panel A/B, and a published face dated before the fix is a hypothesis about its own numbers, not a
  baseline; (2) an arm-vs-engine RATIO published as a percentage is denominator-fragile -- publish the bp
  pair beside it and never quote the share alone; (3) a leg that does not touch the changed arm must be
  published as a bit-identical control, otherwise "we re-derived everything" cannot be believed.
- Reusable check: does the report separate the code delta from the data delta? If both changed at once,
  the report has one number and two causes.
- Verify: python scripts/e109_repairsweep.py selftest (3/3); results/e44_midmonth/REPAIRSWEEP-20261026.json;
  research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e109-repair-sweep.md
- 2026-10-27 r1173 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)

- Card: separation first, verdict second (E110)
- Trigger: a self-check/control that cannot be read as green -- rc=1 on a leg whose own numbers did not move.
- Move: ask what the control READS before asking what it measures. Here burncontrol recomputes nothing;
  it joins three faces off disk, so it compared a face burned 2026-10-22 (pre-E108 factor) with one burned
  post-fix and reported the version gap as a burn defect. Same-panel A/B with only the factor flipped moved
  4 cells (all mid15 x neutralise x 2024_now, delta 369.6bp); the other 7 moved with four days of panel
  arrival. Re-deriving the stale twin at the current cutoff turned the control green, 56/56, 0 mismatched.
- Judgment: (1) "it fails in both arms" is a claim about the INSTRUMENT, not the cause -- a disk-reading
  control is arm-invariant by construction, and bit-identical failure across arms is the tell; (2) an
  exact-equality join over published faces has two implicit axes it never checks: the cutoff and the code
  that burned them; (3) name the condition in the status (mismatch_across_cutoffs + stale_twins + cutoffs)
  rather than emitting a bare MISMATCH, or the control indicts the data for its own stale input.
- Reusable check: for every control, write down its inputs and their versions; if the two sides were not
  produced by the same code at the same cutoff, the comparison is between experiments, not measurements.
- Verify: python scripts/e110_burncontrol_close.py selftest (4/4); e39 selftest 320/320;
  results/e44_midmonth/BURNCONTROL-CLOSE-20261027.json; research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e110-burncontrol-mismatch.md;
  stale-twin fixture reproduces status=mismatch_across_cutoffs with 11 mismatches
- 2026-10-27 r1174 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)
## E139 Hold one axis fixed and move the other - a period/universe 2x3 that costs one run (2026-10-27 r1305 bm-c)

Every published number for the ETF composite book lives in 2020-01..2026-10, on a panel whose eligible
cross-section grows from single digits (2013) to ~720 names. Two stories fit that chart equally well: the
six-factor composite, or a universe that is mostly funds which exist BECAUSE the industry grew. Quoting
either one alone is how "the edge is real" and "the cross-section is the edge" both get published off the
same numbers.

Fix pattern: price the same engine on three cells, never two.
  A  universe = names whose first bar <= the boundary, window = BEFORE the boundary   (the honest test)
  B  same universe, window = AFTER the boundary                                       (period effect isolated)
  C  full universe, window = AFTER the boundary                                       (breadth effect isolated)
A vs B holds the universe fixed and moves time; B vs C holds time fixed and moves the universe. Publishing
A alone invites "small cross-section so of course it failed"; publishing C alone invites "look at the
edge". Together they separate the two claims and give each a number.

Three things make the read honest rather than decorative:
  (1) a random-score null on the primary cell of every cell - p = share of permutation runs that BEAT the
      real ranking. A pre-period book at -8.86pp excess with p=0.96 is not "flat", it is worse than random,
      which is a much stronger statement than "underperformed the benchmark";
  (2) eligibility counts (median/min/thin) published per cell - a top-5 pick from 14 eligible names is a
      universe, not a cross-section, and the reader must be able to see that without re-running;
  (3) an asserted control: re-price the PUBLISHED arm through the same call path and print the delta
      (here 7.62% vs 7.61% published), so the comparison arm is proven to be the published arm (r1297).

Trap that must be pinned by a selftest leg, because it flips the verdict silently: slicing the panel at
`start` to save memory resets the 120-bar history counter, and the book then sits in CASH for its first
six months -- which reads exactly like "the edge never existed". Pass the window to the engine as `start`;
slice only the END. The r1305 leg asserts it directly (full-panel eligible at a date > 0, truncated-panel
eligible == 0), so the memory optimisation can never come back quietly.

How to apply: for any product whose history starts when its UNIVERSE came into existence, the missing
question is never "is the backtest right" but "would this have paid on the instruments that existed
then". One extra run, three cells, two null p-values, and the answer either supports the expectation band
or forces it open. Artifact face: results/etf_book_pre2020.json (schema etf_book_pre2020/v1);
implementation scripts/etf_book_pre2020.py (rides scripts/etf_composite_pf.py, no re-implementation).

- Card: a face's identity is (code version, cutoff) -- and unstamped is UNKNOWN (E111)
- Trigger: a control that can join two faces and call them equal (or unequal) with no record of
  which code burned either one.
- Move: derive a version id from the source of the functions that produce the number
  (panel_screen.split_events+neutralise+implied_ratio -> 12-char sha), stamp it in the ONE writer
  every face goes through (e39_panel_census.write_json), and make the join publish
  version_unknown / mixed_repair_factors instead of identical / MISMATCH. Back-stamping is refused
  on principle: the stamp would be a claim about code that was never recorded.
- Judgment: (1) the cutoff guard and the factor guard are two axes of the same defect -- fixing one
  leaves the control reporting a mismatch it cannot attribute; (2) on this repo the honest post-fix
  verdict is version_unknown for all 70 faces on disk (0 stamped): an unstamped face is not a
  smaller risk than a stale one, it is an unmeasurable one; (3) the stamp must be sensitive to any
  source edit including comments -- a wording change is a reason to re-burn.
- Reusable check: for every writer of a published number, ask "does the file say which code made
  it, and does the consumer refuse when it does not?"
- Verify: python scripts/e111_face_factor_stamp.py selftest (13/13); python scripts/panel_screen.py
  (39/39); python scripts/e39_panel_census.py selftest (324/324);
  results/e44_midmonth/FACESTAMP-20261027.json (writer_proof stamped_on_disk=true)
- 2026-10-27 r1175 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)

- Card: attribute a COUNTERFACTUAL by rewriting returns, and prove the machinery with an identity control (E107)
- Trigger: you have measured an effect on names and now must say how much of a published portfolio
  curve it moved -- and the two arms differ by BOTH that effect and by which names they hold.
- Move: build a third curve from the treated arm's OWN holdings and prices, substituting only the
  target names' session RETURNS with the control arm's inside the affected window. Holdings,
  turnover and cost are then identical by construction, so the difference is the effect alone.
  Publish the raw arm gap beside it and define \selection_effect = arm_gap + read_effect\ with the
  sign convention written down once (read_effect = cost of undoing the treatment).
- Judgment: (1) rewrite returns, never levels -- splicing levels into the other arm's series injects
  a jump at every window edge exactly the size of the effect you are measuring; (2) add an identity
  control (substitute a series for itself, expect residual 0.0) or an off-by-one at a window edge
  hides as a plausible few-bp reading; (3) publish the substituted names' WEIGHT SHARE -- on bm-b's
  near-universe ETF book the persistent names carry 99.4-99.7% of capital, which caps what
  selection can possibly explain and is the first thing a reader needs; (4) the two deltas disagree
  in sign in 4 of 6 cells here, so "the arm gap is the effect" is wrong most of the time.
- Reusable check: does any "change X moved the curve" claim ship (a) a same-holdings counterfactual,
  (b) an identity control, (c) the weight share of the population it substituted?
- Verify: python scripts/e107_heldcurve.py --selftest (20/20);
  results/e44_midmonth/HELDCURVE-20261026.json (max identity_control_residual_bp 0.0)
- 2026-10-27 r1176 bm-b (capture law O-20261002-2100 closing step)

## E140 A contribution share is not a skill share - price the sleeve restricted AND against its own passive alternative (2026-10-27 r1306 bm-c)
A class can supply 30% of gross return while carrying 6% of average weight, and that number is silent about
whether the ranking or the exposure earned it. Two arms settle it and both are cheap because they ride the
existing engine: restrict the score to the sleeve's members (re-rank inside the sleeve) versus equal-weighting
those same members, then add the one passive alternative a holder would actually have taken. Here the
commodity sleeve returned 16.31%/yr versus 11.45%/yr for the sleeve equally weighted (+4.86pp of selection)
but 13.91%/yr for holding the deepest-turnover metal ETF alone - so 13.9 of its 16.3 points were beta and only
+2.40pp was the overlay.
Trap that must be pinned, because it silently manufactures a zero: a sleeve narrower than n_hold never trades
at all (score.dropna() returns fewer names than the book size, so no pick is ever made and the arm reads as
0.00%/yr with zero turnover). The r1306 leg asserts the empty case explicitly (`sleeve narrower than n_hold
never trades`), and the picks replay that recovers WHICH names were held is only trusted when it reproduces
the engine nav exactly (max gap 0.0 pinned as a leg).
How to apply: any "this sleeve contributes X% of the return" claim needs (a) the sleeve restricted vs its own
equal weight, (b) one passive alternative, (c) the concentration read - distinct names, top-name slot share,
and the names held more than half the time - before the word "selection" is used. Artifact face:
results/etf_book_commodity_split.json (schema etf_book_commodity_split/v1); implementation
scripts/etf_book_commodity_split.py (selftest 10/10, rides etf_composite_pf + etf_equity_alpha).
## E141 Price the machinery against not trading at all (2026-10-27 r1307 bm-c)
A book priced only against other ranking rules has never been priced against the alternative a holder actually has. Take the book's own mean class shape (from the attribution chain), hold it statically with no scoring and no rotation, run it on the same grid / same cost rule / same vol overlay, and the difference is what the machinery earns. Here the shipped book did 9.49%/yr and -11.8% DD against 3.13%/yr and -22.9% DD for the static mix: +6.36pp/yr at 10.5x the turnover (results/etf_book_passive_mix.json, scripts/etf_book_passive_mix.py, selftest 8/8, control arm asserted at 0.0pp against the published card).
Two traps. (a) The static arm is FAVOURED: equal-weight class indices rebalance daily at zero cost, so the premium is a lower bound, and saying so is what makes the number quotable. (b) The attribution chain's class weights are already normalised to 1, so a 'raw exposure including cash' arm silently becomes a duplicate of the fully-invested arm -- both printed 3.13% and the label lied. When two arms print the same number, the first hypothesis is that they are the same arm, not that the finding is robust.
How to apply: any 'the strategy works' claim needs one arm that holds the same exposures and never trades; if that arm lands close, the machinery is decoration and the card must say so.

## E142 A twin test must be a code-graph test; column overlap is not coverage (2026-10-27 r1176 bm-b)
Asking "does a screened twin exist for this face?" against face contents does not work: generic metric
names (ann_return_pct, sharpe, max_drawdown_pct) make an unrelated twin match at ratio 1.00, and seven of
25 charged modes picked up exactly that false positive (results/e79/ROUTING-20261027.json, scripts/e97_routing.py,
selftest 14/14). The decisive evidence is the call graph: a twin covers a mode when the twin function calls
that mode's runner (screenspread -> run_spreadsize) or when the twin builds the face in its own body (verified
by a shared >=4-char filename root plus >=3 body mentions, which is how run_screenweek covers the weekly clash
face it never calls). Coverage is then 3 direct + 1 body-built of 25, and 21 modes across 14 runners are still
unscreened.
Two traps. (a) Coverage is not correction: a twin face existing does not mean the face a reader opens was
re-derived, so "covered" and "the published number is screened" are different claims and must be printed
separately. (b) Rank work per RUNNER, not per mode: one burn feeds four modes here (burnin/e44/knife/skinloo
share one face, 1727 numbers), so a mode-level ranking double-counts the same burn and hides where the exposure
actually is.
How to apply: any "is there already a screened/split/robust version of this?" audit must resolve coverage from
code (call graph + body), publish the weak channel as explicitly non-decisive, and rank the remaining work by
distinct experiments, not by published cells.

## E143 A mean-of-means read must publish its additive name decomposition (2026-10-27 r1179 bm-b)
A level that is the mean of a per-period read (churn alpha, per-swap spread, per-window delta) can be 100%
one name, and nothing on the published face says so. The decomposition is exact rather than a correlation
rank: any mean is additive over its inputs, so churn_alpha = sum(r_e/n_e) - sum(r_x/n_x) per transition and
the era level is that divided by the transition count. Publishing the largest single-name contribution with
name/date/side plus the additivity residual (0.0 here) turns "one name probably carried this" into a checkable
reading (results/e44_midmonth/MIDLEVEL-DRIVERS-20261026-s20180101.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
midleveldrivers, selftest 331/331). Outcome on the MID family: 9 of 30 level cells are one-name artefacts,
including the largest cell in the family (1405.0bp, 56.5% one exit) and the newer-era unscreened cell
(-123.7bp, 105.2% one name), while only 2021_2023 me/mid (1866.5 / 2185.4, biggest name 17.8%/14.5%) survive.
Two traps. (a) **A cell that prints identically across arms is not thereby robust** -- here the arm-invariant
cell was the one a single name carried, so arm agreement was the artefact's signature, not replication.
(b) A decomposition must be exact or it is a story: publish the residual, and assert it in the selftest.
How to apply: before quoting any mean-of-means level, publish the max single-input share beside it; treat
>=50% as an artefact, >=25% as concentrated, and put the material floor in the spec so a reader can re-cut.

## E144 When two rounds disagree on one window, publish an asserted ladder, not two numbers (2026-10-27 r1308 bm-c)
One window can print opposite stories and both be correct: r1305 published 2015-2019 as -4.97%/yr (-8.86pp vs equal weight), and r1308's passive-mix cell found the same window at +5.12%/yr with a POSITIVE selection premium. A card carrying both is unreadable, so the gap has to be priced instead of narrated. The ladder moves exactly one axis per rung -- pool (bond/money funds out), then book width (5 -> 12 names), then the sizing rule (8% de-lever overlay) -- holding the window and the fill rule fixed, and it ASSERTS both ends against the published arms (rung 1 vs etf_book_pre2020 cell A, rung 4 vs the passive-mix pre-2020 book arm, 0.5pp each). Result: -4.97 -> +0.85 -> +0.73 -> +5.12%/yr, i.e. +5.82pp from the pool, -0.12pp from width, +4.39pp from the sizing rule, 0.0pp control error at both ends. Neither round was wrong; the window answers a different question depending on the cell.
Two things this buys that a single number cannot. (a) The sizing rule is not an edge but it is worth +4.39pp on this window, so any "the ranking pays before 2020" sentence has to be rule-matched on both arms or it is measuring leverage. (b) The benchmark matters as much as the cell: the same rung 4 against equal weight reads +2.76pp while against the static class mix it reads +2.17pp, so both benchmarks travel per rung rather than one being translated into the other.
Trap: a score rebuilt on the full universe and then subset to a rung's universe does not order that subset the way a ranking inside it would (rank sums are not invariant to the pool), so every rung rebuilds its own score from its own frames.
How to apply: when two published numbers disagree about one window, do not pick one -- publish the ladder that connects them, assert both ends against the published artifacts, and let the per-step deltas name the culprit. Artifact face: results/etf_book_window_decomp.json (schema etf_book_window_decomp/v1); implementation scripts/etf_book_window_decomp.py (selftest 8/8); consumption scripts/etf_book_production.py `## The 2015-2019 reconciliation`.

## E145 A diff needs BOTH axes priced: two arms at one cutoff AND one arm at two cutoffs (2026-10-27 r1180 bm-b)

An A/B comparison has two confounds, not one. E112 re-derived the burn-in face on the split-repaired
arm beside the raw arm at one cutoff: the repair moves 13 of 56 published cells, worst 0.42pp, and moves
zero gate dates. The honest next question is "is 0.42pp big", and the answer is not in the A/B at all --
it is in the *same arm at two cutoffs*. Joining the previously published raw face (cutoff 2026-10-20)
against today's raw arm (2026-10-26, arm held fixed) moves 26 of 56 cells: returns -0.12..-0.28pp and
symbols_eligible +6 names. The refresh axis moves MORE cells than the repair axis.

Reading: (a) the load-bearing columns (gate dates, un-invested sessions, eligible-at-first-trade) moved on
NEITHER axis, which is what makes them safe to cite; (b) on the return columns a 0.2-0.3pp gap between two
published faces is inside refresh noise and cannot be attributed to a code change at all.

How to apply: build the join once as a generic cell harvest + join over the face's own columns (E112's
`_burnin_arm_cells` / `_burnin_pair_rows`), then run three controls through it in the same call --
identity (arm vs itself, must read 0.0), coverage (cells one-sided or null, reported not skipped) and
cutoff (newest previously published face of the SAME arm vs today's). Publish all three on the face.
Without the cutoff row, "the repair is small" and "the repair is invisible" are indistinguishable, and the
first reader to compare two faces a week apart will price a panel refresh as a fix.
Evidence: results/e44_burnin/BURNINSCREEN-20261026.json (control / cutoff_control blocks), scripts/
e39_panel_census.py burninscreen, digest research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e112-burnin-screened-twin.md.


## E146 A ladder of fixed cells cannot test a rule that re-picks cells (2026-10-27 r1309 bm-c)
The shipped ETF book does not hold one configuration: it re-picks (N, R) every year on trailing Sharpe. Every rung of r1308's reconciliation ladder was a FIXED cell chosen with hindsight, so the ladder -- however well asserted -- is silent about the thing the company actually deploys. Priced at 8% de-lever-only overlay, 10bp/side, close t -> open t+1, 193-name pre-2020 universe, years 2016-2019: walk-forward 4.86%/yr (20.91% cumulative), fixed N12 4.97% (21.42%), equal weight 2.69% (11.20%). Control: both fixed arms reproduce the published ladder at 0.00pp (r3 raw 0.73%, r4 overlay 5.12%).
The interesting part is WHY the rule neither rescues nor damages the window: all nine overlay cells are positive (3.80-5.98%, mean 5.14%, sd 0.79pp) and the selector's trailing Sharpe readings were 0.243 / -0.077 / 0.136 / -0.034, i.e. it saw no dispersion to exploit and its four picks were effectively a draw (N8_R40 x3, N3_R40 x1). So "the rule survives" is arithmetic -- the mean of nine positive cells -- not evidence of selection skill. Publishing the rule's arm without the cell dispersion would let a reader credit the selector for the overlay's work.
Two implementation rules come with it. (a) A chained year return must share its boundary bar with the next year (first bar >= Jan 1 of Y, first bar >= Jan 1 of Y+1, both included), otherwise compounding the years silently drops the move between the last December bar and the first January bar and the chain stops equalling the same curve's window return -- the selftest asserts chain == curve return on a constant-pick fixture. (b) A fallback cell must exist in the curve dict the fallback reads from; the production width N12 is not one of the nine candidate cells, so the arms list is CELLS + [FIXED], otherwise "too little history" crashes exactly where it should degrade.
How to apply: whenever the deployed configuration includes a re-selection rule, price the rule, not only the cell -- and publish the candidate dispersion beside the rule's result so the reader can see whether the pick had anything to pick between. Artifact face: results/etf_book_pre2020_wf.json (schema etf_book_pre2020_wf/v1); implementation scripts/etf_book_pre2020_wf.py (selftest 8/8).

## E147 One face can hold three stability classes at once -- sort the columns before quoting any of them

Question: a face publishes N numbers and a repair moved some of them. Is the face now unusable? No -- and the
wrong reaction (throw the face away) costs as much as the wrong reflex (quote it unchanged). The cut sweep
(E58 cutcurve, 1,139 numbers) sorts into three classes that behave completely differently under two independent
axes, at ONE cutoff with ONE load and ONE join:

  (a) flip_count (22 cells)     arm-free AND refresh-free (0 moved on both axes) -> quotable bare.
  (b) era level (88 cells)      cut-INVARIANT by construction (drift 0.0 across all 11 cuts, both cost levels),
                                but the arm shifts it uniformly (+58bp newest era, identical at every cut) and a
                                4-session panel refresh shifts it again (-6bp) -> quote with arm + cutoff,
                                never with the cut.
  (c) band + widest window      dispersion only; 16 of 22 windows move on a panel refresh and 2 of 22 on the
                                arm, including one wholesale swap at cut 5 -> a pointer at (arm, cutoff), not
                                a fact.

Why it matters: a level that is invariant across the whole axis you varied is not evidence about that axis -- it
is evidence that the axis is a dispersion knob. Publish the invariance, or a reader will read a 0.0 drift as
robustness when it is slice geometry.

How to apply: generalise the join, never duplicate it. `_burnin_pair_rows(face_a, face_b, keys, cells_fn=...,
dates_fn=...)` joins any two faces through a per-shape harvest (E112 burn-in columns, E113 cutcurve cells), so a
new twin costs a harvest function and zero new control logic. Then run three controls in the same call --
identity (arm vs itself, must read 0.0), coverage (one-sided / null cells reported, not skipped), cutoff (newest
published face of the SAME arm vs today's) -- and report the moved-cell count PER COLUMN GROUP, because that is
the only output that assigns a stability class to each reading.

Evidence: results/e44_burnin/CUTCURVESCREEN-20261026.json (pair / control / cutoff_control), scripts/
e39_panel_census.py cutcurvescreen (selftest 343/343), digest
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e113-cutcurve-screened-twin.md.

## E148 Repair and refresh move different columns -- measure both axes before quoting any cell (2026-10-27 r1182 bm-b)
The same 95 numbers of one face respond to the two stability axes in opposite ways. The chase leg (E57; 95 joined
cells: bucket shares, bucket forward returns, entrant counts, universe size) was burned twice: raw vs
panel_screen neutralise_int (the repair axis, 67 prints on 67 of 1,317 names, names in = names out) and the newest
published raw face 2026-10-20 vs today's raw arm (the refresh axis, +4 sessions).
  repair axis  -> 5 of 95 cells move, all bucket RETURNS (max +0.166 pp); every count, every share and the
                  universe size are identical: the decision sets do not move at all.
  refresh axis -> 16 of 95 cells move, worst cell is a COUNT (entrant_slots +6, universe_members 1265 -> 1271);
                  shares shift by <=0.45 pp and returns by <=0.60 pp.
So "is this face stable" has no single answer: bucket MEMBERSHIP is arm-free and refresh-sensitive, bucket RETURNS
are repair-sensitive -- in the direction where the raw arm flatters the losing bucket, same as E114. Quote the
shares without an arm; quote the returns with one. And do not import the stability classes of another leg: the
burn-in (E112) was stable in its gate columns and unstable in its returns, exactly the opposite decomposition.
Two mechanism rules came with it. (a) A twin registry is a hand-maintained list, so a twin that is written but not
