registered is invisible: E97's coverage test declared covered_by_twin=3 while four screened twins sat on disk.
Registering them moved the routing table to 11 covered / 13 uncovered / 1,568 cells untouched (from 3 / 21 / 5,282).
Any "is X covered" table must derive from the declaration AND assert that every twin function in the census source
appears in it. (b) A harvest must walk the face's real nesting: chase publishes eras[calendar][era], so a harvest
written for eras[era] silently returned 15 cells of 95 and the join reported "0 of 7 cells moved" -- a green-looking
verdict from an empty read. Assert the cell count against E97's published-number census for that runner, not
against zero.
How to apply: for each new screened twin, publish the moved-cell count PER COLUMN GROUP on both axes plus the
identity and coverage controls; if the joined cell count is far below the routing census for that runner, the
harvest is wrong, not the face.
Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/CHASESCREEN-20261026.json (pair / control / cutoff_control), scripts/
e39_panel_census.py chasescreen (selftest 348 -> 353/353), scripts/e97_routing.py TWIN_FUNCS,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e115-chase-screened-twin.md.

## E149 A strategy arm benchmarked against an unrepaired price face measures the repair (2026-10-27 r1310 bm-c)
The shipped ETF book was regressed on the four liquid exposures an account could hold instead of it (gold 518880 / Nasdaq-100 QDII 513100 / CSI300 510300 / treasury bond 511010), close t -> open t+1, 10bp/side, 8% de-lever-only overlay, 2020-01-03..2026-10-26, 1645 paired days. Book 9.49%/yr SR 0.98 DD -11.8%; equal-weight four-ETF basket on the same grid and cost 9.99%/yr SR 0.974 DD -11.4%; R2 0.43 with loadings gold 0.15 / NQ 0.07 / CSI300 0.25 / bond -0.03; alpha +5.77%/yr t=1.91 (IS t=1.16, OOS t=1.93, walk-forward IS-coefficients-on-OOS residual +7.44%/yr). Reading: the book is neither a gold bet nor a proven skill bet -- what it buys over the basket is a de-lever rule, not return.
Trap this face caught in itself: the first run priced the span arms off the raw panel files, where 513100's 2022-01-14 split prints as a -50% day, because the strategy arm's panel gets repair_flips and the benchmark frame did not. That read the Nasdaq arm at -5.3%/yr and the basket at 6.41%/yr, i.e. it made the book look 3pp better than buy-and-hold -- the entire conclusion was an artefact of the corrupt comparison arm, not of the book. After the same repair: arm +20.4%/yr, basket 9.99%/yr, and the book lands within 0.5pp of the basket. This is r1262's data-pit moved to the benchmark end.
Two publication rules come with it. (a) A span/skill comparison must print the comparison arms' OWN risk (CAGR+Sharpe+maxDD+vol) beside the strategy's: gold alone makes 13.3%/yr against the book's 9.5%, which reads as a verdict until its 18.1% vol and -31.5% drawdown are printed next to it. (b) A t below 2 is published as "not distinguishable", never as zero and never as skill, and both IS and OOS columns travel with it, because IS alone (t=1.16) and OOS alone (t=1.93) tell different stories than the full window (1.91).
How to apply: whenever a face regresses or benchmarks one arm against another, both arms must come out of the SAME repair/normalisation path, with a selftest leg pinning it (here: span frames pass through repair_flips and the flip count is published in caveats). Artifact face: results/etf_book_spanning.json (schema etf_book_spanning/v1); implementation scripts/etf_book_spanning.py (selftest 12/12); chain leg etf_book_spanning; digest research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1310-spanning-four-betas.md.

## E150 A twin boolean must be two facts: "the join worked" and "the numbers held" are not the same test (2026-10-27 r1183 bm-b)
E112-E115 all computed `survives` as `only_in_raw==0 and only_in_screened==0 and control.cells_moved==0 and
control.max_abs_delta==0.0` -- i.e. it tested the JOIN and the CONTROL, never the numbers themselves. On the four
earlier twins that was invisible because cells_moved happened to be 0. On E116's first burn the join was complete,
the control was silent, and 55 of 93 cells had moved, so the face published
`counts_survive_the_repair: true` with the reading "the early ladder survives the repair: 55 of 93 cells move" --
a sentence that contradicts its own flag and reads as a survival to anyone who skims. Extract the predicate into
one function `_twin_survives(summary, control)` that also requires `cells_moved == 0`, apply it to every twin, and
pin the shape with a selftest: a complete join with 55 moved cells must be False, a silent control with 0 moved
cells must be True, and a control that moved its own numbers must block the claim.
Why it mattered: the boolean is what downstream tickets quote (E97, the pool row, the digest headline); the moved
count is a detail. A flag that only tests the machinery will eventually certify a face whose numbers all changed.
How to apply: whenever a face publishes a verdict boolean, write its definition as the predicate the reader would
assume ("nothing moved"), then add selftest legs that feed it the failure case, not just the happy path. And split
any boolean that answers two questions into two keys.
Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/EARLYDELAYSCREEN-20261026.json (answers.counts_survive_the_repair now false,
answers.reading now "the repair reaches the early ladder"), scripts/e39_panel_census.py _twin_survives
(selftest 353 -> 362/362), research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e116-earlydelay-screened.md.

## E151 Two arms must be fitted by one code path, and the control arm must be asserted (2026-10-27 r1311 bm-c)
The staggered ETF book (6+6, second sleeve offset half a rebalance period) was regressed on the same four liquid
exposures as the shipped single sleeve: gold 518880 / Nasdaq-100 QDII 513100 / CSI300 510300 / treasury bond 511010,
close t -> open t+1, 10bp/side, 8% de-lever-only overlay, same universe and score, 2020-01-06..2026-10-27 (1646
paired days). Single sleeve 9.62%/yr SR 0.99 DD -11.8%, alpha +5.85%/yr t=1.94, residual vol 7.7%, R2 0.43.
Staggered arm 10.61%/yr SR 1.07 DD -10.4%, alpha +6.77%/yr t=2.36, residual vol 7.3%, R2 0.50.
Reading: the split clock clears a t of 2 where the single sleeve does not (+0.92pp/yr, lower residual vol) -- so
r1301's "the turnover saving is the entire effect" is too strong: the saving is real but not the whole difference.
One arm over the threshold is a hypothesis, not an edge; the OOS half (t=2.30) is the only part that carries it.
Two rules the code now enforces rather than documents.
(a) ONE regression path for every arm. The single-book regression had been inlined in build(); adding a second arm
    by copying that block is how two "comparable" alphas turn out to be fitted by different arithmetic (r1262's
    lesson applies to the estimator, not just to the experiment). Both arms now go through arm_from_returns(), and a
    selftest leg pins that the helper reproduces a direct fit exactly, plus an identity control (same series in ->
    same intercept out) and a sign-aware planted-difference leg (+5bp/day must read higher, not merely "different").
(b) THE CONTROL ARM IS ASSERTED, NOT LOOKED UP. The staggered arm's configuration is read off its own artefact
    (machinery block, never typed) AND its CAGR is compared against the published stagger face with a 0.5pp
    tolerance before any alpha is quoted (this window: 10.61% vs published 10.41%, delta 0.20pp). A reader of
    "arm A vs arm B" needs proof that A is the arm somebody else published -- r1297's rule, now on its second face.
    Self-caught in the same window: the first version read doc["config"], which does not exist (the rule lives under
    "machinery"), and silently fell back to defaults -- i.e. it would have priced a guessed book and printed it as
    the comparison. The fallback now prints its own reason into the artefact.
How to apply: when adding an arm to an existing regression/comparison, (1) route it through the existing estimator,
(2) assert the control arm against its publisher, (3) publish which config key was read and where, and (4) word the
verdict from the t-stats of BOTH arms (three states: same intercept / one arm crosses 2 / different intercept) --
never from intent. Artifacts: results/etf_book_spanning.json (arms + stagger_control), results/etf_book_production/
latest.json (spanning.staggered_arm), scripts/etf_book_spanning.py selftest 12 -> 23, digest
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1311-stagger-spanning.md.

## E152 A key's unit is in its name: derive it, do not maintain a second list (2026-10-27 r1311 bm-c)
The fleet heartbeat carried `gpu_vram_free_gb = 15099` -- a key whose name promises GB holding a MiB number -- while
`gpu_free_vram_mb` still carried a previous probe's 14877. Smoke flagged only the MiB family spread (207 MiB); the
wrong-unit key was invisible because the writer and the checker shared the same blind spot: `fields_from()` wrote
MiB into it (it was on FREE_KEYS but not on GB_KEYS) and `_mb()` read it back as MiB, so both sides agreed and the
face said "15099 GB free" to anyone who trusted the name. Root cause: two lists that must stay in sync (the key table
and the unit table) with nothing forcing them to.
Fix: the unit is derived from the spelling itself (key_unit() -> gpu_mem_family() token derivation), used by both the
writer and the auditor; the frozen GB list survives only as a fallback for keys the token rule cannot classify.
Selftest 28 -> 30 legs, including "every GB-named key carries GB" and "a MiB number under a GB name is caught, not
agreed with" (the read must differ by >64GB, i.e. the checker must stop confirming the defect).
How to apply: whenever a field name encodes a unit (gb/mb/pct/bp), derive the unit from the name at BOTH ends -- the
producer and the auditor -- and add a leg asserting a value under a mismatched name is caught. Two hand-maintained
tables over the same key set will eventually disagree, and the disagreement is silent because each table validates
the other. Artifacts: Tools/gpu_face.py (selftest 30/30), fleet/machines/bm-c.json (audit 0 findings, 30 GPU keys).

## E153 A census over a repairable panel is an arm statement -- split its count columns from its value columns (2026-10-27 r1184 bm-b)

`run_panelsplit` (E75) counts the very prints that `panel_screen` repairs, so its twin could not ask "does the
conclusion survive": it had to ask how much of the audit IS the repair. Both arms off one load at cutoff 2026-10-27,
screen `neutralise_int`: events 161 -> 96, names 152 -> 90, adjacent 135 -> 70, integer-ratio fingerprints 74 -> 9,
book cost 1468.23 -> 1058.25 bp (-409.98 over the window, -60.14 bp/yr). Gapped events (26) and non-integer prints
(87) do not move by a single count.
Three rules follow, and the second is why this card exists:
1) When the leg's subject IS the repair, publish count cells and value cells as separate blocks (`count_cells_moved`
   vs `value_cells_moved`) and say in-line that a count delta is the repair working, not a stability finding. One
   `cells_moved` headline lets a reader conclude "unstable measurement" from a face where the measurement was
   supposed to move.
2) The UNMOVED columns are the proof of mechanism. Gapped and non-integer counts staying bit-identical, while every
   adjacent and integer count drops by the same 65, says the repair's boundary is adjacency rather than "splits" --
   a stronger claim than any number that moved.
3) Beware the derived denominator: `by_era[*].years` is measured between the first and last SURVIVING event, so
   removing events shortens the span (2024_now 2.6256 -> 2.2012) and `bp_per_year` inherits it. Never divide era
   cost by this column when comparing arms; use a fixed span.
Also: the post-repair residual (96 events, 87 of them non-integer) is a DIFFERENT claim -- resumptions after
suspension and instruments whose daily limit is not 10/20% -- not a cleaner version of the pre-repair number.
Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/PANELSPLITSCREEN-20261027.json (73 cells, 40 moved: 26 count / 14 value; identity 0,
refresh control 9 with max 183 on breadth_min, coverage 4 null-on-both all era/pre2017), scripts/e39_panel_census.py
_panelsplit_arm_cells + run_panelsplitscreen (selftest 362 -> 371/371),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e117-panelsplit-screened-twin.md.


## E154 An alpha test only ever run inside the window the book was fitted in is a description, not an edge (2026-10-27 r1312 bm-c)

The four-span regression (gold / Nasdaq QDII / CSI300 / bond) had been run twice on 2020-2026 -- once for the
single sleeve (r1310: +5.85 %/yr, t=1.94) and once for the staggered book (r1311: +6.77 %/yr, t=2.36) -- and both
times inside the window in which the ETF universe grew and the book made all of its money. Re-running the SAME
regression on the universe the boom did not contain (193 pre-2020 names, 2015-01-07..2019-12-31, control delta
0.13 pp against the published pre-2020 cell) gives single +2.03 %/yr t=1.04 and staggered +1.00 %/yr t=0.48, with
the OOS half (2018-2019) at t=-0.39 / -0.77 and R2 0.70/0.68 against 0.43 in the boom window.
Three transferable rules:
1) The window is a parameter of the claim. Any intercept quoted without its window is quoting the window.
2) A higher R2 outside the fitted window turns "the sample is short" into evidence: if the span betas explain MORE
   of the book there, the exposures were doing the work and the intercept was the weather.
3) Print the buy-and-hold basket beside the intercept. Here the static four-ETF basket made 8.64 %/yr at a
   shallower drawdown than the book's 5.12 % -- "rotation cost money" is a stronger, more usable statement than
   "t below 2", and it is what tells a reader not to size on the headline.
Mechanism note: extend `arm_from_returns` with an `is_end` argument instead of writing a second regression -- one
code path, two windows, and the default keeps every existing caller bit-identical (r1262's rule applied to time
rather than to width). Evidence: results/etf_book_spanning_pre2020.json, CARD.md section "Does any of it survive
outside 2020-2026?", scripts/etf_book_spanning_pre2020.py (selftest 15/15), smoke +1 leg,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-r1312-preboom-span-test.md.

## E155 A track record that carries no benchmark on its own bars answers no question (2026-10-27 r1313 bm-c)

The forward ledger seeded 2026-10-23 recorded the book's own nav and nothing else. Every "is this better than
just buying the four exposures" question therefore had to go back to the annual pre-2020/2020-2026 regression
(E154) -- a window statement answering a daily question. The ledger now appends, per bar, the same equal-weight
four-ETF basket (gold 518880 / Nasdaq QDII 513100 / CSI300 510300 / bond 511010) priced on the BOOK's decision
grid with the BOOK's next-open fill and cost rule, rebased to the same seed bar, and publishes
`benchmark.latest.excess_pp` (book_nav / basket_nav - 1). First reading on 2026-10-27: book 1.00400 vs basket
0.99649, excess +0.75 pp over 3 bars.
Four rules that made it usable rather than decorative:
1) Read the basket's keys from the spanning study's own SPAN list, never a copy -- the annual and the daily
   comparison must be the same basket (a selftest leg asserts key equality).
2) Re-derive the benchmark block on every run and freeze the ledger when a published basket bar stops
   reproducing, exactly like the book's own marks; a published daily answer that silently re-prices itself is
   worse than none.
3) Prove the arm moved. An equal-weight basket that was not invested at its first bar published a flat 1.0 line
   and passed every identity leg (r1261 class); the opening position and a "basket actually moved" assertion are
   both load-bearing.
4) Do not double-charge one side. The engine convention charges weight drift on NEITHER arm, so this leg sizes
   that omission (0.0147 drift-turnover per decision, ~1.8 bp/yr) instead of booking it against the basket alone;
   an exact cost-sweep identity leg pins that decision in place.
Also: excess must be a ratio against the basket (1.05/1.02-1 = 2.94 %, not 3.00 pp) -- a difference flatters the
book by the size of the basket's own move, and the difference is exactly what a reader will compute by hand.
Evidence: results/etf_book_forward/LEDGER.json (benchmark block), scripts/etf_book_forward.py (selftest 33 legs,
9 of them new), scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py (lifted into the CEO one-pager, selftest 26 -> 28), chain leg prints
the pair on every run.
## E156 On a face that already carries a guard, a screened twin tests whether the two screens agree -- not whether the face is stable (r1189 bm-b, E118)

The stale tail (E74) already excludes, before pricing anything, every event whose span carries a >30% single-session
move; panel_screen repairs a different population (integer-ratio prints). Joining the two arms on the same 77 cells
moved 19, but the priced tail came back bit-identical (raw_clean 112 events / -6.079 bp on both arms, raw_all_events
131 / -514.454 on both) and the artefact list held 19 events with the same top print (sh510150@2021-05-31) on both
sides. Only the relative (name-minus-wide-book) columns moved, and by 0.42 bp of a -522 bp total -- because the repair
edits the wide book's members' returns, not the exiting name's.

1) Ask which columns SHOULD move before you read the moved-count. "19 of 77 moved" reads as instability; split into
   guarded (3) / unguarded (8) / other (8) it reads as "the priced tail is arm-invariant, the denominators are not".
   A twin reported without that split destroys the finding it exists to produce.
2) Two guards over overlapping populations are not redundant, but their agreement is a measured fact, not an
   assumption. Getting "both arms bit-identical" is the evidence that lets the tail be quoted; it is not a proof the
   panel is clean.
3) Size the refresh in the same breath. The cutoff control moved 44 cells with a 6.0 bp max on this family while the
   repair moved 19 cells with 0.42 bp -- a number quoted without its cutoff is comparing two panels and calling the
   difference a repair effect.
Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/STALETAILSCREEN-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py run_staletailscreen
(selftest 362 -> 380), scripts/e97_routing.py TWIN_FUNCS (covered 13 -> 14), research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e118-staletail-screened.md.

## E157 A delegating runner's twin must split the copy from the source -- and check the copy invariant on each arm (2026-10-27 r1190 bm-b)

Some census runners measure nothing themselves: `run_entrydelay` calls `run_midsweep` and
republishes that face's `me_lagK` arms. On such a face every delta has two possible owners,
so a twin that harvests only the republished rows cannot tell a repair effect from a
delegating-layer defect. Three rules carried out of E119:

1) Harvest both layers and name the owner. The join covers the copy (`delay/...`) and the
   arm it was copied from (`gap/...`) separately; the published `delegation_attribution`
   picks one of "both layers moved", "copy only", "source only", "neither", in that order.
   On this face both moved (12 copy / 13 source), i.e. the ladder itself changed -- a
   copy-only reading would have been a bug report, not a finding.
2) Check the copy invariant BEFORE reading any delta. `_entrydelay_copy_mismatch` asserts
   the republished row equals its source on each arm independently. A face whose copy
   disagrees with its own arm is joining the wrong layer and no delta from it is readable.
   This is cheap, deterministic, and the only thing the delegation adds.
3) Thread the seam through every layer. `raw=` had to be added to `run_entrydelay` AND
   already existed on `run_midsweep`; a seam that stops at the outer function would burn
   both arms off the same universe and report a beautiful zero.

Sibling finding worth keeping: **the repair dominated the cutoff axis here** (15.0 vs 6.0 bp
max) whereas on E118 the cutoff was an order of magnitude larger than the repair. Same
family, opposite conclusion -- each face carries its own controls instead of inheriting a
house rule. And shape claims are fragile in a way level claims are not: all four newest-era
rungs stayed negative after the repair (levels survive) while a 9 bp improvement at one rung
flipped monotone to non-monotone (shape does not), because the repair was not proportional
in k.

Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/ENTRYDELAYSCREEN-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
run_entrydelayscreen / _entrydelay_arm_cells / _lag_ladder_read / _delegation_attribution
(selftest 380 -> 393), research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e119-entrydelay-twin.md.

## E158 A frozen bound cannot move: a bracket twin must re-burn its own shoulders (2026-10-27 r1191 bm-b)

`run_neighborhood` publishes a three-point bracket (a session either side of the month
boundary plus the boundary itself) but burns only the centre: its k-1 / k+1 bounds are read
off the E65/E66 faces **on disk**. A bound read from a static file is arm-invariant by
construction, so a same-spec twin of that leg would publish a guaranteed zero shoulder count
— a measurement that always agrees with itself. Three rules carried out of E120:

1) **Re-burn the shoulders you intend to compare.** The twin burns `neighbors=(1,2,3)` so all
   three rungs exist on both arms. The consequence must be disclosed in the same breath: the
   resulting bracket is *not* comparable cell-by-cell with the published face, because the
   published one mixes a fresh centre with frozen bounds.
2) **Name each moved cell by its position, not just by the count.** `_neighborhood_cell_role`
   classifies every cell centre / shoulder / book / baseline, and the published verdict is
   built from the two counts: centre-only = the repair bends the shape; both = it shifts the
   level; nothing = the bracket survives. On this face 28 centre + 36 shoulder + 26 book of
   163 cells moved (max 15 bp) and all four brackets kept their `ramp` label — the repair
   moved the level, not the shape.
3) **Classify arms by magnitude, not sign.** The early arms live in the gaps layer as
   `me_lag-K` (signed) while the published copy renames them `me_mK`; a role keyed on the
   sign or written only for the published spelling sends half the bracket into "other" — the
   first burn did exactly that (8 cells mislabelled) and the mislabel survived every numeric
   check, because the numbers were right and only the naming was wrong. Accept both
   spellings for one magnitude and assert the role of the signed form in selftest.

Sibling finding worth keeping: **the registry is the range** (third recurrence, E115 -> E118
-> E119/E120). `run_entrydelayscreen` was burned in the previous round and never declared in
`TWIN_FUNCS`, so the routing face still called `run_entrydelay` uncovered while the twin sat
on disk; the L15 leg caught it as a red at the open of this round and the face re-derived
`covered_twin 14 -> 16, uncovered 10 -> 8`. The habit is not the fix — the selftest leg is.

Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/NEIGHBORHOODSCREEN-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
run_neighborhoodscreen / _neighborhood_arm_cells / _neighborhood_cell_role /
_neighborhood_shift_verdict (selftest 393 -> 403), scripts/e97_routing.py TWIN_FUNCS
(15/15), research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e120-neighborhood-twin.md.


## E159 A face that prices the calendar cannot be quoted without its role split (2026-10-27 r1192 bm-b)

`run_rebalday` publishes a table of *calendars x books* (month-end-1 / month-end / month-end+1 /
mid-month). A screened twin of it is not the usual "did the numbers move" question, because the
moved cells fall into three kinds with different meanings, and one total blends them:

1) **Publish the split, not the count.** `_rebalday_cell_role` names every moved cell
   `calendar` (rebalance counts, turnover) / `returns` (ann return, drawdown, win rate) /
   `spread` (ladder-vs-wide) / `frame` (bars, years, universe size) / `other`, and the verdict
   is built from the two headline counts: 114 cells, 33 moved, **0 calendar** / 17 returns /
   16 spread. That single zero is the finding — the repair changed WHAT the book earned
   (+0.29…+0.45 pp on the wide book only, spread widening +29…+45 bp) and left WHEN it traded
   bit-identical on all eight calendar rows. A "33 moved" headline would have buried it.
2) **Never harvest a boolean beside numeric columns.** `spread_all_positive` sits in the same
   `answers` block as the bp levels, and `True - False` is a legal subtraction — the join would
   have booked a delta with no units and no complaint. Skip booleans by isinstance, publish the
   flag per arm as a verdict, and pin the absence in selftest.
3) **A cutoff control that looks in the obvious place returns `null`, and `null` reads as
   clean.** This runner's published raw face is written by the `rebalday` CLI mode to
   `results/e41_rebal_day/SHIFT-<cutoff>.json` — not to the twin's own directory and not under
   a `REBALDAY-` prefix. The first burn therefore published `cutoff_control: null`, which is
   indistinguishable from "no older face exists". Factoring the lookup into
   `_rebalday_older_raw` and pointing three selftest legs at a temp dir (finds a real face /
   refuses today's own cutoff / a missing leg directory must not lose the fallback) turned a
   silent absence into a measured control: 40 of 114 cells, max 7.0 bp, so the repair's 45 bp
   dominates the four-session refresh by ~6x.

Sibling reading worth keeping: the two controls disagree by family, not by quality. E118 was
cutoff-dominated (6.0 bp refresh vs a sub-bp repair), E119/E120/E121 are repair-dominated.
A twin that publishes both controls can be compared across families; one that publishes either
alone cannot.

Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/REBALDAYSCREEN-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
run_rebaldayscreen / _rebalday_arm_cells / _rebalday_cell_role / _rebalday_shift_verdict /
_rebalday_older_raw (selftest 403 -> 416), scripts/e97_routing.py TWIN_FUNCS,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e121-rebalday-twin.md.

## E160 An ordering that survives a repair says nothing about the levels inside it (2026-10-28 r1194 bm-b)

The E45 warm-spread leg publishes two ORDERINGS of eras (warm and cold, thinnest to widest)
plus a warm-vs-cold difference per era. Its screened twin (E122) had to answer two questions
that a delta table cannot separate:

1) **Publish the order layer beside the cell layer.** `_warmspread_arm_dates` renders each
   published order as a comparable string (`2024_now>pre2017>2021_2023>2017_2020`) and hands it
   to the same `_burnin_pair_rows` join. Result: 17 of 68 numeric cells moved (max 133 bp) while
   **0 ordering claims moved** -- so `ordering_survives_the_repair=true` AND
   `counts_survive_the_repair=false` at the same time. A twin that read only deltas would have
   said "the repair reaches this face" and stopped there, missing the one claim the leg exists
   to make. Pinned by a selftest leg that flips an order in a synthetic face and asserts
   `cells_moved==0` while `gate_dates_moved==1`.
2) **A zero can be the control that licenses the reading.** The ladder book is a fixed
   five-name list, so a repaired print can never enter it: 0 of its cells moved while 8 wide
   cells moved. That zero is what makes the 8 moves attributable to the repair rather than to
   the join -- without a book the repair cannot reach, "the numbers moved" has no owner.
3) **Leg attribution must key on the SUFFIX.** First live burn reported `0 wide / 13 other`
   beside a worst cell derived from the wide book, because the test was `"/wide/" in start`
   and the harvest keys end with the leg name (`era/2021_2023/warm/wide`) with no trailing
   slash. E94's "a label must not be narrower than its evidence" family, third recurrence;
   the face now publishes `moved_attribution_complete` so a bucket that fails to sum to
   `cells_moved` is visible to the reader, and two selftest legs pin both the wrong test
   (matches nothing) and the right one (finds warm AND cold wide).

Cross-family reading: E95/E157 said levels are not levels; E122 says the same for an ORDERING's
implied levels. Both orders held, yet two of the newest era's three headline levels changed
sign under 67 neutralised prints of 1,317 names (cold spread -55 -> +25 bp, live-window spread
-92 -> +41 bp, cold_minus_live 37 -> -16). Quoting "the newest era is thinnest" is safe;
quoting any 2024-now bp figure off this face is not.

Evidence: results/e44_midmonth/WARMSPREADSCREEN-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
run_warmspreadscreened / _warmspread_arm_cells / _warmspread_ordering / _warmspread_arm_dates
(selftest 393 -> 427), research/digests/DIGEST-20261027-e122-warmspread-screened.md.
## E161 A fix is only live where the scheduler actually runs it (2026-10-28 r1195 bm-b)

The hourly oversight task does not run `Tools/fleet-oversight.ps1`; it runs a deployed
copy at `<KRoot>/.codely-cli/oversight-<id>/fleet-oversight.ps1`. Nothing compared the two.
D-20261028-02 repaired the machine-health writer in the repo (in fact only in one
uncommitted worktree), the deployed copy stayed at v1.0 from 2026-10-11, and the very next
hourly fire re-broke `fleet/machine-health.json` into the exact `{"value": ...}` nesting the
repair removed. The timeline is the tell: face rebuilt clean at 00:22, broken at 01:11, zero
repo edits in between -- one scheduled fire.

Two rules fall out. (a) Any task whose entry point is COPIED outside the repo needs a drift
leg that compares the deployed file to its source (`oversight_deploy.ps1 -Check` -> 0
identical / 1 drift / 2 never deployed), wired as a smoke leg so stale running code is RED
instead of a damaged shared face that a reader has to notice. (b) Deploy tooling must resolve
the SAME path the task uses, not a tidy variant of it: `oversight-bm-b` (dashed) and
`oversight-bmb` (what the 2026-10-11 registration runs) both "look right", and deploying to
the wrong one reproduces the very bug the tool exists to catch -- caught here only because
the first deploy wrote 20,923 chars into a directory no task reads.

This is the same family as the worker-reads-the-code-on-disk-at-spawn pit, one step earlier:
before the worker reads stale code, the scheduler started it.

Evidence: group FluxGroup `76bf885c` (Tools/fleet-oversight.ps1 fix + Tools/oversight_deploy.ps1),
bigmoney smoke leg `fleet: oversight writer's deployed copy tracks its source` (104/104 PASS),
rebuild + live fire of the DEPLOYED writer (machine-health: OK machines=1 junk=0),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1195-deployed-writer-drift.md.

## E162 A drawdown profile is two sentences, and a repair can move one of them (2026-10-28 r1196 bm-b)

E40 publishes, per window, three profiles (ladder5, the wide book, the ratio of their curves)
and reads them as "drawdown shape". Burning the same leg on the raw and on the print-screened
arm moves 30 of 75 cells with every percent delta under 0.5pp -- and flips the sign of
`underwater_delta_sessions` (+48 -> -92) while the wide book's longest underwater run falls
1094 -> 954 sessions. The book's drawdown ADVANTAGE is intact (maxdd delta +6.17 -> +6.59, same
sign); its underwater TIMING is not. Quote the two separately or you are reporting an average
of two different claims.

Two rules. (a) A twin's "arrow layer" must enumerate every answer the reader consumes as a
direction -- the first version of this leg listed the spread and drawdown-delta signs, missed
the underwater sign, and printed `arrows_survive: true` directly beside the flip; the fix is
module-level `_pair_arrows` + an explicit column tuple + a selftest leg per column, not a
wider if-statement inside the runner. (b) A cutoff control that is the same SIZE as the repair
does not rank the two: here the old-face refresh moves 28 cells (max 5) against the repair's
30, the opposite of E122 where the repair dominated 3.6x. Same family, opposite ordering --
so each face ships its own two controls and neither number is quoted alone.

Evidence: results/e40_pair_drawdown/PAIRSCREEN-20261027.json, per-arm checkpoints,
_pair_arm_cells/_pair_arm_dates/_pair_arrows + run_pairscreened (selftest 427 -> 442),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1196-e123-pair-screened.md.

## E163 A decomposition has two axes and a repair can move one of each (2026-10-28 r1197 bm-b)

E43 publishes each era's spread split into a size-neutral alpha and a size beta term, per era.
Burning the raw and print-screened arms of that leg moves 17 of 93 cells, and every moved cell
sits on the attribution the face exists to publish (16 era / 0 skin / 1 answer). Two readings
the totals hide: (a) the repair lands on ONE component -- the size base term moves at most
3.1bp while the size-neutral alpha moves +22 to +47bp, so "the spread moved 20bp" and "the
size-adjusted sleeve moved 20bp" are different sentences; (b) it lands on SOME eras --
`2017_2020` moves 0 of its 24 cells while the newest era's spread flips sign (-21.6 -> +25.1).
When a face is a per-era attribution, publish the component split AND the per-era split, and
verify the decomposition sums on both arms (here `incomplete_eras: []` at 0.2bp) -- otherwise a
split that silently stops adding up makes the attribution unreadable while every individual
number still looks plausible.

Sibling rules, all three already in this file and all three re-confirmed here: a level is not
a sign (the newest era's `tilt_annualised` moves -114bp across a seven-day cutoff refresh,
twice the largest repair delta, while only the sign is quotable); a cutoff control that is
larger than the repair does not let the repair be quoted alone (E122 repair 3.6x, E123 same
size, E124 refresh 1.7x/2.3x -- three orderings, so quote both controls every time); and a
leg's own docstring is prose evidence -- this one claimed a 2013 reach-back that the face
never delivered, because the binding universe member changed under it.

Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/TILT4SCREEN-20261027.json, per-arm checkpoints,
_tilt4_arm_cells/_tilt4_arm_dates/_tilt4_arrows + run_tilt4screened (selftest 442 -> 459),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1197-e124-tilt4-screened.md.

## E164 A multiplier axis survives a repair only if the levels move uniformly (2026-10-28 r1198 bm-b)

E40 bounds the cash leg by re-running one spec at repo multipliers 0x/1x/3x. Burning the raw and
print-screened arms of that leg moves 18 of 63 cells -- and the moved delta is IDENTICAL at all
three rate points (+0.36pp annualised at x0, x1 and x3) while the derived attributions
(ladder 36.0bp, wide 37.0bp, x3 109/111bp) do not move at all. That uniformity is the test, not
a coincidence: a repair that touched the rate exposure would have to move the three points by
different amounts. So when a face has an axis that is a TRANSFORMATION of the answer
(multiplier, cost level, horizon), publish the per-point deltas side by side -- a uniform delta
says the axis is orthogonal to the repair, a gradient says the repair reached it, and the total
cannot tell you which.

Sibling rule learned in the same burn: a zero on the CONTROL book is not a clean bill. The
ladder5 rows move 0 of 27 cells here, and 0 of theirs in E121/E123, because none of the five
ladder names is among the 67 names the screen neutralises -- the control arm is a control. A
twin has to publish that fact mechanically (``ladder_exposure``: names neutralised, the ladder
intersection, plus the ladder names whose candidate prints were declined as gapped), or a reader
reads "0 moved" as "the repair is harmless". Declined candidates are part of the claim: two
ladder names carry +248.55% and +176.27% gapped prints that the screen keeps today.

Evidence: results/e40_cashleg_drag/CASHLegSCREEN-20261027.json, per-arm checkpoints,
_cash_arm_cells/_cash_arm_dates/_cash_arrows/_ladder_exposure + run_cashdragscreened
(selftest 459 -> 476), research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1198-e125-cashdrag-screened.md.

## E165 Two counts are not a comparison until they sit on the same cells, and a missing census is not a null census (2026-10-28 r1199 bm-b)

E122-E125 each published "the repair moved X cells, the data refresh moved Y" and let the reader
rank them. Collecting all fourteen twin faces into one ledger
(`results/e79/SCREENREACH-2026-10-27.json`) shows the ranking is **not** uniform: 5 faces are
repair-dominated, 5 refresh-dominated, 3 tied -- the family's four published orderings (3.6x,
equal, equal, refresh-bigger) were a sample, not a law. Two ways the ratio lies:

1. **Different denominators.** `NEIGHBORHOODSCREEN` publishes 90 moved of 163 repair cells beside
   a cutoff control that shares only 9 cells: 90/3 = 30x reads as the biggest repair in the family
   and is an artefact of the refresh arm having a ninth of the surface. Publish `cells_compared`
   on BOTH sides, flag the mismatch, and keep those rows out of any matched tally.
2. **Absent census read as empty census.** Two declared twins (`NEUTRAL`, `ROBUST`) publish no
   per-name screen census. Treating the missing block as "zero names neutralised" prints
   `ladder_row_is_a_control = true` for a face that never said which names it touched. A verdict
   the face cannot support is `null` + a flag, and the aggregate must count those rows separately
   (29 of 32 have a census; the ladder reading is only asserted over those 29).

Third finding worth carrying: the ladder-control fact itself is published by exactly ONE of the
29 faces that rely on it. A reading every sibling depends on belongs in the shared ledger, not in
the newest face that happened to think of it -- which is what this leg exists to be.

Evidence: results/e79/SCREENREACH-2026-10-27.json, scripts/e97_routing.py (`screenreach` mode,
selftest 15 -> 29), research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1199-e127-screen-reach-ledger.md.
## E162 Publish the horizon a reader can wait for, not the bars a test would need (2026-10-28 r1316 bm-c)

A significance block that only answers "how many bars for a t=2 test" is unreadable when the
noise is large: over this book's 8.46%/yr excess dispersion a 3pp edge needs 7,758 bars
(31.8 years) and a 2pp edge has no finite row at all, so the honest answer to "is the book
beating buy-and-hold yet" degrades to "never". The reverse question is answerable today and
is the one a holder asks: `E = z_power * sd_annual / sqrt(H / BARS_PER_YEAR)` with
z_power = 2.802 (80% power, two-sided 5%) gives the smallest resolvable edge inside 6m/1y/
2y/3y (33.5 / 23.7 / 16.8 / 13.7 pp/yr here), and `Y = (z_power * sd_annual / E)^2` prices
the edges the book actually published (2.03pp -> 136.3 y, 5.85pp -> 16.4 y). Two rules:
(a) candidate edges are READ from the studies' own result files with their units labelled
(the stored alpha is a fraction -- r1292's 100x class), never typed into the power table, and
a missing study is omitted with a reason; (b) the conclusion must be printed, not implied --
a ledger that can never adjudicate its own edge is a health check, and the verdict has to
come from a fixed-window out-of-sample study instead. Evidence: scripts/etf_book_forward.py
`power_table()` (selftest 43 legs), LEDGER `benchmark.significance.power`, smoke 113/113,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1316-power-floor.md.
## E166 A window that is really a burn-in gate must say so, and an identity's residual must be quoted with the share it was measured at (2026-10-28 r1200 bm-b)

The E40 cash leg publishes one window (`first trading rebalance 262`, `cash 263 bars = 15.95%`)
and one attribution per book, but only the ladder's window. Adding the per-book window
(`run_cashdrag.cash_window_by_book`) answers two questions the face could not:

1. **Is the ladder/wide attribution gap (36.0 vs 37.0bp) a window effect?** No. Both books first
   trade on the same month end in both tested windows (`same_window_for_both_books=true`,
   `ladder_gap_from_window_bp=0.0`, `ladder_gap_unexplained_bp=-1.0`). A wider universe can only
   make a book eligible EARLIER, never later, so a shared window means the gap is valuation.
2. **Is the repo stretch a market fact?** No. `base.elig` demands `MIN_BARS=250` non-missing
   closes on the WINDOW-LOCAL price array, so every window sits in repo until the first month end
   past bar 250: measured `bars_beyond_burn_in` = 12 (2020 window) and 13 (2023 window). "The book
   is in cash for 13 months" is the backtest's own burn-in, not scarce investable names.

The identity `rate_pct x share_pct = bp` closes to 4.42bp (ladder) / 5.42bp (wide) on the 2020
window, but to 20.13/21.13bp on a 2023 start where the share is 28.66% instead of 15.95% -- the
ratio `measured/expected` rises 1.14 -> 1.39 with the share, i.e. the linear sum is a LOWER bound
and the extra is the cash gain compounding at the book's own return after the repo stretch.
Consequence for the tolerance: a single absolute band declared on one window is a band on that
window's share, not a property of the identity -- quote the residual beside `cash_bar_share_pct`
or scale the band with it, otherwise a second window silently "proves a defect".

Evidence: results/e40_cashleg_drag/CASHLeg-20261027.json (`cash_window_by_book`,
`cash_window_note`), results/e40_cashleg_drag/IDENTITY-20261027.json (two windows),
scripts/e39_panel_census.py (`_first_trade_index`, `_window_is_burn_in_driven`, `_cash_identity`,
selftest 476 -> 488), research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1200-e128-cash-window-identity.md.

## E167 A skin that reaches further is a different reading, and its price must be published (2026-10-28 r1201 bm-b)

A "reach-back" wider window is not free evidence: in the size-tilt family the five-member,
four-member and three-member skins each price a *different* small leg (3 names / 2 names /
`sh510500` alone), so 2013-2016 is measurable only by the narrowest one. Publishing that number
as "the tilt" would present the most compositionally extreme arm as the identity.

Two rules fall out, and both are mechanical:

1. **Reach is a predicate, not a claim.** Which months a skin can price is decided by one
   function (here `_skin_months`: every member of both legs present on both books). Name it and
   give it a liveness selftest asserting the narrow skin reaches a month the wide one cannot,
   otherwise "we reach 2013" is a docstring.
2. **A delta across different month sets is an artefact, not a price.** Publish both
   denominators beside every comparison and set the delta to `null` + a reason string when they
   differ; never let a null collapse to 0.0, and never print a ratio between unequal cells.

Reading that motivated it: pre2017 tilt +1186.4bp/yr (44 months) with the size-neutral leg only
+134.4bp, then -923.9bp/yr in 2017_2020 -- the sign is era-dependent, and the 4-vs-3-member
composition price swings -69.4 / +343.7 / -120.7bp across the eras that share months.

Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/TILT3REACH-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
(`TILT3_SMALL`, `_skin_months`, `run_tilt3`, `_tilt3_arm_cells`, selftest 488 -> 504),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1201-e126-tilt3-reachback.md.

## E168 A mean's width is not its depth: publish leave-one-out beside the counting rule (2026-10-28 r1202 bm-b)

Two numbers, both cheap, decide whether an era mean is quotable:

1. **Leave-one-out span** -- re-derive the mean with each month removed once. A mean carried by a
   handful of months has a wide span, and the heaviest single month is named.
2. **Months to half** -- order months by their own contribution *in the sign of the mean* and
   count how many reach a declared half of the total. A "44-month era" that needs 2 months to
   reach half is 2 months of evidence with 42 months of context, and that is a different claim
   from "robust because the window is long".

Neither replaces the other: span answers "how far can one month move it", the counting rule
answers "how concentrated is it". Publish both, plus each month's share, and declare the half
threshold before the burn (`TILT3_HALF_SHARE`), not after looking.

Decomposition rule that came with it: when a reading is the difference of two legs, publish each
leg's own annualised return and check the identity (`small - large == published tilt`, residual
0.0bp here) instead of fitting a second model; a difference of two *positive* legs is not a
"one side beat the other" statement.

Readings that motivated it (three-member tilt skin, cutoff 2026-10-27): pre2017 is +1186.4bp/yr
over 44 months but its LOMO span is 643.7..1997.5bp with 2014-12 alone worth -64.5% of the sum and
two months reaching half; both legs are positive (+683.5 large / +1869.9 small), so the tilt is a
ratio story, not a rotation story. The **newest** era is the fragile one: 2024_now +252.8bp flips
sign under a single drop (-139.4..+768.4, 2026-07 = -195.0% of the sum) while the era everyone
calls thin keeps its sign. Long window != safe reading; recent window != safe reading.

Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/TILT3LEGS-20261027.json, scripts/e39_panel_census.py
(`_leg_monthly`, `_lomo_sweep`, `run_tilt3legs`, selftest 504 -> 521),
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1202-e168-tilt3-leg-split.md.

## E169 "The repair moved the family" is a per-cell claim, and a flipped sign is a claim of its own (2026-10-28 r1204 bm-b)

A screened twin answers two questions that a single max-delta number merges: *how much* moved and
*where*. E170 burned the E126 three-member tilt on both arms off one load (137 cells joined) and the
shape of the answer was not "everything moved a little":

1. **Publish the per-era arm pair, not just the delta.** pre2017 moved by exactly 0.0 bp on all four
   of its columns while 2021_2023 and 2024_now moved by 17-57 bp. A summary "18 of 137 cells moved,
   max 57 bp" cannot tell a reader which era's published headline is still safe to quote; the era
   table can.
2. **A regressor identical on both arms is a result.** `tilt_bp_delta = 0.0` on every era says the
   tilt is a ratio of member returns and the repair reaches the engine's own path instead. Publish it
   as a finding and keep the engine column in the same table, or the zero reads as a failed burn.
3. **A sign that flips under the repair must be published as a sign.** 2024_now's raw spread went
   -21.6 -> +25.1 bp/yr: "the wide book lost that era" was an artefact of the unscreened panel. The
   magnitude table shows +46.7 bp and hides the rewrite; the arrow list shows it in one line.
4. **A control with nothing to compare against is null *plus a reason*.** The only published raw face
   carried the same cutoff, so the stale-face control could not run; the face says so in words.
   Null must never be readable as "clean" (E164 family).

How to apply: any "the repair/screen/regeneration moved it" verdict publishes (a) a per-era or
per-cell arm pair, (b) a separate list of cells whose *sign* changed, (c) the number of harvested
cells that moved by exactly zero, and (d) an explicit reason wherever a control is unavailable. All
four are cheap once the join exists; none of them can be reconstructed from a max delta afterwards.


## E170 A regressor's sign is not the book's sign, and an era agreement can be pure level (2026-10-28 r1205 bm-b)

Three-member tilt vs the engine's own wide-vs-ladder spread, 162 joined months, four eras.

1. **Two of four eras point opposite ways.** 2017_2020: tilt -923.9 bp/yr beside a +331.7 bp/yr traded
   spread; 2024_now: +252.8 beside -21.6. A tilt is a ratio of member returns; the spread is what a
   portfolio is paid. Publishing one row without the other lets "small caps led" be read as "the wide
   book got paid", which in 2017_2020 is exactly backwards.
2. **Publish the month-level agreement beside the era-level one.** No era cleared 57% (range 23.5-56.8),
   and the full window's echo of its own agreeing arrows is 43.8% -- the era agreement is a statement
   about two LEVELS while the monthly series underneath is a coin flip. One number without the other
   reads as "the signal works".
3. **A deadband must be declared before the burn and printed beside the raw rate.** Months where either
   series is under 10 bp are not signs. On 2024_now the deadband makes the reading worse (23.5 -> 18.8),
   which is why it is published rather than applied silently.
4. **Cross-check the derived series against the sibling face's own column.** engine_mean_ann_bp
   reproduces TILT3SCREEN's spread_raw_bp_raw exactly on all five rows (287.2 / 331.7 / 251.1 / -21.6 /
   227.6): a re-derive that lands on the published number is the cheapest proof the join is the same one.

How to apply: any "factor X says the book did Y" claim publishes (a) the traded quantity beside the
factor per period, (b) both scales of sign agreement with their denominators, (c) any deadband as a
pre-declared constant with the raw rate retained, and (d) one exact reconciliation against an already
published column from the same family. A tie counts as a disagreement, never as an agreement.




## E171 A repair can move a level without moving a rate, and equality is not deterioration (2026-10-28 r1206 bm-b)



Two findings from the screened twin of the tilt-vs-engine sign face

(`results/e43_tilt_4member/TILT3SCREENSIGN-20261027.json`).



1. **Level and rate answer different questions about the same repair.** The

   split-event repair moved 5 of 78 cells, every one of them an era LEVEL

   (engine +29.3 / +46.7 / +16.3bp; 2024_now flips sign -21.6 -> +25.1) and

   moved ZERO rate cells: month-level agreement is bit-identical on both arms

   (45.8 / 44.4 / 23.5 / 43.8 / 56.8%). So a repaired era whose sign verdict

   flips can still be an era whose month-by-month information is unchanged --

   and if that rate is 4 of 17 months, the post-repair "agreement" is a level

   with almost no resolution behind it. Publish both scales; a flip on one is

   not a flip on the other.

2. **A boolean derived from `>` silently pronounces "worse" on equality.** The

   first cut published `recovered = screened > raw` and its negation as

   `worsens`, which printed "worsens in all five eras" beside five pairs of

   identical numbers. Three-state the change: `recovered` / `worsened` /

   `unchanged` (+ `not_measurable` when an arm is missing), and pin equality in

   the selftest. Same family as E94: the label was narrower than the evidence.



How to apply: when a twin is expected to move only one column, split the join by

column prefix (tilt_* vs engine_*) and report each bucket's moved count and its

sign flips separately -- and re-measure an earlier invariance claim on the new

leg rather than importing it. Any "better/worse" verdict computed from a

two-state comparison of two rates needs an explicit "equal" branch before it is

allowed to print.


## E172 A constant nobody sweeps is a claim nobody checked (2026-10-28 r1207 bm-b)



E169 declared a 10bp monthly deadband before its burn and published both the raw

and the deadband rate. On 2024_now the deadband made the reading *worse*

(23.5% -> 18.8%), and that single pair cannot distinguish "this era is

anti-correlated" from "this era is noisy and the band ate the evidence". Sweeping

the same constant over 0/10/25/50bp, with the surviving month count printed at

every step, answers it: 2024_now reads 23.5 / 18.8 / 17.2 / 17.4% on 34 / 32 / 29

/ 23 months -- the rate is low at *every* band, so the collapse is not the band

choosing its sample.



1. **A band's rate is meaningless without its denominator.** The sweep publishes

   `months_outside_deadband` beside every rate and labels any band that keeps

   fewer than 6 months `thin` instead of printing a percentage from 3 months. On

   this panel no era goes thin even at 50bp (98 of 162 months survive), which is

   itself a published answer (`first_band_with_any_thin_era = null`), not an

   absence of one.

2. **A sweep of a filter cannot move a level, and saying so is part of the

   table.** The deadband drops months; it never edits a return, so `tilt_sign`,

   `engine_sign` and `signs_agree` are identical at all four bands *by

   construction*. Four rows of the same signs are one finding. The face publishes

   `signs_band_invariant` per era so a reader cannot mistake construction for

   corroboration (E94 family: the label must not be wider than the evidence).

3. **Stability of the verdict and stability of the number are different

   questions.** Verdicts here are stable (all four eras keep their side of the

   50% line at every band with support), while the *numbers* move a lot:

   2021_2023 spans 44.4 -> 20.0 (24.4pp). Reporting only "verdict stable" would

   hide that the band is a strong lever on the level; reporting only the span

   would hide that the sign of the reading never flips. Publish both.

4. **Direction of the movement is a finding.** pre2017's agreement *rises* with

   the band (56.8 -> 67.9) while the other three eras fall -- its agreement is

   carried by the large months, theirs by the small ones. A single band value

   hides which kind of era you are looking at.



How to apply: any pre-declared constant that gates a published rate gets a sweep

row (0 / declared / 2.5x / 5x) with its denominator beside it, a support floor

below which the cell reads `thin`, and an explicit note for constants that are

band-invariant by construction. Sweeping is a sensitivity table on an already

published reading -- it selects nothing, so it needs no new prereg; but the

band that is *quoted* must remain the one declared before the first burn.

## E173 A cross-run difference is not a capacity cost (2026-10-28 r1319 bm-c)

The capital-placement face (`scripts/capital_placement.py`,
`results/capital_placement.json`, `research/CAPITAL_PLACEMENT.md`) put the book,
the four-ETF basket and the futures sleeve on one axis -- account size -- for the
first time. Three reusable readings:

1. **The first version of the decomposition charged a run difference to the
   ceiling.** It split the "basket minus book" gap into `rule gap` + `capacity
   gap` and the capacity term printed **+2.34pp/yr at CNY 300k**, where the book
   deploys every yuan and the ceiling cannot bind. The 2.34pp is the distance
   between the spanning face's headline CAGR (9.62%) and the capacity face's own
   return on deployed capital (7.28%) -- two published runs that do not reproduce
   each other even with no size limit. Charging it to the ceiling would have made
   capacity look worse than it is at every tier. Fixed by three terms that add up
   exactly (`rule gap` / `run diff` / `capacity cost`), with the middle one printed
   as **unattributed** and a selftest leg asserting it does not move with capital.
2. **A "capacity cost" term must be proven to bind.** The same selftest family
   requires it to be exactly 0 under the ceiling and > 0 well above it -- the r1261
   "prove the mechanism ran" rule applied to a limit rather than to a spread.
3. **One like-for-like pair, one measured pair.** The rule gap (basket vs book
   rule, same spanning run) is +0.47pp/yr and constant; the capacity cost rises
   0 -> +5.33pp/yr across the ladder. The headline the allocator needs is the one
   the ladder prints, not the rule-level 0.47pp: at CNY 1M the achievable gap is
   CNY 28,100/yr, and the futures sleeve below its one-lot floor earns repo only.

How to apply: when a face compares two artefacts produced by DIFFERENT scripts,
first ask whether their overlapping cells agree; if they do not, publish the
disagreement as its own term instead of folding it into whichever mechanism you
were trying to price. Any split whose terms are supposed to sum needs a leg that
asserts the sum (and a tolerance that scales with the base, or the rounding of a
3-decimal pp times CNY 50M fails the leg on rounding alone).

## E174 A two-book spread's delta must name its carriers, and a verdict that dies with one name is not a verdict (2026-10-28 r1208 bm-b)

E171 published that the split-event repair moves the ENGINE column of the
tilt-vs-engine face and flips 2024_now's era verdict. The engine column is
wide-minus-ladder, i.e. a difference of two held books, so a +46.7bp era delta
can in principle be one name-month. E174 decomposes it by booking every day's
`w * r` per `(month, symbol)` inside the simulator for both books (an optional
`contrib` sink, default off) and reading `wide - ladder` per name.

1. **A spread delta is attributable, and the attribution must reconstruct the
   published number.** Contributions are the same terms the curve compounds, so
   the named carriers sum to the delta minus an intra-month compounding residual.
   Publish that residual per era: 2024_now -2.90bp on a 46.7bp delta, 2021_2023
   -0.90bp. A residual of zero would have to be asserted; a quoted one can be
   checked.
2. **An era with `delta = 0.0` has no carriers — do not name one.** Naming the
   largest |contribution| beside a zero delta reads as a finding while carrying
   none; the face empties the carrier list and says why.
3. **A name held by both books nets out, and the cash leg is a pseudo-name.**
   Dropping the un-invested residual from an attribution makes it read complete
   while being short; it gets a key (`__cash__`) and shows up in the totals.
4. **The drop test is the point, and "survives" is not monotone in how much you
   drop.** 2024_now keeps the flip with no carriers dropped, loses it when the
   single biggest carrier (`sh510580`, -14.51bp, all of it in 2024-09) is
   removed, and regains it when the top five are removed. Two drop sizes
   disagreeing is itself the finding: the era verdict is a property of the
   carrier composition, not of the books. Publishing only the top-1 drop ("the
   flip disappears") or only the top-5 drop ("the flip survives") would each be
   a defensible-looking half of the story.
5. **Share of delta needs a sign convention.** A carrier whose contribution runs
   against the delta prints a NEGATIVE share (`sh510580` = -31.1% of +46.7bp).
   Flipping it to an absolute share would hide the direction of the repair.

How to apply: any published delta computed as a difference of two books gets a
name-level carrier table with (a) the residual, (b) a coverage ladder (how many
names for 50%/80%), (c) a drop-the-carrier survival test at two sizes, and (d) an
empty carrier list when the delta is zero. Attribution is descriptive: it selects
nothing, so it needs no new prereg, but it must reuse the one burn path (a
separate implementation would measure a different book).

## E175 A ladder must start where the published face starts: a decomposition's own levels are not the level it is explaining (2026-10-28 r1209 bm-b)

E174 ran a carrier-drop test off the decomposition's own era levels and published
"the flip is gone at top1, back at top5". The first cut of E175 found its ladder's
k=0 verdict disagreeing with the headline it was supposed to start from, and the
reason generalises: **the per-name terms are sums of daily `w*r` inside a month
while the published level is a compounded monthly mean.** On 2024_now the two
bases sit 52.9bp apart -- more than the 46.7bp delta being attributed -- and the
offset alone flips the raw arm's sign (+31.3 vs -21.6). Any drop read off the
unanchored base measures the offset, not the carrier.

1. **Anchor a counterfactual on the published level, not on its decomposition.**
   A contribution is a DELTA quantity (E174 reproduces the arm difference to
   2.9bp) even when the levels differ by tens of bp. So subtract contributions
   from the published level (`_drop_ladder(base_raw=, base_scr=)`) and make k=0
   equal the headline by construction; publish the offset beside it, plus a
   boolean `offset_alone_flips_*_sign` for each arm.
2. **Two bases, same carriers, opposite verdicts = a selftest leg.** The fixture
   is two arms whose decomposition sums are both positive while one published
   level is negative, so a future caller cannot silently publish an unanchored
   ladder as the ladder.
3. **Run the ladder on a flat era as its own control.** Eras with a zero delta
   have zero moved carriers: the ladder must be a single rung, and a ladder that
   is not flat there is broken before it reads anything about the era you care
   about. The eras that did move but never flip (2021_2023: 625 carriers, "agree"
   at every k) are the second control -- what a verdict not carried by a few
   months looks like.
4. **Publish delta coverage with every rung, and flag past 100%.** The answer is
   often "the verdict holds until you have removed more than the delta is made
   of"; that is only visible when each step carries
   `share_of_delta_removed_pct` and a `beyond_delta_span` flag. The `full` era's
   seven verdict bands all live past k=1160 of 1193 carriers -- noise, flagged,
   not suppressed.
5. **A published face read off the wrong base is corrected by a new face, not by
   editing the old one.** The frozen face keeps its numbers; the new face carries
   a machine-readable `corrections` block naming the field, the issue and the
   corrected reading, and the digest says which one to quote.

How to apply: any drop/bootstrap/counterfactual that starts from a decomposed
quantity starts from the published quantity instead; the decomposition's own
levels are a diagnostic, never a base. And when a new leg's own k=0 or t=0 does
not reproduce the headline it is extending, stop and check the base before
publishing -- that mismatch is the cheapest defect detector in the family.

## E176 A two-book column's delta must be split by BOOK before any book is named (2026-10-28 r1210 bm-b)

The engine column of the tilt family is `wide book - ladder book`, and E174 named its
carriers name-by-name. Every one of those carriers is a net of two held books, so the
table can be perfectly audited while the sentence built on it is false: if the repair
re-priced the fixed ladder leg, "the wide book won the era" is the wrong sentence even
though its arithmetic is right. Rebuilding the delta as `wide_delta - ladder_delta` from
each book's own contrib sink gave a cleaner answer than any name table: the ladder side
is 0.0bp in all five eras, every moved era is `wide_only`, and the zero is STRUCTURAL --
67 events were neutralised in the panel and none of them is one of the five fixed ladder
members, a fact derived from the screen report rather than asserted.

Two habits follow. Publish the book split beside the name split, because they answer
different sentences. And when one side reads exactly zero, say whether it is a measured
zero or a structural one: "no member of that book was repaired" and "that book was
unaffected after adjustment" are different claims, and only the first one is derivable
from the report. The same leg re-measured E175's base offset from a second direction
(+52.9/+55.8bp on 2024_now), which is a reminder that the DELTA is the quantity that
reproduces and the decomposition's levels are a diagnostic.

How to apply: for any published difference of two held books, split the delta by book
before naming a name, and derive "this side cannot move" from the repair report instead
of publishing a bare zero beside a moved headline.

## E177 A decomposition's level offset is NOT the compounding gap: compounding the difference moves it the wrong way (2026-10-28 r1211 bm-b)

E175 measured the offset between the per-name decomposition's era level and the published
level at up to 55.8bp and, on the strength of a code comment, called it "the compounding
gap"; E176 reprinted the same number and left it explicitly unexplained. Measured three
ways on both arms and every era, that sentence does not hold.

The three readings per era: (a) the sum of the daily `w*r` terms the decomposition
publishes, (b) those same daily terms compounded inside the month, (c) the published
object, i.e. each book compounded on its own and the engine column taken as that
difference (rebuilt from the daily walk it reproduces the published 1-decimal levels to
0.0485bp, so the wiring is sound). At BOOK level (b) and (c) are the same computation --
that identity is why the offset looks like compounding and why it survives a sanity
check. At ENGINE level they are not: compounding a difference is not the difference of
two compoundings, and what separates them is a cross term, not a rounding artefact.

Measured: offset +28.41 / +30.51 / -1.19 / -49.49 / +6.11 bp on raw (identical on the two
pre-2021 eras when screened, -2.10 / -52.45 / +5.28 elsewhere); compounding the difference
(b)-(a) moves it by -20.10 / -4.54 / +2.19 / +14.03 / -3.37 bp -- the WRONG SIGN in every
era, "explaining" between -70.7% and -184.0% of it. Residual (c)-(b) is 48.51 / 35.04 /
-3.38 / -63.51 / +9.48 bp raw and up to -67.21bp screened: in all 10 arm-era cells the
cross term remains, so no tolerance makes the decomposition quotable for levels.

How to apply: a decomposition that reproduces the DELTA to a couple of bp is not thereby
usable for the LEVEL. Publish (a),(b),(c) side by side and read the SIGN of (b)-(a): if
compounding were the cause, step and offset would share a sign and the residual would
vanish. Anchor every name-level leg on the published level and quote the offset as a
tolerance, never as an explanation. Sibling habit: a book-level identity passing its
sanity check proves nothing about the engine level, where the identity no longer holds.


## E178 A fixed leg's zero is a fact about MEMBERSHIP, not about the strategy (2026-10-28 r1212 bm-b)

E176 split the engine column's repair delta by book and published the ladder leg at exactly
0.0 in every era, with the cause derived from the screen report: no ladder member carries a
repair event. That reads like a property of the construction. It is a property of the
membership set, and the difference matters the moment somebody quotes it for a different
fixed composition.

Measured on three pinned skins (tilt5 / tilt4 / tilt3, all subsets of the same five ladder
names), per member, per era, both arms: the maximum absolute per-member repair delta across
every pinned member and every era is **exactly 0.0 bp** -- a structural zero, not a small
number -- while the control that rotates its members (the wide book) reads +30.204 /
+49.598 / +17.121 bp on 2021_2023 / 2024_now / full with 7 / 45 / 52 repaired names. The
pinned universe is disjoint from all 67 repaired names at that cutoff.

So the invariant transfers exactly as far as "membership stays out of the repair set", and
no further. Three skins printing zero is ONE fact reported three times, not three
confirmations; a family-wide reading of it is unearned.

How to apply: when a leg prints zero, publish the branch that produced it --
`pinned_no_repair_event` / `carries_repair_event_flat` / `carries_repair_event_moved`. A
fixed leg that holds a repaired member and still reads zero is a wiring defect, and it must
be a word, not an indistinguishable 0.0. Two arithmetic habits ride along: (a) a month
priced on only one arm is dropped, never read as a zero return (otherwise the measurement
fabricates the very delta it reports), and (b) annualising a per-member MEAN takes
`mean*12*1e4`, while the family's `_era_side_bp` convention multiplies a monthly SUM by
`12/n*1e4` -- copying that scale onto an average halves every number at n=2 and looks
plausible. Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/TILT3LEGFAMILY-20261027.json,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1212-e178-leg-family.md.

## E179 A side-level zero must be re-published per holding before it can be quoted (2026-10-28 r1213 bm-b)

E176 published `ladder_book_delta_bp = 0.0` in every era for a fixed five-name leg. That is a
SUM over five members plus a `__cash__` residual the leg books, and a zero sum has three
different meanings: every holding stayed put, two holdings moved in opposite directions and
cancelled, or the leg holds none of the names the repair touched. Only the first is evidence
about the strategy.

Measured per holding, per era, both arms: the gross absolute delta is also exactly **0.0 bp**
in all five eras, so the net zero is five zeros and not a cancellation (`classification =
flat_per_holding`; `eras_cancelling = []`). The members are not all present in every era --
pre2017 holds three (510050/510300/510500), 2017_2020 holds four, from 2021_2023 all five --
so "the fixed book is five names" is itself an era-dependent statement, and a missing member
is disclosed as `members_missing` rather than silently weighted to zero. The cash residual is
not a security: it books only in the 15 earliest months of the panel (2012-05..2013-07) and
carries 1.39%-3.0% of the ladder level in the two eras that overlap them.

How to apply: whenever a published level is a sum over a fixed basket, publish the basket's
per-holding levels beside the net and classify the zero (`flat_per_holding` / `cancelling` /
`moving`). A net-over-gross ratio near zero is the tell that the sum is hiding offsetting
moves. Two sibling habits: label a pseudo-name (`__cash__`) as a residual so it can never be
counted as a holding, and publish holdings the sink turns up but the basket does not declare
as `unlisted` instead of folding them in. Evidence:
results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERSIDECASH-20261027.json,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1213-e179-ladder-side-cash-split.md.
## E181 A level that survives every single-member drop is a book statement; one that needs a holder is a holding (2026-10-28 r1214 bm-b)

E179 published the fixed ladder leg per holding and the shares; the open question was whether
the era LEVELS are those holdings in disguise. Leave-one-member-out (re-weight the survivors to
stay fully invested, re-annualise on the surviving months) says they are not: gross signs hold
+1018.2 / +1004.8 / -923.5 / +1088.6 bp across pre2017..2024_now and no single drop flips any of
them, while pre2017 is the only era whose biggest member (sh510500, 61.89%) is over half.
How to apply: a single-name test on a BASKET must re-weight, not subtract -- subtracting the
dropped contribution compares an un-invested remainder against a fully invested book and
understates every drop by exactly the dropped weight (here 800 vs 1200 bp on a three-name
fixture, i.e. -33%, a number that reads as a result and is an artefact). Two siblings: a month
whose only member is the dropped name is excluded from BOTH sides and published as
unholdable rather than read as a zero return, and the re-weighted level must reproduce the
published one when nothing is dropped (join 0.000216 bp here) or the sweep is a second engine
pass in disguise. Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERLEVELS-20261027.json,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1214-e181-e182-ladder-lomo-wide-gross.md.

## E182 A net delta on a rotating book is a thin residual; publish gross and name the opposing carrier (2026-10-28 r1214 bm-b)

E176 published the wide book repair delta as one net number. Per holding, 2024_now reads
+49.6 bp net against 113.1 bp of gross absolute movement (net/gross 0.4387, 45 names moved),
and the largest single carrier is NEGATIVE: sh510580 -14.5 bp (-29.3% of the net). So the
headline is neither a single-name story nor a cancellation to zero -- it is a thin sum of
offsetting repairs, which is what +49.6bp cannot say on its own.

How to apply: on any book that re-picks (so a name can enter/leave mid-window) publish net,
gross abs, net/gross, a classification word (flat_per_holding / cancelling / moving) and the
list of names whose REMOVAL flips the net sign. Three readings here: flat is not cancelling
(a zero net with zero gross is stillness and says nothing about offsetting moves), an empty
flipper list is a positive finding (the net is distributed, not carried), and net/gross near
1 is a one-name story even when no flipper exists. Evidence:
results/e43_tilt_4member/WIDESIDEGROSS-20261027.json,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1214-e181-e182-ladder-lomo-wide-gross.md.

## E180 A field name is not an arithmetic: audit which published levels are sums and which are books (2026-10-28 r1215 bm-b)

Three thousand cells of "level" sit in the tilt family and nothing said which arithmetic produced
each one. Classified by the producer's code path (the def whose body carries the face's study
literal, then the tokens it reaches through the module's own call graph) rather than by the field
name, the 25 faces split as: 979 ANCHORED book levels, 50 DERIVED sums of terms, 5 sums of levels,
2041 published offset cells, 0 of them leaky (every face that quotes a sum also publishes an
offset beside it).

The finding is not a leak, it is a COLLISION. `decomposition_engine_bp` is published twice: once by
the daily-sum producer (E177, a sum of daily w*r terms) and once by a monthly producer (E176, a sum
of monthly per-name contributions). On the same (era, arm) the two answers differ by 2.92..3.63bp
across all 10 comparable cells -- and a near-constant gap across four eras and two arms is a
DEFINITIONAL wedge, not a compounding artifact (compounding would scale with each era's
volatility). A reader quoting "the decomposition level" gets one of two objects and the label does
not say which.

How to apply: (a) when two legs publish the same quantity, the audit question is not "do they
agree" but "are they the same object" -- classify by producer code path, never by column name
(generic names make column matching a false-positive machine, r1176); (b) a near-constant
cross-face gap earns its own label -- publish the per-cell diffs, their spread, and the word
`definitional_wedge` when spread <= 1bp, because the magnitude of a gap without its sign structure
is unreadable; (c) an offset is book-specific (E177: engine 28.41 / wide -29.33 / ladder -57.74bp
on pre2017), so a face's own offset may only be differenced against the E177 column of the SAME
construct -- everything else is inventoried as `not_the_same_construct`, never differenced; (d) a
leg that publishes into a directory it scans must exclude its own study from the sweep or it
audits itself (this window: 25->26 faces / 1034->3274 cells by self-inclusion).

Evidence: results/e79/DECOMLEVELQUOTES-2026-10-27.json, scripts/e97_routing.py mode levelquotes
(selftest 14->40 legs), research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1215-e180-level-quote-audit.md.

## E183 A survival sweep publishes a verdict about ONE annualisation unless it is run on both (2026-10-28 r1216 bm-b)

E181 said every era's ladder LEVEL survives every single-member drop. It was measured on the
arithmetic reading only (mean monthly return x 12). Re-running the identical drop set on the
geometric annualisation of the same monthly returns: the LEVEL verdict holds on both arithmetics
(no era flips either way) but the offset that separates the two readings is 69.3-318.4 bp per era
and up to 425.6 bp on one dropped book -- and 4 of the 22 drops reverse the sign of their OWN
effect (2024_now without sh510050: +73.2 bp on the sum, -34.0 bp compounded). So "dropping this
holding helps" was an arithmetic statement, even though "the level keeps its sign" was not.

How to apply: (a) before quoting a robustness verdict, ask which arithmetic its level was built
from; if the other reading is computable from the same months, run it and publish both, because
the cost of the second reading is one pass and the cost of the wrong one is a claim that reverses
on a definition; (b) an offset whose sign is guaranteed by a theorem (AM >= GM makes every
compounding offset non-positive here) has no sign to report -- publish the magnitude and say the
sign is arithmetic, or a reader will read it as a finding; (c) the LEVEL and the EFFECT of a drop
are two readings: the level can survive while the effect reverses, so a "survives" table without
the per-drop effect column hides the arithmetic dependence; (d) a drop sitting inside the dead
band on either side has no effect to reverse -- flag only genuine sign reversals, or the split
degenerates into noise. Evidence: results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERLOMOCOMPOUND-20261027.json,
research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1216-e183-lomo-compounded.md.

## E184 Four published numbers, one book: reconcile the STACK before the rule (2026-10-28 r1320 bm-c)

`scripts/etf_book_cagr_reconcile.py` -> `results/etf_book_cagr_reconcile.json`,
`research/ETF_BOOK_CAGR_RECONCILE.md`. The ETF book had been published at 9.62% (card
config: 12 names, 8% vol target, 10bp), 7.80% (5 names, beta 0.20, 8bp, no size), 7.38%
(same at NAV 1e6) and 7.28% (1%-ADV ceiling), and r1319 put the 9.62 end next to the 7.28
end and called the 2.34pp between them an unattributed "run diff". A 2x3x2 grid (width x
risk rule x cost) run on ONE stack, plus the same grid on the other face's stack, closed
it with nothing left over:

1. **A "run diff" between two published faces is usually a STACK difference, and a stack
   difference is a risk statement.** Holding the knobs fixed (5 names, flat, 10bp), the
   production panel (class filter: equity/commodity/convertible only, 1,499 symbols)
   returns 15.80%/yr at 16.4% vol while the capacity face's raw panel returns 7.29% at
   10.3% vol -- 8.5pp apart, of which the engine accounts for 0.2-1.5pp. So the filter
   buys return by forcing the book out of bonds/money funds: quote it as a risk choice
   (Sharpe 1.02 vs 0.76), never as alpha, and never compare levels across the two faces.
2. **The card headline is not the rule the order sheet trades.** On the production stack
   the card is 9.62% (vol 10.17%, DD -11.8%) and the executable ticket (5 names, beta
   0.20, 8bp) is 13.35% (vol 13.46%, DD -16.0%), +3.73pp: width +2.04, rule +1.29, cost
   +0.40, **interaction -1.25pp**. Both path orders sum to exactly 3.73 and both endpoints
   agree on the interaction, so the knobs must be quoted together or not at all.
3. **Transfer a size effect as a DELTA, never as a level.** The capacity ladder's absolute
   cagrs belong to its own universe; only its impact delta (no-size minus at-size) is
   portable to the traded stack, and it must be priced at the DEPLOYED amount, not the
   account size -- otherwise an account far above the ceiling pays the ladder's 50M
   impact instead of the ceiling's.

Result downstream: the book is the best vehicle at CNY 300k/1M/1.5M (+9,780 / +32,600 /
+40,635 per year) and the four-ETF basket takes over from 3M -- the previous face had the
basket winning all six tiers because it priced the book at the ceiling's 7.28%.

How to apply: any face that compares two artefacts from different scripts must (a) run the
same knobs on both stacks to size the stack term, (b) reproduce each published endpoint
from one cell of one grid before decomposing anything, and (c) publish path sums AND both
endpoints' marginals, so an interaction cannot be silently charged to the last knob stepped.

## E184 A census gate must gate the surface the reader opens -- and a comment naming a chain leg is not a chain leg (2026-10-28 r1322 bm-c)

`scripts/reader_text_audit.py` -> `results/reader_text_audit.json`, chain leg
`reader_text_audit`. r1320 fixed one published caveat that read "13.5%% vol" with a gate on
that single face's output and recorded three more faces of the same class "for the next
sweep". The sweep was written by a window that was silence-killed before it committed, so
this round read the tree instead of a digest -- and the sweep had two defects of its own:

1. **The gate's only failing input would have been a fixture.** `scan_md` labelled every
   `.md` "doc-prose" and the verdict read only the `live-face` bucket, so the page the CEO
   actually opens (`docs/live_usage/LIVE-*.md`) could carry the exact doubled-percent defect
   and the census would still print PASS. Ask which bucket a real reader hits, and gate that
   one; today docs prose is 0 hits, and 300 previously mislabelled hits correctly re-bucket
   to frozen / closed-round evidence.
