2. **A claim in a comment is not wiring.** The smoke leg's comment said "the chain leg
   re-derives the census every round"; `Tools/s6_chain_bmc.py` had no such leg (the r1266
   class: a verdict that no next step reads). Grep the runner for the leg name before
   writing the sentence.

Cost disclosure: the sweep walks 12,943 files in ~110s, which is the price of gating prose
and not only JSON fields -- disclosed here so a later round does not "optimise" the scan
roots back down to `results/`.

How to apply: when a defect class is swept, (a) name the surfaces a reader opens and make
those the gate, (b) publish the non-gated buckets with counts so the class stays visible
instead of silenced, (c) prove the leg exists in the runner, not in a comment.

## E185 A thin net is a product of two mechanisms, and only one of them is about the signal (2026-10-28 r1217 bm-b)

`run_tilt3widesplitmonth()` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e43_tilt_4member/WIDEMONTHCANCEL-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3widesplitmonth`, selftest 617->628. E182 published the wide book's repair as one net beside one gross (2024_now +49.6bp over 113.1bp, ratio 0.4387) and the ratio could not say where the movement went. Keeping the delta per decision month splits the loss exactly:

    |sum_m net_m| / sum_m gross_m = (sum_m |net_m| / sum_m gross_m) x (|sum_m net_m| / sum_m |net_m|)

The first factor is motion surviving WITHIN-month cancellation (two holdings repaired opposite ways at one rebalance = a membership/weight artefact), the second surviving ACROSS-month sign disagreement (the era averages away a time-varying signal). Measured: 2024_now keeps 0.872 within months but only 0.472 across them, at most 47.7bp removed inside months vs 194.7bp between them (4.1x) -- so the repair is time-varying and era-averaged, and the residual must never be quoted as an edge or a level.

Two traps this closes, both worth carrying: (a) **the two removed amounts must add to `gross - |net|`** or the split is a second story rather than a decomposition -- pin it with a selftest leg; (b) **name-level gross is not the width of the movement** -- E182 netted each holding over the whole era first, so a name repairing +X then -X contributes 0 to its gross while the path carried the motion twice (path/name gross 1.037/1.067/1.060 on 2021_2023/2024_now/full; net/gross 0.4387 on the name basis vs 0.4114 on the path basis), and the two nets still agree to 0.0004bp because it is one computation read two ways.

How to apply: when a thin aggregate is published, ask which axis it was netted on (names) and whether the decision axis (months) was netted first; publish both gross bases with their ratio, and classify the cancellation in words so the reader knows whether the follow-up is a membership question or a timing question.

## E186 A gross-vs-net label, not a second arithmetic: the two-producer wedge is the turnover fee (2026-10-28 r1218 bm-b)

`run_tilt3levelwedge()` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e79/LEVELWEDGE-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3levelwedge`, selftest 628->636. E180 found one field name (`decomposition_engine_bp`) published from two producers differing by 2.92..3.63bp with a 0.70bp spread and could not attribute the gap. Three candidates are tested per era and per arm: an additive constant (residual 0.20..0.35bp), a scale factor (up to 3.4bp), and a dropped term (0.0000bp on 10/10 cells). The dropped term is the turnover fee, measured by re-running both books with `cost_mult=0.0` on the same panel (`_monthly_core(cost_mult=...)` is the new seam) because `simulate()`'s sink records only `growth = sum w*r` while the curve is net of the fee booked at each rebalance.

    dec_from_contrib  = sum_m sum_s w*r                 (GROSS of the fee)
    dec_from_daily    = sum_m sum_d (r_wide - r_lad)    (NET of the fee)
    gap = dec_from_contrib - dec_from_daily = fee_wide - fee_lad = 2.92..3.62 bp/yr

Fee term per era 2.9207 / 3.5300 / 3.6208 / 3.3984 / 3.3570bp; implied extra turnover ~2.17%/month at 13.041bp a side. Both published faces are reproduced exactly (max |diff| 0.0bp, 10 cells each), so the gap is the faces' own number.

Two transferable rules: (a) **a near-constant offset across eras of very different volatility is a per-period charge, not compounding or scale** -- compounding grows with the era, a fee is paid every period; (b) **the decisive instrument for a definitional question can be a parameter seam, not a cleverer decomposition** -- `cost_mult` turned the question into two runs of one panel, which is why the answer is a number rather than a reading of the code. Consequence is a labelling duty: sink-based is gross, curve-based is net, and quoting either as "the decomposition" without the word is the category error.

## E186-DW A reversible drop effect has no bad month: the era sign is decided by scaling, not by any one month (2026-10-28 r1219 bm-b)

`run_tilt3ladderdropwhere()` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERDROPWHERE-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3ladderdropwhere`, selftest 636->644. (Card disambiguator `-DW`: the numeric card E186 was taken by the r1218 fee-label study the same day; this card answers pool row E186-DROP-EFFECT-REVERSES-WHERE.) E183 published four drops whose effect flips sign between the summed level and the compounded one while every era LEVEL keeps its sign. Each flagged pair is now split per decision month:

    delta_sum  = dropped_month_ret - full_month_ret          (what the arithmetic level averages)
    delta_link = (1+dropped_month_ret)/(1+full_month_ret)-1  (what the compounded level compounds)
    gap_m      = delta_sum - delta_link                      (definitional, second order)

Era-level effects rebuilt from these month vectors reproduce E183 to 0.000634bp. Measured carrier concentration (share of total |gap| held by the largest month, floor 0.50, effective months 1/HHI): pre2017 sh510500 = single_month, 2014-12 holds 0.591 (eff 2.62); pre2017 sh510300 0.358 (5.45); 2024_now sh510050 0.237 (8.97) carrier 2026-07; full sh510050 0.118 (25.36) carrier 2014-12.

The finding that kills the obvious follow-up: **zero months in any of the four pairs disagree in sign between the two readings**. So "the effect reverses" is never one bad month flipping -- every month moves the same way under both readings and the era-level sign comes from how the two columns scale the same months. A reversal is an accumulated scaling statement, i.e. a property of the arithmetic, unless a carrier month is doing the work (one case here, and it is a deep-history month).

Two transferable rules: (a) **before hunting a culprit month, count the months whose two readings disagree in sign** -- zero means there is no culprit and the search is misdirected; (b) **a carrier-share floor plus 1/HHI turns "is it one month or many" into a number**, which is the difference between a verdict a reader can act on and a verdict nobody can. Also: annualising the RATIO of the two growth multiples is not the same object as differencing two annualised levels (2.43bp apart here); the first is published as `effect_comp_ratio_bp`, the second as `effect_comp_bp`, and a selftest leg pins that they cannot be swapped.
instead of silenced, (c) prove the leg exists in the runner, not in a comment.



## E187 A razor-thin cut line does not mean the trades are marginal -- measure the executed pairs, not the boundary (2026-10-28 r1324 bm-c)

The book is turnover-bound (r1260/r1261) and its score is an average of percentile ranks, so
"it trades on noise" is a one-line narrative. Measured on 83 decisions (2020-01 -> 2026-10-27,
12 names / 20 bars): the 12th-vs-13th gap is a median 0.018 sd of that bar's own
cross-section, and 86.8% of decisions sit under 0.10 sd -- the boundary really is arbitrary.
But of the 590 trades actually executed, the median (leaver, entrant) gap is 0.974 sd and only
2.9% are under 0.10 sd. **A name leaves the top 12 because it collapsed, not because it lost a
coin flip.** The boundary census and the trade census answer different questions and the second
one is the one that explains turnover; quoting the first alone manufactures a defect.

Second reusable piece: incumbent-protection buffers are a cost knob, not an edge. k in
{0.05..0.50} sd moves turnover 0.934 -> 0.702 monotonically while CAGR stays within +0.07pp
down to +0.04pp -- and the k=0.05 arm is the only one that improves turnover, CAGR, Sharpe and
maxDD together, so "more buffer is better" is false at k>=0.35 where depth starts to cost. Check
the cost arithmetic against the measured delta (0.092/decision x ~12.3 decisions/yr x 10bp =
0.11pp/yr vs +0.03pp measured): same order means the arm is pricing cost, not skill.

Third: when a shared engine gains an optional hook, pin two things separately -- (a) the default
path is bit-identical (on a TIE-FREE fixture, because `nlargest` breaks exact ties by index order
while an incumbent-protection rule keeps the incumbent), and (b) a hook returning the wrong shape
raises instead of silently falling back. And gate the published face with an engine-free
`--check` (control arm reproduces the published cell / monotone turnover / census arithmetic /
face not older than the card it reconciles) with one selftest leg per way it can lie -- 10 legs,
9 of them red-cases.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_hysteresis.py (selftest 32/32) + results/etf_book_hysteresis.json +
research/ETF_BOOK_HYSTERESIS.md + Tools/s6_chain_bmc.py leg + smoke leg; engine change =
scripts/etf_robust_pf.py select_hook (default None bit-identical).
## E189 A carrier month is the whole verdict: removing the one month E186 named collapses all four era-level reversals (2026-10-28 r1220 bm-b)

`run_tilt3ladderdropcross()` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERDROPCROSS-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3ladderdropcross`, selftest 644->651. E186 published four (era, drop) pairs whose effect reverses sign between the summed and the compounded reading and classified them single_month/dispersion by carrier share. This walks the two cumulative paths month by month and re-runs each era with E186's own declared carrier month removed:

    cum_sum_t  = sum_{m<=t} delta_sum_m              (the additive path)
    cum_link_t = prod_{m<=t}(1+delta_link_m) - 1     (the compounded path)
    diff_t     = cum_sum_t - cum_link_t              (what has to cross for an era reversal)

Result: **4/4 reversals disappear once that one month is gone** -- 2024_now sh510050 (carrier 2026-07) arith 73.16 -> 204.25bp / comp -34.01 -> 141.93bp; full sh510050 (2014-12) 0.07 -> 44.89 / -38.20 -> 1.63; pre2017 sh510300 (2016-01) -32.57 -> -103.85 / 6.18 -> -95.56; pre2017 sh510500 (2014-12) 36.58 -> -197.98 / -9.66 -> -203.73. The crossing month of the two paths is never the carrier (0/4), and 2024_now carries an era reversal with no path crossing at all (+296.5bp end gap, 0 crossings) because E186's `comp` is a difference of two annualised levels while `cum_link` compounds ratio steps -- published as `reversals_without_path_crossing` rather than folded into the crossing count. Join to E186 max |diff| 8.8e-05bp.

Three transferable rules: (a) **a published reversal must be re-run without the month the same study named as its carrier** -- if it vanishes, the era verdict was a one-month artefact and may not be quoted as a property of the arithmetic; E186's dispersion labels (carrier share 0.118-0.358, 5.45-25.36 effective months) did not predict fragility, so the label is not a substitute for the removal test. (b) **a crossing month and a carrier month are different objects** -- "when do the two paths pass each other" answers a different question than "which month carries the gap", and reporting one as the other would invent a date for a verdict that has none. (c) **when two published readings disagree in sign but their step paths never cross, the disagreement is in the annualisation, not in the data** -- that has to be its own published bucket, otherwise the crossing count silently implies a turning point that does not exist.

**Correction (r1325, measured):** the k=0.05 arm above does NOT survive the path test, so read the
second piece as "the buffer is a cost knob, full stop". Re-priced on 5 books x 4 decision phases
(`results/etf_book_buffer_adoption.json`, 60 engine runs): turnover falls in 100% of the 20
comparison cells, but return falls in 14 of them (median -0.140pp, worst -0.44pp) and risk-adjusted
return falls in 14 of 20. The +0.07pp that made k=0.05 look like the one arm improving everything
is the production book started on 2020-01-02; the same book started a week later prints -0.24pp and
a week after that -0.14pp. A better arm on one decision calendar is one decision sequence.

## E188 Path-test an arm before adopting it -- one cell is one sequence, and a decision count is not a signature (2026-10-28 r1325 bm-c)

An arm that is measured once, on one decision calendar, cannot be separated from a lucky sequence of
decisions -- and the separation decides whether the production config changes. The test is cheap
relative to the mistake: re-price the same arm on several books AND several decision-grid phases.

Reading (k=0.05 incumbent buffer, 5 books x 4 phases = 20 comparison cells, 60 engine runs):

* turnover down in **100%** of cells (median -0.030/decision) -- the turnover claim is real;
* return down in **70%** of cells (median **-0.140pp**, worst -0.44pp), risk-adjusted down in 70%
  -- so the arm is NOT an edge, and at k=0.20 the direction is a coin flip (10 up / 10 down,
  exact sign test p=1.0). Verdict under the frozen rule: **REJECT** (no config change). (Re-derived one bar later at cutoff 2026-10-28: median **-0.140pp**, the same 100% turnover result and the same verdict -- a one-bar shift, not a different reading.)
* phase noise is the ruler: the production book's own CAGR spans **9.12%..10.93%** across four
  phases of one 20-bar cycle (IQR 0.53pp) and the 40-bar cadence spans **3.69%..10.20%** (IQR
  2.99pp). A headline quoted from one phase is a point estimate pretending to be a level -- quote
  the band.

Three mechanisms worth reusing:

1. **Freeze the decision rule above the code.** Thresholds are module constants written before the
   run and echoed into the payload; the gate refuses a face whose echoed criteria differ, so a
   later "small adjustment" cannot masquerade as the same rule. The gate also RECOMPUTES the
   verdict from the summary and compares it to the published label -- a hand-written verdict is a
   claim, not a result.
2. **A decision count is not evidence that the calendar moved.** Four phases inside a 20-bar cycle
   produced counts 83/83/82/82; a count-based activity check declared the test void while the
   calendars were genuinely different. The signature is the decision DATES (first/last), and the
   second decision count is disclosed, not required to differ. (r1261 family, one layer down.)
3. **A caveat that only warns leaves the reader free to believe the arm.** r1324 shipped "a single
   better arm can be one decision sequence" as a caveat; the arm still read as an improvement. The
   hysteresis face now prints the measurement and its verdict inline, so the two faces cannot
   disagree with the reader's takeaway.

Attached defect, caught by a report leg rather than by reading: a missing comma between two
adjacent string literals concatenated the table header with an empty string, and the following
`lines.pop()` then deleted the header -- the table shipped with a separator row and no columns.
Every reader-facing renderer now has a leg asserting its header row exists.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_buffer_adoption.py (selftest 33/33) + results/etf_book_buffer_adoption.json
(60 cells) + research/ETF_BOOK_BUFFER_ADOPTION.md + Tools/s6_chain_bmc.py leg + smoke leg;
cross-pointer emitted by scripts/etf_book_hysteresis.py.
## E191 A discount nobody measured is a guess: the wide book's monthly repair motion is independent of its own churn, and an empty repair set is not a flat net (2026-10-28 r1221 bm-b)
`run_tilt3widechurntie()` + `_wide_month_membership` / `_membership_turnover` / `_wide_turnover_rows` / `_pearson` / `_churn_tie_verdict` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e43_tilt_4member/WIDECHURNTIE-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3widechurntie`, selftest 651 -> 664 (13 new legs), panel_screen 39/39 unchanged.
    turnover_m = (entered + left) / mean(|members_{m-1}|, |members_m|)
    claim under test: r(net_m, turnover_m) >= 0.5 -> quote the spread as a
    membership statement, discounted by churn
Result: **the discount does not apply in any measured era** -- r = +0.048 (2021_2023), +0.159 (2024_now), -0.013 (full); repairing-months only +0.192/+0.135/+0.122; absolute-motion r mildly NEGATIVE (-0.107/-0.051/-0.122). The high-churn half carries LESS motion (2024_now 7.27 vs 10.26 mean |net| bp, 41.5% of the era's absolute motion; 2021_2023 2.25 vs 4.86, 31.6%), the months that fight the era total sit in the COLD half (against-month mean turnover 0.0204 vs 0.0255 on 2024_now; against-in-hot-half 57.1/20.0/33.3% = chance), and the largest months are quiet months (2024-09 turnover 0.0196 with -55.3bp against an era max of 0.0388). E184's between-month reversal is the same names moving, not different names being held.
Three transferable rules: (a) **a correction factor has to be measured before it is applied** -- "the spread tracks churn, so discount it" is a plausible story that one correlation refutes; applied by default it would have removed motion for no reason, so the honest output is the correlation itself, published beside the number it would have modified. (b) **an empty set is not a zero** -- pre2017 and 2017_2020 have zero months in which the repair touched the wide book, and reporting them as a flat net claims a measurement nobody took; give them their own bucket (`no_repair_in_era`) and let "exactly flat" point back at an empty repair set. (c) **churn must be normalised by size and counted from a presence recorder** -- raw entry counts track the book's growth (0 -> 56 -> 159 -> 619 -> 1202 names here), and membership must come from a sink written per name-day while the name is held, with the cash residual excluded: swallowing the cash leg makes a name count read complete while being short by one pseudo-member. Side reading worth keeping: this book's churn FELL as it widened (0.057 -> 0.035 -> 0.050 -> 0.025 per month).
## E188 A label on a different file is not a label: the two fee stances of `decomposition_engine_bp` are one number plus the rebalance fee, and only the audit face says which (2026-10-28 r1222 bm-b)
`fee_ladder` / `gross_ladder` / `net_ladder` / `published_ladder` / `stance_of` / `wedge_rows` / `label_sweep` / `label_duty` in `scripts/e97_routing.py` -> `results/e79/LABELDUTY-2026-10-27.json`, mode `labelduty`, selftest 40 -> 48 (8 new legs).
    gross - net == rebalance_cost_term            (E185 joins, tolerance 0.01bp)
    offset_net - offset_gross == the fee          (E176 stores reading-published,
                                                   E177 stores published-reading)
    inside_the_wedge: |offset| <= that cell's own fee
Result: **10/10 cells are one number plus the fee** (worst residual 0.0050bp), the label moves the level by **1.129%..12.187%** of itself, and **10/10 published offsets** are the same construct with the fee between them. **0/10 cells flip sign** with the stance -- which is why this survived review: the stance changes sizes, never directions. **4 cells quote an offset narrower than their own fee** (2021_2023 -1.14 net / -4.77 gross against a 3.62bp fee; full 6.13 / 2.78 against 3.36bp), so a verdict quoting "about 2bp" is quoting a number the label can double. Family sweep (30 faces): three faces publish the cell and **none of them declares a stance**; the only face that names both stances is the E185 audit itself.
Four transferable rules: (a) **a label duty is a sweep, not a fix** -- once one gross-publishing face is found, every sibling consuming the same cell is a candidate and the join is cheap because the two stances differ by a published term. (b) **classify by publishes-vs-declares in four states** (labelled / unlabelled / mixed / mislabeled) using a value match against the stance ladders plus a token scan of the face's own text, and exclude the slug: a face named `..._gross_...` is an identifier, not a claim. (c) **publish the margin in fees, not in bp** -- "offset 2.10bp, fee 3.58bp" is actionable; the bare number sits beside another legitimate value 170% away. (d) **a sign test is not the safety test** -- zero flips looks like immunity and is precisely the mechanism that hides the error; check magnitudes against the fee, not directions.
## E190 A label on a different file is not a label (renumbered from E188 -- bm-c r1325 published E188 in the same window, theirs kept verbatim): the two fee stances of `decomposition_engine_bp` are one number plus the rebalance fee, and only the audit face says which (2026-10-28 r1222 bm-b)
## E190-CRV A fragility claim is not a fragility measurement: publish the k at which the pair agrees, under four trim rules, and let the rule-sensitivity count veto the label (2026-10-28 r1223 bm-b)
`_drop_trim_order` / `_drop_trim_effect` / `_drop_fragility_curve` / `run_tilt3ladderdropcurve` in `scripts/e39_panel_census.py` -> `results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERDROPCURVE-20261027.json`, mode `tilt3ladderdropcurve`, selftest 664 -> 670 (6 new legs).
    k_agree(rule) = min k in 1..min(8, months-2) s.t. signs agree after removing the k worst months
    class = two_month_artefact (k<=2) | fragile_window (3..k_max) | era_property (never) | no_era_reversal
Result: **all four flagged reversals die at k=1** under both magnitude rules (|gap_bp| and |delta_sum|) -- the era verdicts of the E183-E186 family are one-month events -- **but three of the four pairs are class-sensitive**: trimming the era's *edges* instead needs 4 months (`full`), 8 (`pre2017`/sh510300), and never clears within k<=8 on `pre2017`/sh510500. So "the verdict is fragile" is true, and "the verdict is a one-month artefact" is true only under a rule that ranks by month magnitude. Join with E186's published month rows: 8.8e-05 bp.
Four transferable rules: (a) **replace the fragility adjective with a curve** -- "fragile" is a feeling until you publish the k at which the two readings agree, and the k is the number a reader can act on. (b) **a trim rule is a question, not an ordering** -- ranking by |gap| removes the months that move the level, ranking by |delta_sum| removes the months that move the additive path, and first/last ask whether the verdict is an edge-of-window effect; publish all four and count how many pairs change class (`classification_is_rule_sensitive`), because a label that survives only under one rule is a label about the method. (c) **cap k at months-2** -- a short era trimmed to one observation reads as agreement, so the cap has to be published (`k_limit`, "never" = not cleared within the cap) rather than silently shrinking the sweep. (d) **a trim can manufacture the disagreement it is supposed to test** -- record `k_reversal_created` on pairs that already agree; otherwise the "first k that agrees" is answering a question nobody asked.
## E195 Choose the trim rule before you see the answer (2026-10-28, bm-b r1224)
Evidence: `results/e43_tilt_4member/LADDERDROPRULE-20261027.json` · leg
`scripts/e39_panel_census.py tilt3ladderdroprules` · digest
`research/digests/DIGEST-20261028-r1224-e195-drop-rule.md` · selftest 670->678.
E190 ended with a class that flipped between trim rules, i.e. the label was being
chosen by the removal method. E195 fixes the rule *before* the removal: four
rules that read **only the book's own monthly return** (median+3xMAD outlier,
>2x era median, top-decile magnitude, chronological first+last month) and never
`delta_sum`/`delta_link`/`gap_bp`, which are the drop's own measurement. Result:
3 of 4 flagged ladder-drop pairs die under *every* pre-registerable rule (quotable
as artefacts), and the fourth -- full-era sh510050 -- survives only edge trimming
because its arithmetic level is **+0.07bp** against a -38bp compounded gap: a
near-zero level whose sign is a rounding-scale fact, not a direction.
Four transferable rules: (a) **a removal set is only evidence if it could be
written down in advance** -- a rule that ranks months on the quantity under
question has already read the answer, so publish it as a dependent contrast
(`reads_gap_columns: true`) and never count it as the test. (b) **prove the
blindness mechanically, not by intent** -- a selftest leg feeds the same book
column with the gap/delta columns inflated 1000x and asserts identical
selections; "I only used full_ret" is a claim until a leg pins it. (c) **a
verdict that survives some rules and dies under others is still a knob
statement** -- publish `rules_that_kill_the_reversal` and
`rules_under_which_it_survives` as two sets on every pair, so the reader cannot
pick the half that suits them. (d) **check the level's magnitude against the gap
it is being told apart from** -- "+0.07 vs -38.20" is not a sign disagreement
worth naming; when |level| is two orders below |gap|, the honest sentence is
"the readings differ in magnitude, not in direction".

## E192 Churn-independence is not carrier persistence: measure WHO repaired, and never score a sparsity hole as recycle (2026-10-28 r1225 bm-b)

E191 (the E187 leg) measured that the wide book's surviving monthly motion is
independent of its own membership turnover (|r|<=0.16) and read that as "the same
names moving". That is a name-level claim built out of a book-level measurement: a
book can hold an identical membership two months running and still be repaired by a
different name each month. Measuring the carriers directly settles it -- 94-97% of
each month's repair motion is carried by names that already repaired the month
before, the persistent names carry 80-89% of the era's gross, and the months that
fight the era total are carried by those same names at 100.0% (2024_now, vs 97.2%
elsewhere), not by newcomers.

Three transferable rules: (a) **match the measurement's unit to the claim's unit** --
"who was held" and "who did the repairing" are different sets and only the second
one backs a sentence about the same names; (b) **a month where nothing happened is
not a month where something went the other way** -- an empty repair set has no
carrier, so skip the pair and count it (`pairs_unmeasured`) instead of scoring 0.0,
which would manufacture a "recycle" verdict out of sparsity alone (134 of 161
full-era pairs are unmeasured; the sparsity is itself a finding); (c) **when you
re-derive a sibling face, compare at the same cutoff** -- the head-to-head gap here
is 0.242bp purely because the sibling was built one panel month earlier, so the leg
recomputes the sibling's own formula in-process (residual 0.000399bp = double
rounding) and publishes `cutoff_match=false` rather than quoting the number as a
reproduction.


## E199 A persistent carrier is not a flipping carrier: weight the events before you name a mechanism, and check whether your "new" ratio is a published one (2026-10-28 r1226 bm-b)

E192 shipped a stable *set* of carriers (94-97% carry share). The reading
attached to it -- "the between-month reversal is one set of names moving" -- can
be three different mechanisms, and they imply opposite follow-ups. Keeping the
SIGN of each name's monthly repair settles it: of the era's 341.6bp of name-month
gross motion only 8.13bp (2.4%) is destroyed by a name changing direction; 94% of
the motion survives inside a name (`within_name_keep` 0.9377); and the mass that
does point the other way is owned by three names that repair DOWN month after
month with keep ratio 1.000 (sh510580 net -41.11bp, 16 of 24 months down).

Three transferable rules: (a) **a count of flips is not a measurement of flipping
mass** -- 45.7% of adjacent months change sign while the weighted rate is 35.3%
and the destroyed motion is 2.4% of gross, so publish both or the reading is an
artefact of counting pennies; (b) **an exact match with a published ratio means
the same construct, not a confirmation** -- `across_name_keep` equals E182's
`net_over_gross` to 0.0000 on all three eras because E182 netted each holding
before dividing, i.e. its headline was already the name-level disagreement ratio;
(c) **`keep_ratio` separates the two name-level stories** -- a name with 16
down-months and net -41bp on 41bp of gross is a one-way carrier, not an
alternator, and only the ratio says so.


## E200 Classify a published delta by how it was BUILT, not by what it prints: bind by name, verify against a construct, and refuse to score a zero (2026-10-28 r1227 bm-b)

E193 asked which build path produced each published delta (two published levels,
two readings of one stance, or two stances). The sweep of 152 era-level bp deltas
in 14 faces finds **zero cross-stance deltas** - the construct that would carry
the whole 2.9-3.6bp fee is published nowhere as a delta - so the feared defect
class does not exist here. Two others do: every ladder-rebuilt delta (30 of them)
reproduces only as the **negated** construct (the faces print `screened - raw`
where the rule reads `raw - screened`, uniform and stated nowhere), and
`net_delta_bp` carries the family's "net of the fee" vocabulary while being a
fee-free book-level difference (30.204 / 49.598 / 17.121 bp over three eras).

Four transferable rules: (a) **bind by name, verify by construct, publish both** -
a name-bound cell whose number matches nothing is a finding ("the label names one
object, the number is another"), and a number-matched cell whose family the label
does not name must be published as a reading, not a declaration; (b) **a zero
delta proves nothing** - 34 of 152 cells are 0.0 and match any zero construct in
either sign, so counting them as reproductions manufactures agreement (the E162
"two flat curves because neither side ran" failure in delta clothing); (c) **a sum
of absolute per-name deltas is not a difference of two levels** - its fee exposure
scales with how many names moved, so no era-level rebuild exists and the honest
verdict is `not_checkable`, not `contradicted`; (d) **the unit token may sit
mid-name** (`engine_bp_delta` vs `delta_bp`) - a suffix-based unit filter silently
drops half the family (8 of 17 names here) and still looks like a clean sweep.

## E201 Publish the two numbers a sign is quoted from, and let a declared floor decide which one is a hair (2026-10-29 r1228 bm-b)

E195 left the full-era `sh510050` drop labelled a *reversal* because its additive
level (+0.07bp) has the opposite sign to its compounded gap (-38.20bp).  E196 swept
all **164** cells of the family that print both numbers and asked a different
question: is the printed sign large enough next to the gap it is told apart from to
be quoted at all?  Two floors declared before the sweep (5% of the gap, 1bp of gap)
sort the family into 157 quotable, **6 sign_is_hair**, 1 level_agrees.  The six hairs
carry |level|/|gap| = 0.001946 five times over (level and gap scale together, so the
hair is a property of the pair and not of a step), and the face also prints
`gap_needed_for_quotable_bp`, the gap that would have had to exist for that same
level to carry a direction (1.49bp against the 38.20bp actually present).

Four transferable rules: (a) **a sign printed from two scale-incompatible readings is
a rounding fact, not a direction** - publish the ratio, not just the sign, and let a
pre-declared floor decide; (b) **the floor is symmetric** - one live cell prints
27.99bp against -0.52bp, where the GAP is the hair, so an audit that only asks "is
the level small?" mislabels it as a clean reversal; publish `hair_side` per cell;
(c) **sweep by schema, not by face list** - "every dict carrying both `arith_bp` and
`comp_bp`" cannot miss a face someone adds next month, while an enumerated face list
rots silently; (d) **a derived cutoff must be validated, not just maximised** - a
status face carries prose in `evidence_cutoff` ("panel 2026-10-14 (bars 10-15 due
15:30)") which wins a lexicographic max over every real date and lands in a filename;
match `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` and derive only from contributing faces.

Rider (same round, delivery pit): a partial clone that cannot fetch a single object will **hang a push
forever**, and the hang and the rejection are the same fact - `$env:GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` turns the 5-minute
silence into a 17-second named failure ("could not fetch <oid> from promisor remote"). Try that first; then
`--no-thin` and `-c pack.window=0` to rule out delta bases (both died identically here, so the object was
genuinely absent). When killing a stuck push, kill the whole subtree (parent + `pack-objects` + the fetch it
spawned + `index-pack --promisor`) and only that subtree - other repos' git processes can be hung at the same
time and are not yours.


## E202 Sweep the depth, not just the sign: a verdict that "survives / dies at k" is a single-point artefact until the whole k curve is published (2026-10-29 r1230 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e43_tilt_4member/EDGEDEPTH-20261027.json`, code `tilt3ladderdropedge`
(`scripts/e39_panel_census.py`, selftest 697 -> 706), upstream E186 month rows + E195 rules,
digest `research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e197-edge-depth.md`.

E195 published `chrono_edges` as the one pre-registerable rule under which a full-era pair keeps
reversing, but that rule removes exactly two months on a 162-month era (1.2%) while the magnitude
rules removed 10-48. The natural audit question -- "is the survival just a cheap trim?" -- was swept
(k=2..10, first k/2 + last k/2 months, answer-blind) and came back inverted:

(a) **the kill is not monotone in k** -- 3 of 4 pairs reverse again after they stop: pre2017
sh510300/sh510500 look killed at k=2 and reverse through k=4..10, and the one E195 survivor dies at
k=8 but reverses at k=10. So neither "survives the trim" nor "dies at k=8" is the finding; the first
stopping point is a single-point artefact and only `n_ks_reversing` plus the revival list describe it.
(b) **a revival costs more evidence than a death** -- one k aligning signs kills it; returning needs
every deeper k to align. Publish the revival list rather than a count, so a 4-in-a-row revival cannot
be read like a one-off.
(c) **falsifying a suspicion can invert it** -- the suspicion was "trim too shallow, therefore the
survival is fake". The measurement says the shallow depth is where the sign happens to align and the
survival is real (it holds to 4 months per side, cheaper than the cheapest magnitude rule at 10).
(d) **run the comparison rules from the same rows** -- recomputing book_mad3/gross_x2/top_decile in
the same pass (instead of quoting E195's k) is what keeps the two k values from drifting, and the
E195 classification is carried as a cross-read field that never feeds the verdict.


## E203 Read a removal rule against a removal-count-matched random removal, or the verdict is about the row count and not the rows (2026-10-29 r1231 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e43_tilt_4member/PLACEBO-20261027.json`, code `tilt3ladderdropplacebo`
(`scripts/e39_panel_census.py`, selftest 706 -> 714), upstream E186 month rows + E197 depth face
(cross-read only), digest `research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e203-drop-placebo.md`.

E195/E197 publish a depth curve ("the reversal dies at k=2", "survives at k=10") whose numbers all
come from removing one particular month set -- the era's chronological ends.  Remover, M=200 draws,
null = remove the same NUMBER of months at random from the pair's own pool:

(a) **the null must be count-matched** -- drawing from the pair's whole month pool makes the edge set
one of its own draws, so this is the same experiment with the choice handed to a coin; a weaker
placebo (different size, different pool) measures a different experiment.
(b) **report the same-outcome share per cell, not a p-value** -- 20 cells collapse into three readings
(null does it too / null rarely does it / null is flat both ways); the 50% line is descriptive and
stated in spec, never a gate.
(c) **the direction is asymmetric and must live in the class name** -- 10 of 20 cells were
"edge survives where random removal kills", only 2 were "edge kills where random removal survives".
One summary sentence would let the same table be quoted as "the edge months are critical, therefore
the era is fragile" when the measurement says the calendar ends are the LEAST destructive months.
(d) **a cross-read that returns None everywhere looks like "no comparable record"** -- the first draft
read `rec["edge_k_stop"]` off a face that nests `edge_curve.k_stop`, so every join row silently failed
and the output read as clean.  Publish `record_present` separately from `matches`, emit one join row
per drop, and treat an empty mismatch list as "read it AND equal", never as "nothing to read".
(e) seeding: `zlib.crc32("era|name|k|seed")` per cell -- string hashing is not stable across processes,
so a `hash()`-seeded draw is not reproducible; publish the draw count as randomisation draws, not as a
strategy trial count.



## E204 Before you publish "this face should declare X", ask who reads the number: a defect is only a trap if a second script pulls it by name (2026-10-29 r1232 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/SIGNEXPOSURE-2026-10-27.json`, code `signexposure`
(`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 70 -> 77), upstream E193 + E201, digest
`research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e204-delta-face-readers.md`.

E201 published that all 14 decomposition faces never teach their sign, plus the sentence each
would have to add. Before writing 14 lines, measure whether any of them is watched: for every
face, collect its delta leaf names, find every in-repo script that names one **as a quoted
literal**, subtract the face's own producer, and only then ask whether the text is silent.

(a) **the answer is documentation debt, not exposure** -- 14 of 14 faces are named only by the
script that writes them (`producer_only`), zero have a reader outside their producer, so E201's
lines rank as prose hygiene rather than as a hazard. Publish `trap = needs_line AND
read_outside_the_producer` as its own boolean instead of letting "silent" stand for "dangerous".

(b) **the auditor scores itself as the reader** -- the first fire reported 5 traps whose only
external reader was `scripts/e97_routing.py`: the module's own classification table
(`LEVEL_OVERRIDES = {"engine_ann_diff_bp": "anchored"}`) and its own selftest fixtures spell the
leaf names without ever consuming the number. Exclude the measuring module from its own reader
set, publish the exclusion (`spec.self_excluded`), and pin it with a leg (L74). Any name-scan
study of a codebase must first ask whether the scanner is inside its own candidate set.

(c) **quote-adjacency, not substring** -- `engine_ann_diff_bp` occurs inside
`engine_ann_diff_bp_raw`, so a substring test makes every arm-suffixed reader look like a reader
of the short leaf (the r1292 mid-name unit-token pitfall, one level down).

How to apply: any "this file/field should say X" audit gets a second leg that measures who reads
the file before the fix is prioritised; and any source-tree scan excludes its own writer and
proves the exclusion with a leg.
## E205 A snapshot of "nobody reads this" expires inside the round that wrote it: ratchet the reader map and let the first durable reader fail the build (2026-10-29 r1233 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/SIGNGROWTH-2026-10-27.json` + seeded `results/e79/SIGNGROWTH-BASELINE.json`,
code `signgrowth` (`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 77 -> 87, smoke leg added), upstream E201 +
E204, digest `research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e205-sign-growth-gate.md`.

E204 answered "who reads this silent face today". That answer is a snapshot, and the event it
cannot see is the one that matters: a face that never taught its sign acquiring its **first
durable reader**. The gate diffs two reader maps and refuses the crossing.

(a) **the published "0 readers" was already stale when it shipped** -- the sweep of r1232 said
14/14 `producer_only`; re-running the identical code one round later reported **6 of 14**
`read_outside_the_producer`, and every one of them was read by exactly one file:
`Tools/_r1232bmb_card.py`, the scratch script that round wrote to author its own card. A
measurement that scans a tree which the same round is still writing is a measurement of a tree
that does not exist yet. Fix the reader classes, not the prose: underscore-prefixed helpers are
`read_by_a_round_scratch_script` (new class), durable readers keep
`read_outside_the_producer`, and only the latter can be a trap. After the fix the honest split is
8 producer-only / 6 scratch-only / **0** durable.

(b) **a ratchet, not a threshold** -- publish the current map as an explicit baseline
(`SIGNGROWTH-BASELINE.json`), then fail (rc 3) on exactly two events: a baseline face moving
from a clean class into a durable external read while it still needs a line, and a **new** face
born already read by a durable consumer. Report a vanished face as retired, never as a failure.
Seeding blesses today's map on purpose, so `--seed` is a deliberate act with a published
statement, not a convenience fallback.

(c) **the gate is only real when something runs it** -- a crossing that no scheduled check reads
is a comment. Wire the leg into smoke (124/124) so "the build fails" is a fact about the repo
rather than a promise in a card.

How to apply: for any "nothing consumes this yet" conclusion, publish the reader map as a
versioned baseline and turn the *first* consumer into a blocking event; and when a study scans a
directory the running round also writes into, classify round-local artefacts before believing
the first number.
## E206 A count published under a name the family's classifier reads as a measurement is a fabricated quote; gate the producer half of silence with a leaf-name ratchet (2026-10-29 r1234 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/SIGNLEAF-2026-10-27.json` + seeded `results/e79/SIGNLEAF-BASELINE.json`,
code `signleafgrowth` (`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 87 -> 97), upstream E201 + E204 + E205,
digest `research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e206-leaf-growth-gate.md`.

E205 asked who *reads* a silent face. The other half is who *writes* it: a face that never taught
its sign and then grows a **new** delta number exports that ambiguity to readers that do not exist
yet, which no reader map can see. The gate records each face's delta names (key path, list indices
elided), diffs them against a seeded baseline, and fails on a new name in a face that still needs
its sign line. Baseline 14 faces / **319** delta names, clean on the first live run.

(a) **publish the gate's own blind spot, as a number** -- the test is a token test on the key name,
so it can only see deltas spelled `delta`/`diff`/`difference`. Same 14 faces carry **629** further
numbers with a unit/sign token but no delta token: 140 are arm pairs (one level quoted twice), and
the residue splits 208 anchored / 71 offset / 210 unclassified. Saying "319 names are covered" and
stopping there would overstate coverage by up to 3x; publishing the floor is what lets the next
card be a question instead of a guess.

(b) **the family audits by NAME, so a count under a measurement name becomes a quote** -- this
round produced the defect twice in one edit cycle. `{"offset": 71}` (a class count) was read by
`level_quotes()` as an offset cell worth 71 bp, and the live smoke leg went red because the face
then "quoted" a level with no producer. Restructuring to a list of records fixed it -- and the
next edit reintroduced it as `of_which_arm_paired_levels`, where the token `level` alone was enough
to fabricate one more anchored cell. Fix is structural: records not dict-keys, and count fields
may not carry measurement tokens (`unit_names_in_arm_pairs`, not `..._levels`). Pin it with a leg.

(c) **the live leg is the only reason this was caught** -- the fixture-only tests were green
through both instances; L37 ("on the real disk every quoting face has a producer") failed because
the new product sat in an audited directory. A product that publishes numbers into a directory the
family audits must be run past that audit before it is claimed as done.

How to apply: when you gate one half of a measurement (readers, producers, levels, deltas), publish
the boundary of the test as a count on the face itself; and never let a count share a name with the
quantity the family classifies, because name-based audits will happily quote it.

## E207 The classifier's blind spot also has a *volume*: sweep the whole tree and count the cells a name-based reader would quote that could not be a quote (2026-10-29 r1235 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/QUOTECOUNT-2026-10-28.json`, code `quotecount`
(`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 97 -> 107), upstream E180 + E206,
digest `research/digests/DIGEST-20261029-e207-count-as-quote.md`.

E206 admitted "a delta spelled `engine_gap_bp` is invisible to this gate" as a caveat. E207 turns
that caveat into a measurement, and it is large: across 12,111 faces and 71,245 measurement-named
cells, **37,817 could not be bp quotes at all** -- 30,357 integers whose own name says "a number of
things" (`gapped_kept` 15,682, `gap_sessions` 11,619), 1,728 booleans, 5,732 small integers. Only
3,759 of them sit inside the two directories the level-quote auditor already scans; 34,058 are
outside it. So the auditor is not merely blind at the margin, it is blind across almost the whole
tree -- and `level_class()` would file `gap_sessions` as an **offset of 11,619 bp**.

Three reusable pieces:

(a) **A name test has a false-positive side that is itself quotable.** Splitting the candidates into
`count_word` (the name says count), `small_integer` (no count word, |v| <= ceiling) and
`integral_quote_sized` (5,776 bp is a real reading) is what makes the headline defensible; the
ceiling is a line, so the face publishes a sensitivity table (10/100/1000 -> 4,186/5,732/9,446)
instead of an unqualified total.

(b) **Counts must not be published under classifier vocabulary.** Every count-word hit is a defect
with a one-line fix available at the producer (rename), which is why this is an inventory and not a
rewrite: the audit's job is to put the number where the fix can be argued from.

(c) **A sweep that publishes into the tree it scans must exclude its own study AND its own previous
file**, and `evidence_cutoff` cannot be copied straight into a filename: one page stores a sentence
there and the colon in "15:30" produced a zero-byte face on Windows. Derive the cutoff from strict
ISO dates, and name the pages that misuse the field.

How to apply: before trusting any name-based classifier, count the cells it would classify wrongly
and publish the total with its sensitivity; and when a sweep's total is dominated by one production
family, read the top faces before believing the headline.

## E208 A name-based guard must be pruned by value shape, not by vocabulary alone (2026-10-29 r1236 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/QUOTECLASSFIX-2026-10-28.json`, code `countworddelta`
(`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 107 -> 113), upstream E207,
audit face re-published `results/e79/DECOMPLEVELQUOTES-2026-10-28.json`.

E207 said `level_class()` would file `gap_sessions` as an 11,619 bp offset. E208 fixed the table and
then measured the fix: on the live tree the count guard retires **3,109 cells the old table was
quoting** (3,108 offsets, 1 anchored level) out of 5,537 level cells -> 2,455, across 45 faces.

The reusable piece is what the *first draft of the fix* did wrong. The verified count-word list
(kept/eligible/sessions/n/cells) is safe; but the same list also contained `size`, `days`, `added`,
`rows`, and **every cell those matched was a real non-integral bp reading** --
`spread_size_neutral_ann_bp` 293.7, `published_rebuilt_from_days_bp` 287.2454,
`offset_added_by_compounding_the_difference_bp` -20.1, `month_rows_net_bp` 90.612. `size` here is
the low-vol factor, not a quantity of things. Retiring a real quote is worse than the hazard the
guard exists for, so those four words are pruned from the guard and published as
`rejected_word_hits` with their values.

Three reusable rules:

(a) **Measure the guard against the values it would move.** Per-word integral/non-integral split of
the moved set is the cheapest possible precision test, and it is what turned a 3,194-cell "fix" into
a 3,109-cell fix that lost nothing.

(b) **Keep a legacy reader next to every changed classifier.** `level_class_by_shape()` is the
pre-change table, verbatim; the delta leg reads the same cells with both, so the change is a
measurement over the tree, not a claim about it, and the retired cells carry the class they would
have had.

(c) **A retired cell is recorded, never dropped.** `count_named_cells` per face carries path, value,
count word and legacy class; the audit's totals shrink *and* say why.

Honest limit: the vocabulary is closed and hand-written, so a count spelled without one of these
words is still read as a level -- the list is the whole coverage claim.

## E210 The reader side needs its own test: can a page number be sourced, and is the cell a quote? (2026-10-29 r1237 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/DOCQUOTES-2026-10-29.json`, mode `docquotes`
(`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 113 -> 119), wired into `Tools/s6_chain_bmb.py` **after**
`daily_report` and `ceo_live_usage`, with a smoke leg that asserts that ordering.

E207/E208 measured the hazard where faces live (`results/`). The reader the CEO actually opens is
`docs/`, and nothing tested it. First run, five families (daily report, live usage, ETF live book,
portfolio recommendation, tactics report): **534 numbers, 471 traceable to the page's own JSON twin
and 61 more to a bounded 1,200-face corpus -- 99.6% sourced**, 2 unsourced, 5 cells flagged as a
count wearing a unit, 12 filed as `integral_under_a_unit`.

What the numbers taught:

(a) **Integrality is a signature, not evidence, and the first rule was wrong.** The first draft
called any integral value under a unit a defect and immediately flagged `cpu_pct: 43.0` printed as
"43.0%" and `py_series_tail: 100.0` -- both real readings that merely land on whole numbers. A
defect now requires the page to print a unit **and** the matching cell's own NAME to carry a count
word; exact integers with no count word go to their own bucket and are never called defects.

(b) **All five survivors were value coincidence, and the face says so.** A printed "RED: 20% cap"
matches `n_holdings: 20`; a printed "-86% ~ -14%" return range matches `n_evidence_missing: 14`.
Value-only matching cannot tell those apart on CJK pages (no shared token to check), so a hit is
published as a candidate to read, with that coincidence named in the caveats -- not as a proven
error.

(c) **The tokeniser is where such an audit fabricates its own quotes.** Four masks were needed
before the totals meant anything: code spans (`510300`), dates, clock times, version strings; commas
are a thousands separator only in groups of three ("T-{74,75,77}" is a set of ids, not a number);
bare six-digit security codes are instruments; and "-Y60,100" puts the sign *before* the currency
symbol, so a loss reads as a gain unless the page is read for it.

How to apply: any audit of a rendered product needs a mask list plus a "candidate vs verdict"
split -- publish what the audit cannot decide, and give the reader the cell that explains the value.

## E209 A count that can prove itself: sibling length as a value-level witness (2026-10-29 r1237 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/COUNTWITNESS-2026-10-28.json`, mode `countwitness`
(`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 119 -> 123).

E207's guard is a WORD test, so it can only speak about counts whose names say "a quantity of
things". E209 supplies the measured side: a numeric leaf whose value equals `len()` of a sibling
list or dict in the same parent object is a count whatever its name says. Over the newest 1,200
faces: **373 measurement-named counts prove themselves; the count-word test already caught 299 of
them (80.2%) and the remaining 74 were invisible to it.** 4,329 witnessed counts sit under names
the quote hazard never reaches, and 62,372 leaves below the floor of 2 are skipped.

Two rules worth keeping:

(a) **A witness is not a proof, and the floor is a line you draw.** 0 and 1 match a single-member
sibling all over any face, so they are skipped and the skip count is published. Even above the
floor, an equal length can be a coincidence -- `published_bars: 4` next to a four-entry
`noise_band_pp` is not a count claim about the same thing. The face reports the witnessing sibling
key so a reader can judge it; it never asserts causation.

(b) **Self-inclusion is a live hazard, and it caught this leg on its first publish.** Run one wrote
4,702 witnessed cells; run two wrote 5,406, because the face published in between was scanned as
input. The study name on disk still said the old id, so the exclusion list did not match it and
nothing looked wrong -- the number simply grew. Renaming the study to the id the exclusion list
declares restored idempotency (4,702 twice). Publish twice and compare, or you cannot tell a
measurement from a self-portrait.

How to apply: when a classifier can only work on names, look for a value-level witness beside it and
report both totals side by side -- the gap between them is the classifier's real coverage claim.

## E211 A measurement that runs once is not a gate: ratchet the page audit on its signature, not on its numbers (2026-10-29 r1238 bm-b)

Evidence: `results/e79/PAGEGATE-2026-10-29.json`, baseline `results/e79/DOCQUOTES-BASELINE.json`,
mode `pagegate` (`scripts/e97_routing.py`, selftest 123 -> 128, legs L121-L125), wired into
`Tools/s6_chain_bmb.py` right after `docquotes`, with a smoke leg that asserts that ordering and
that a baseline exists.

E210 answered "how many numbers on the CEO's pages cannot be sourced" once. r1266 already settled
the general form of that problem: **a verdict that lives only in a receipt is not a gate**. So the
same sweep now runs every round and refuses growth: seeded with 2 signatures, the live gate reads
`2 of 534 numbers have no producer and 0 new ones appeared`.

The design decision worth copying is the signature. It masks every digit:

    family (page path with digits -> '#') | unit context | snippet with every digit -> '#'

so `REPORT-2026-10-29.md` and `REPORT-2026-10-30.md` are the same family, and `-701 份` and
`-702 份` in the same sentence are the same signature. Consequences:

(a) **Daily churn is invisible, so the gate can run every round.** A page whose numbers change and a
page whose numbers *appear* are different events; only the second is a defect. A ratchet keyed on
values would go red every day and be disabled within a week.

(b) **Shrinking is reported and permitted.** A gate that shames a fix is a gate people switch off.
`shrunk` carries the disappearing signature with both counts, and the leg stays green.

(c) **A gate must publish the bound it was seeded from.** `corpus_bound` goes into the baseline
because the trace corpus is a newest-1,200-face window: a number can stop being traceable without any
page changing, simply because its producing face rotated out of the bound. The grown row names the
family to read before anyone widens the bound.

Two wiring rules, both smoke-asserted: the gate runs *after* the sweep it ratchets (a gate in front of
its own subject compares today's pages to yesterday's baseline and reports clean), and a missing
baseline is a seeded state that says so -- never a silent pass.

How to apply: to turn a one-off audit into a standing guard, ratchet a signature that is stable under
legitimate churn (mask the values), publish the set you declared, and treat shrink as success.

## E212 A 0.0bp era gap is not calendar evidence until you know who was holding (2026-10-29 r1239 bm-b)

E59 read the mid-sweep's 2017_2020 column as identical for every off-cycle day at start 2020 and as
-26..+1bp for the same era at start 2013, and treated the second reading as the honest one because a
start-late window looked like the conservative window. It was the other way round: at start 2020 the
engine does not buy until 2021-01-29 (calendar index 262, twelve decision bars after the first one), so
the whole 2017_2020 slice sits inside the eligibility burn-in and earns the repo rate on a 100% cash
book. Every calendar prints gap 0.0 there for the arithmetic reason, not the calendar one.

The leg (mode `midsweepburnin`) publishes, per source face, per calendar and per era: the first decision
on which `e27._rank` returns a name (the exact condition `e27.simulate` buys on, imported rather than
re-implemented), the bar date, and the share of the era slice that sits before it. It re-simulates
nothing -- it reads the gaps E59 already published and derives the boundary beside them.

Readings: 84 cells over four faces; 10 `degenerate_cash_slice` (exactly the ten start-2020 off-cycle
cells in 2017_2020), 59 informative, 1 `exact_zero_with_invested_bars` (mid5 at start 2013 with the fee
off, pre share 0.0 -- a real zero with a real book, published as such and not folded into the bucket).

Why it matters beyond this leg: "a gap of zero" gets read as "these two things behave the same", which
is the strongest and most quotable form of a negative result. Three different claims print as 0.0 -- the
arms genuinely agree, they cancel, or one arm never traded. Only the third one is free to produce, and it
is also the one that looks most convincing because it is exact.

How to apply: before quoting an exact zero (or an exact equality) as robustness, publish the population
that produced it -- first invested bar, cash share, decision count -- beside the number. If the slice is
all cash, the zero is a statement about the window, not about the calendar, and the round should say so
in the face rather than in a footnote. The classification is a pure function of (gap, pre-invested share)
so it can be pinned by selftest legs, which is what stops the next window from re-reading the same zero.

## E213 An off-book penalty has to survive its own liquidity terciles (2026-10-29 r1240 bm-b)

E57 put the whole 2024_now mid-month gap in one bucket: entrants the month-end book neither holds nor
is about to drop earned +0.67% per swap under the mid-month book against +2.30% for the rest, and the
sentence "the calendars read different slices" was published on that difference. Two readings fit it and
the bucket-level number cannot tell them apart. Either the disjoint slice is drawn from thin names that
simply move less -- a composition effect that says nothing about calendars -- or the two books are
reading different moments of similar names, which is a timing claim.

The leg (mode `offbookliq`) republishes the same per-swap reading inside the entrant's own liquidity:
every entrant carries the mean traded amount over the 60 sessions ending at the decision bar, one set of
tercile cuts is drawn from the pooled distribution of BOTH calendars (per-calendar cuts would put one
name in two buckets and destroy the only comparison the leg exists to make), and the bucket is then read
tercile by tercile for each calendar. Nothing is re-priced: the rows are E57's own chase rows with one
field added, and the books still come from the engine's own rank.

Readings on the 2024_now era, disjoint bucket (341 month-end / 326 mid-month entrant slots): the pooled
gap +2.006 vs +0.672 is not uniform inside the terciles. Month-end minus mid-month is -0.475 in the thin
tercile, +0.570 in the middle and +3.774 in the thick one -- the entire headline is a thick-name effect,
and in thin names the sign reverses. One era earlier the same thick column reads -1.584, so the effect is
not even stable in sign across eras.

The classifier publishes what the numbers support instead of picking the nicer half: a gap that holds
inside every tercile in one direction is a timing reading, one whose terciles do not separate is a
liquidity reading, one that changes sign by tercile is its own class (heterogeneous bucket), and one that
holds but where the thick-minus-thin gradient is at least as large as the gap is published as both.

Two rules come out of this card.

(a) **A bucket statistic must be re-read inside the bucket's own covariate.** "The gap sits in bucket X"
is a statement about an average over a heterogeneous set. Until X is split by the thing that plausibly
causes the difference, the bucket is a name for a mixture, not a mechanism.

(b) **Run the verdict on at least two aggregators and publish the disagreement.** The same classifier on
the cell MEANS says `gap_changes_sign_across_terciles` while on the cell MEDIANS it says
`timing_survives_inside_every_tercile` -- on identical cells, because the thin tercile's mean is dragged
by a few large losers (mean 1.573 vs median 1.361 for month-end, 2.048 vs 0.435 for mid-month). A verdict
that only exists under one aggregator is not a verdict, and the instability is itself the publishable
finding: it says the effect lives in the tail, not in the typical swap.

How to apply: any "this slice is different" claim should ship with the slice split by its most obvious
covariate, a pooled threshold set so the split is comparable across arms, and the verdict computed twice.
Publish `verdict_stable_across_aggregators` beside the verdict; a false there is a stop sign for quoting
either number.

## E214 A two-era sign disagreement is not a flip until the span is widened (2026-10-29 r1241 bm-b)

E60 published the thick-name penalty and called it unstable in sign because one earlier era read the
opposite way: 2021_2023 thick-gap -1.584 pp against 2024_now +3.774 pp. That sentence rests on two
eras -- one forward and one reverse, which is a difference, not a flip. Widening the same computation
to a 2017 start (same tercile split, re-cut on the wider pooled distribution) changes the count but not
the shape:

- the third era, 2017_2020, prints +4.288 pp (mean) / +2.301 (median) -- the SAME side as 2024_now,
  so the "unstable" era is not the early panel, it is the 2021_2023 window;
- it is nevertheless NOT counted: its thick disjoint cell holds only 15 priced entrants, below the
  20-slot floor, so it is published under `excluded_eras` with its reading intact instead of being
  allowed to vote. Counting a 15-name cell would have let it decide the verdict.

Both aggregators agree (`thick_gap_sign_flips_across_eras`, spread 5.227 pp on means / 2.982 on
medians), which is the opposite of the E60 result where the mean and the median verdicts disagreed --
so the honest statement is narrower than either: the thick-bucket calendar gap changes sign across
eras, and in this panel only two counted eras exist to say so.

(a) **A verdict over eras needs a counted-era floor and a published exclusion list.** An era whose
    gating cell is thin is a reading, not a replication. Publish it with its slot count and exclude it
    from the vote rather than dropping it silently or letting it swing the sign.

(b) **Widening a span invalidates the old tercile cuts.** The cuts are re-computed on the pooled
    distribution of the wider window, so the per-era gaps here (-1.626 / +3.601) are not the E60 face's
    numbers (-1.584 / +3.774) and must not be joined across faces. Any leg that changes its own window
    has to say so on the face, not leave it to the reader.

How to apply: when a finding is stated over eras, publish `measured_eras` beside the verdict, keep a
per-cell priced floor with the excluded eras listed, and never merge two faces computed on different
spans into one table.
## E215 A gap split into two parts has to print its identity residual (2026-10-29 r1242 bm-b)

E57 (r1134) read the mid-month calendar at the name level: half its entrants are names the month-end
book still holds, so the mid book is "early, not wrong" (+0.96% vs +0.89%). That is a name reading of a
portfolio question. E61 asks the engine instead, by re-running the mid arm with its candidate pool
restricted **at every session** to the month-end book's holdings: same names on offer, different
decision sessions = the timing part; narrow pool vs full universe = the selection part.

The split is an identity, and it is printed as one:

    total = mid - me;  timing = mid_shared - me;  selection = mid - mid_shared;  residual = total - (timing + selection)

Reading (pick_n=10, 2020-01 start, x0 arms): full window +18.0 bp total = **+84.0 timing - 66.0
selection**, residual 0.0. In 2024_now the same two parts are **+191.0 / -117.0** netting +74.0; in
2021_2023 they are +26.0 / -42.0 netting -16.0. So the published net gap is the small difference of
two much larger opposite-signed parts, and both the E57 name reading and the portfolio reading are true
while neither is the headline.

(a) **A decomposition must carry the residual that ties it to the total.** Three numbers that are
    algebraically linked are a check on how the arms were run; without the residual a reader cannot
    separate a decomposition from three independent measurements. The leg's smoke leg fails when
    `identity_ok` is false.
(b) **"The candidate set" is a per-session object.** The engine only had a static universe, which is
    why this card sat `waiting` for three weeks. A default-`None` seam (`simulate(universe_at=...)`)
    keeps every published curve bit-identical (pinned by an e27 selftest leg); the idx-keyed rank cache
    must be bypassed in that mode, since two decisions can share an index with different pools.
(c) **Pin the width before reading a two-way split.** At pick_n=9999 both books hold the entire
    universe, the selection part reads 0 by construction and the "decomposition" would confirm the
    timing story for free. The leg pins pick_n=10 and publishes the pool-size distribution
    (`pool_median`, `pool_binds`), so a restriction that never bound cannot pass as a result.
(d) **An era where all three arms read the same number is a burn-in block** (E212 sibling): offset
    2017_2020 prints 0/0/0 at a 2020 start because the first invested decision is 2021-01-29; it is
    tagged `degenerate_cash_slice` and excluded from the dominance vote.

How to apply: when attributing a calendar (or any structural) cost, restrict the treatment arm to the
control arm's own pool per session, publish the identity residual beside the parts, pin the book width,
and never quote the net gap without the era it was measured in.
## E190 A source that lags is not a source that rewrote history -- split the overlap gate in two (2026-10-28 r1326 bm-c)

WHY
---
`scripts/update_etf_daily.py` refused to write for two consecutive rounds with
`510300@2026-10-28:absent_in_source` (and three siblings) -- exit 3, "source rewrites
history, conscious re-anchor required". Nothing had been rewritten. The wide lane
had verified 2026-10-28 against the same endpoint family and written it into the
same `data/daily/sh<code>.csv` at 17:08; this leg's 19:53 fetch came back only
through 10-27. Two collectors, one file namespace, two vintages of one series.

The gate could never re-open: every later fetch was still shorter than the local
tail, so a correct collector priced a permanent red. Its own exit contract then
pushed the reader toward "history rewrite", which is a *different* claim.

THE SPLIT
---------
A local date that the source does not reach because the source series is SHORTER
(`d > max(source dates)`) is not contradicted by that source; it is also not
verified by it. Publish it as `source_lag` (count + dates in the status face,
merged with nothing appended and nothing deleted) and let the run proceed.
A local date that falls INSIDE the source's range and is still absent stays a hard
exit 3 -- that is the case the gate exists for, and the exemption must not leak
into it.

Two defects this also fixed: every return path of the merge helper now carries the
new key (a reader that has to `.get()` a key is the r1280 family), and the selftest
pins both directions plus an empty-local edge, because an exemption tested only in
its passing direction is a hole.

Pos: scripts/update_etf_daily.py (selftest 15/15, was 12) + live `--takeover` run
rc=0 with `[source_lag 1: 2026-10-28]` on four members.

## E191 A column that always equals another column is not a measurement (2026-10-28 r1326 bm-c)

WHY
---
The E187 pool batch asked for "the repair's per-name count" beside the per-month
net and the book's month-on-month membership turnover. The first cut published
three columns and the third printed 340/340, 791/791 -- identical to book size in
every era, because the screen re-weights every held name rather than adding or
dropping one. The column looked like a count of the names the repair moved and was
a book-size echo.

The batch's own hypothesis (against-months are the high-churn months) failed on the
era it was asked about: 2024_now turnover ratio 0.56 against the frozen 1.10 floor,
high-churn against-share 0.00, corr +0.146. That is a usable negative -- the thin
across-month net may NOT be discounted as membership churn -- but shipping the
degenerate column beside it would have invited the reader to do exactly that.

RULES
-----
1. Before publishing a derived count, ask what makes it differ from the primitive
   it is derived from. If nothing can, publish it as a stated echo (with the
   reading attached) or drop it.
2. Thresholds for an adoption-style verdict are frozen as module constants BEFORE
   the first run and echoed into the payload, so a later reader can tell a rule
   from a description (E188).
3. A zero-delta era must print `no_against_months`, not a passing discount with a
   missing ratio: "cannot be measured here" and "measured and clean" are different
   sentences.

Pos: scripts/e39_panel_census.py `widechurntie` (census selftest 658/658, +7 legs)
+ results/e43_tilt_4member/WIDECHURNTIE-20261028.json (5 eras, 161 months).


## E192 A lag you can only see through one lever is still two lags (2026-10-28 r1327 bm-c)

WHY
---
The book's 8% sizing overlay is `scale = min(1, 0.08 / est_vol)`, where `est_vol` is a
trailing realised vol of the proposed book computed AT a decision and then FROZEN until the
next one (20 bars). Two different lags sit inside that one number -- the estimator's window
and the slot freeze -- and the published headline cannot separate them. The only lever the
engine exposes is `vol_window`, so that is what was swept (20/40/60/120), with the control
arm asserted against the published production cell first.

READING (full window 2020-2026, n12/r20, 10bp/side, next-open)
  20 bars  CAGR 11.30%  SR 1.093  maxDD -11.87%
  40       10.28%       1.057       -11.40%
  60*       9.70%       1.000       -11.77%   (control, reproduces the card)
  120       7.31%       0.837       -12.19%
Depth is nearly flat across estimators (spread 0.79pp) while return is not (spread 3.99pp),
so the lateness is a RETURN cost, not a drawdown benefit: a stale high estimate keeps the book
de-levered through the rebound. The slot census says the same from the other side -- 79 slots,
median realised/estimated 1.06, p90 1.90, 20.2% of slots past 1.5x, 87.3% of slots delevered,
median delivered vol 8.31% against the 8% target. The deepest drawdown (2021-09-14..2022-11-01,
-11.77%, 271 bars) had the book at full size on 31.4% of its bars, and 42% of the depth accrued
on exactly those bars. Verdict under the frozen rule: KEEP (no window buys 1pp of depth inside
a -0.5pp return floor).

RULES
-----
1. A frozen size is two lags (estimate + refresh cadence). Sweep the lever that exists and say
   which of the two the answer belongs to; do not report "the overlay is late".
2. Measure the error, do not assert it: engine `diag` gives `est_vol` at every decision, and
   the next slot's realised vol of the SAME book turns it into `ratio = realised/estimated`.
3. Attribute DEPTH, not a level: the drawdown peak bar is at full size by definition, so
   reading the level there always prints 100%. Use the log-return share accrued on full-size
   bars (here 42%).
4. An overlay that does not move depth is a cost knob, not risk insurance -- quote the return
   it gives up beside the headline instead of calling it free protection.
5. Equivalence legs need a discriminating fixture: on a stationary-vol fixture all four windows
   print the same curve (correct, not a dead test), so the regime-shift fixture is what proves
   the estimator can move (r1261 family).

Pos: scripts/etf_book_voltarget_lag.py (selftest 13/13, chain leg is the engine-free `--check`)
+ results/etf_book_voltarget_lag.json + research/ETF_BOOK_VOLTARGET_LAG.md.

## E193 A placeholder cost is not a measurement -- publish the breakeven, not just the return (2026-10-28 r1138 bm-a)

WHEN: a face prices an overlay/sleeve at one assumed friction (1bp) and the whole
conclusion rests on it.
WRONG: report the headline +0.90pp at equal risk and keep the cost as a footnote. A
gain that only exists at the cheapest possible fill is not a gain -- it is a rounding
artifact of the assumption.
RIGHT: climb a cost ladder on the SAME decision sequence (weights, panels, lag, vol
target all frozen; only the per-side number moves) and publish the breakeven: the highest
cost at which the sleeve still clears the book at equal risk. Real reading of the ETF book
x futures TSMOM overlay: 1bp/side -> +0.90pp (60d) / +0.78pp (120d); 3bp -> -0.10 /
+0.15; 5bp -> -1.09 / -0.48. Breakeven = 1bp (60d) / 3bp (120d), i.e. the overlay has
essentially no friction headroom and must be described as a risk reducer, not a return
source.
GOTCHA: two of them, both caught by the selftest before the face shipped.
1. Turnover must be identical at every cost level -- if it moves, the levels describe
   different strategies and the breakeven is meaningless. Assert it (smoke leg).
2. Rows store weight_in_sleeve rounded to 4dp, so an exact-float lookup of 1/3 matched
   nothing and the breakeven silently came back None. Match weights on the stored
   precision grid, and let the selftest prove a rounded row still resolves.
SHAPE: the breakeven is a cliff, not a number to beat: +0.90pp at 1bp collapsing to
-0.10pp at 3bp says the edge is thinner than the modelling error, so the next move is
cutting turnover (buffer band, E188) -- not re-weighting.
## E193 A faster window can win every phase and still not be a level (2026-10-28 r1328 bm-c)
WHY
---
r1327 swept the sizing overlay's `vol_window` and printed +1.60pp/yr of CAGR for the 20-bar
arm over the 60-bar control, on ONE decision calendar. r1325 had already shown, on the same
book, that a single-cell improvement reverses on 70% of phase-shifted cells. So the ruler was
applied to the sizing question: 4 windows x 4 decision grids started at four bars inside one
20-bar cycle (16 engine runs), every arm measured against the 60-bar control of its OWN
phase, so the calendar is held fixed and only the estimator moves.
READING (full window, n12/r20, 10bp/side, next-open; phase 0 reproduces the published cell
9.70% to the basis point)
  dCAGR(20 vs 60) per phase: +1.60 / +2.76 / +1.40 / +0.54 pp  -> 4/4 up, median +1.50
  the 60-bar control's own CAGR across those phases: 9.12%..10.93%  -> 1.81pp of ruler
  claim of +1.60pp reached at that size in only 50% of phases; sign test p=0.125 at n=4
  w40: +0.58/+0.65/+0.66/+0.34 (direction also stable, half the size); w120: -1.42..-2.39
Verdict under the frozen rule: WITHIN NOISE -- the direction is stable in every phase, the
SIZE is smaller than the calendar noise, so the production window stays and the card prints a
range. That is a different sentence from "the 20-bar window is worth 1.6pp/yr".
RULES
-----
1. Win-rate and effect-size are two doors. A rule that only asks "does it win more often than
   not" would have called +1.60pp a level off 4/4 phases whose minimum is +0.54pp.
2. The ruler is the CONTROL arm's own phase spread, not the effect's spread. Comparing the
   effect's spread to its own median says nothing about whether calendar luck could produce it.
3. Print the per-phase grid, not just the median: the four numbers (and the min) are the whole
   answer, and a table is what lets a reader see the claim is a point estimate from phase 0.
4. Keep the arms paired inside a phase. Comparing arm-A@phase0 to control@phase1 would fold
   the calendar spread (1.81pp here) into the effect and could manufacture either sign.
5. Units cross files: the summary stores control CAGR in percentage points, and rendering it
   through the fraction formatter printed "912.00%" for 9.12pp in the very line describing the
   ruler (r1292/r1316 family). A value's unit is a property of where it was computed, so a
   renderer needs a pp helper and a selftest leg that pins it.
Pos: scripts/etf_book_voltarget_phase.py (selftest 34/34, chain leg is the engine-free
`--check`) + results/etf_book_voltarget_phase.json + research/ETF_BOOK_VOLTARGET_PHASE.md.

## E194 Publish the fragility curve, not the crossing point (2026-10-29 r1330 bm-c)
WHY
---
E189 took out exactly one month per pair -- the month E186 itself named -- and all four
era-level reversals collapsed. That answer is binary: fragile or not. It cannot distinguish
"a one-month object wearing an era verdict" from "an era property that happens to lean on
its worst month", and the difference decides whether the headline may be quoted at all.
READING (four pairs x 3 trims x k=1..8; untrimmed control reproduces E186 to 8.8e-05bp)
  k=1 by |gap|      : agrees 4/4   (identical numbers to E189's carrier removal)
  k=1 by |delta_sum|: agrees 4/4   (same month removed -- the two columns coincide)
  k=1 by era edges  : agrees 3/4   (full-era pair needs k=4, and only stabilises at k=8)
  edges curves are non-monotone: pre2017 pairs agree at k=1, disagree k=2..5/7, agree at 6/8
Verdict: 11 of 12 cells are two-month artefacts, 1 is fragile_mid, 0 are era properties.
RULES
-----
1. Publish the curve, not the crossing. "It agrees once k months are gone" hides whether
   the agreement holds: two of four edge curves flip back to disagreement two bars later.
2. Keep `first_agree_k` and `stable_agree_k` as separate readings. The first says when the
   disagreement stops; the second says whether it stayed stopped. Only the pair is a claim.
3. Trimming the ends is a different experiment from trimming the concentration. They share
   a k axis and nothing else; the face states that edges removes 2k months (k per end) so
   the columns cannot be read at face value against each other.
4. A corroboration is not a discovery. Two trims naming the same month is evidence they
   measure one object -- worth printing, because it is the cheapest way to catch a month
   table that has drifted from the effect it feeds.
5. Stop the ladder before the annualisation stops being honest: below six kept months the
   face stops and publishes `k_max_tested` instead of annualising a two-month window.
