returns 0.928 [0.830, 1.026] on sh510300 and 0.917 on the basket, pre-2017 1.079 / 2017+ 0.883:
the assumption was inside the measurement, and E232's headline erosion is real but far smaller
at the measured exponent (toll -217.3/-187.3/-163.0 bp/yr over the interval, i.e. ~16% lost
across the path, not 2.6x). The toll clears its bar on 6/6 arm cells at every point.

Three rules:
1. **The sampling interval is not the uncertainty.** One control set (same panel, same estimator
   family) moves gamma from 0.928 to 0.255 and the intervals are disjoint; on the basket the
   naive reading collapses to 0.003. Publish the identification span as an explicit point set
   and re-derive every verdict inside it -- otherwise "gamma is known to +/- 0.10" is a claim
   about the regression, not about the number.
2. **A reverse regression's reciprocal is not the forward elasticity.** Publish the reverse fit
   (amount on |r|) as a diagnostic, fence it in code so it can never be read as an estimate,
   and pin that with a selftest leg: 1/0.169 = 5.9 is an object with no interpretation.
3. **Fence the bridge in prose on the face itself.** What is measured is the market's elasticity
   of price to aggregate turnover; using it as our own |dw| exponent assumes the traded amount
   is proportional to |dw| and that our flow meets the same concavity. `frozen_spec.bridge`
   carries that sentence and the smoke leg refuses a face without it.

Sibling findings worth carrying: E230's one passing band is BAND_PAYS on the fine scan from
gamma 0.25 all the way to 1.10, so it is not a curvature artefact inside the measured range
(its limit is path fragility, E231: 12/12 positive, 4/12 clearing the strict bar); and 3 of 12
ladder cells flip only at the naive gamma, all in the same direction -- counted, printed, not
left for a reader to find.

Pit: a constant regressor does not make X'X singular in floats (`1e8` columns invert to
garbage and yield a confident gamma), so the collinearity guard must be scale aware -- test the
determinant of the correlation form, not of the raw cross-product.

Pieces: scripts/e233_empirical_gamma.py (run / selftest, 13 hermetic legs); face
results/state_tilt/EMPIRICAL-GAMMA-<cutoff>.json + empirical_gamma_latest.json; S6 leg
empirical_gamma after cost_curvature; smoke leg requires a published interval containing the
point estimate, the interval endpoints among the re-derived points, a surviving toll channel,
a stable/inside partition of the ladder cells, and the bridge disclosure.

### E235 -- when two regressors can trade places, the split is unmeasured (r1261 bm-b)

E234 priced the five-sleeve split without asking whose turnover the market prices. Fitting
each sleeve's `log|r|` jointly on its own turnover share and the book's aggregate turnover
(HAC 20, volatility-clustering control) gives two of five sleeves a NEGATIVE own elasticity
(sh510500 -0.122, sh512100 -0.147) and signs that move in lock-step across sleeves, while the
pooled book-scaling elasticity collapses to 0.068 against the family's single-regressor 0.622.
That 5.5x attenuation is channel cancellation, not measured concavity.

Three reusable pieces:
1. **Write the regression in identified coordinates.** With `share = lo - la`, (share, la)
   spans the same space as (lo, la) but is numerically well conditioned; the coefficient on
   share IS the own-channel elasticity and the coefficient on la IS the book-scaling
   elasticity, so `b_agg = beta_la - beta_share` and its error bar comes from the coefficient
   COVARIANCE, not the sum of two standard errors. Pin that covariance against the family's
   own estimator in a selftest leg (here: equal to `e233.ols_hac`'s published se to 1e-9).
2. **A negative elasticity on its own channel is a specification verdict, not a number.**
   No impact story prices a move smaller when turnover is larger. When it appears, the
   published verdict is `unresolved_spec_inadequate` and no figure from the face becomes a
   bill -- the pooled slope would otherwise have cleared the bar and been reported as a
   finding.
3. **A control block that cannot invert is a measurement, not a nuisance.** Aggregate
   turnover is 0.936..0.968 autocorrelated on these panels, so `la` and `la_lag` are one
   regressor: the E233 lagged block inverts on 2 of 5 sleeves and those two flip the sign of
   every channel against the identified block. Publish `spec_stable` per sleeve and demand
   sign agreement before any verdict, rather than reading the block that happened to invert.

Selftest gotchas worth keeping: a single hermetic draw carries ~2.7 sigma of sampling error
at n=4000 with R2~0.1, so recovery legs must average across seeds (a per-draw tolerance is
either vacuous or fails a clean estimator); and pooled member dummies must be indexed by
position among the members that ENTERED the design -- indexing by basket position makes the
first surviving member a column of ones and the design exactly singular.

Runner `scripts/e235_charge_granularity.py` (run / selftest, 18 hermetic legs); face
`results/state_tilt/GRANULARITY-<cutoff>.json` + `granularity_latest.json`; S6 leg
`charge_granularity` after `per_member_gamma`; smoke leg requires intervals containing their
points, `g_scaling == b_own + b_agg` per sleeve, and a negative own elasticity carried into
the published verdict. Follow-up card E236 (share-and-difference design) seeded in the pool.

### E236 -- sign agreement is not size agreement (r1262 bm-b)

E235 left the granularity question open on levels. This face re-asks it with three stationary
designs on the same five sleeves and the answer changes shape rather than settling:

- **differences** `dy on d(share), d(book)` -> b_own 0.431 [0.341, 0.520] t 9.43, b_agg 0.879
  [0.769, 0.990] t 15.57
- **frozen_z** (demeaned share + aggregate level in frozen-first-half sd units) -> b_own 0.017
  [-0.004, 0.038] t 1.59, b_agg 0.101 [0.065, 0.138] t 5.43
- **two_way_fe** (member intercepts + date FE) -> b_own 0.023 [0.012, 0.035] t 3.93, b_agg
  absorbed (the aggregate turnover takes one value per date, so date FE eat it entirely)

Every design agrees b_own is POSITIVE and every one has non-overlapping intervals with the
difference design: span 0.017..0.431, ratio 25.4. Verdict `unresolved_magnitude_disagreement`
-- so the family's single exponent stays a price without a bill and E234's per-sleeve sum is
not a substitute. The negative own elasticity E235 saw does NOT survive (negative_survives
false), and per member 2 of 5 sleeves are sign-fragile.

Four reusable pieces:

1. **Demand size agreement, not just sign agreement.** Two designs returning +0.43 and +0.017
   are not "agreed"; a bill built on the first is 25x the bill built on the second. Frozen
   rule: 95% intervals of any two identifying designs must overlap, and the ratio is
   published beside the verdict. Without this gate the same data names `both_channels_live`.
2. **A design that cannot see a regressor must publish `identifies: false`, never a number.**
   Date fixed effects and a date-common aggregate are collinear by construction; the face
   reports `b_agg: null` with `absorbed.date_fixed_effects = 5233` and the agreement test then
   scores b_agg over 2 readers instead of pretending to 3.
3. **A design with one observation per cell identifies nothing, and must say so.** With a
   single member, within-date demeaning removes the share along with everything else -- the
   fit returns `identifiable: false` ("share carries no within-date variation"). A selftest leg
   pins that a single-member panel never publishes b_own.
4. **Two quantities can wear one name.** The difference design prices a session against that
   session's own turnover change, which co-moves with the same information arrival that sets
   `|r|`; the cross-sectional designs price the share. That asymmetry is the mechanism behind
   the 25x gap and is what makes the surface reading ("both channels live") wrong.

Runner `scripts/e236_share_difference.py` (selftest 18/18), face
`results/state_tilt/SHAREDIFF-<cutoff>.json`, S6 leg `share_difference` after
`charge_granularity`, smoke leg requiring the absorbed-aggregate disclosure and that an
unresolved verdict publishes no granularity charge.

### E237 -- an artefact claim needs a control that is not a component of the dependent variable (r1263 bm-b)

E236 left a 25x size gap between its designs and named the mechanism in prose. This face
measured it instead: the share coefficient (0.431) loses only 0.037 when the market's own |r| is
controlled (0.394), and the first stage says the share never carried market information
(d(share) on d(log|r_book|) = -0.0024, t -1.23, R^2 0.0001) -> verdict `own_channel_survives`,
4 of 5 sleeves survive, sh510300 is `residual_only_channel`. The card's bill rule still refuses
the per-member charge: the two return components both clear the bar (0.1085 [0.1001, 0.1169] vs
0.0730 [0.0658, 0.0802], 7.4s / 9.7s against a 2.77 t bar over 9 pooled tests) but their
intervals are disjoint, ratio 1.49.

Three reusable pieces:

1. **A control that is a component of y is not a control.** dy = d(common) + d(relative)
   identically, so controlling the relative part leaves only the common part and every other
   coefficient measures its covariance with d(common) -- the first draft would have stamped
   "artefact" on a synthetic panel whose truth was a genuine relative channel. The control has
   to be an outside regressor (the market's own |r|).
2. **Refuting an artefact claim is two measurements**: the coefficient under the control, and
   the first stage that says whether the regressor ever carried the confounder. One alone
   cannot distinguish "the confounder was absorbed" from "the channel died of collinearity"
   (the face ships `unresolved_share_dies_without_comovement` for exactly that state).
3. **Direction belongs to the design, not to the analyst.** d(share) + d(book) = d(own), so the
   turnover-direction version of the artefact test is a 1.0/1.0 split regardless of the data;
   the share designs keep E236's direction (dy on turnover changes) and the components designs
   take the card's (turnover on return components), and no coefficient is compared across them.

Runner `scripts/e237_information_structure.py` (selftest 18/18), face
`results/state_tilt/INFOSTRUCT-<cutoff>.json`, S6 leg `information_structure` after
`share_difference`, smoke leg requiring a live co-movement reading beside the verdict, the
published identity residual, and a refused gate that prices no granularity charge.

### E238 -- a denominator that contains the numerator is not a control, and the fix can go either way (r1264 bm-b)

E236/E237 chased an own-turnover coefficient that came out 25x apart depending on design, and
E237 left "what it measures" unnamed. The cheapest remaining explanation was arithmetic: the
share is own turnover over BOOK turnover, and the book is the same five members, so every
member's own turnover sits inside its own denominator. This face rebuilt the book EXCLUDING the
member (leave-one-out) and re-ran the same three designs on the identical sessions
(13,911 pooled differenced rows, 2026-10-29 cutoff).

The answer splits by design, and that is the point:

- **differences** (design A): inclusive 0.4371 [0.3467, 0.5275] -> leave-one-out
  0.5963 [0.5100, 0.6825], gap -0.1592 with block-bootstrap [-0.1778, -0.1359]. Removing the
  member from its own book makes the coefficient BIGGER, so the inclusive denominator
  ATTENUATES; a denominator artefact would have killed it. Verdict
  `own_channel_survives_the_denominator` (4 of 5 members, sh510300 unresolved).
- **frozen_z** (design B): inclusive -0.0366 [t -3.68] -> leave-one-out +0.0013 [t 0.14]. The
  level design's reading WAS the share arithmetic: gap -0.0379 against a predicted -0.0354
  (93% of the gap), verdict `own_channel_is_a_denominator_artefact`.
- **two_way_fe** (design C): 0.02346 -> 0.02289, invariant to three decimals. The date fixed
  effects absorb the book, so the denominator never entered this design at all.

Reusable pieces:

1. **Publish the mechanical prediction, not just the gap.** With own share w the regression is a
   reparameterisation of one object, so the gap the arithmetic predicts is
   `kappa*(b_own_loo - b_agg_loo)` with `kappa = w/(1-w)`. Realised turnover share on this panel
   is 0.218 (kappa 0.279), NOT the nominal 1/5 (kappa 0.25) - the book is equal-weight in
   capital, not in turnover, and the per-member shares run 0.03..0.46. Only with that number
   beside it can the measured gap (-0.159) be read: arithmetic accounts for 0.049 of it and
   points the same way, so ~0.11 is a real effect and the artefact label is correct only for
   the design where the prediction covers the whole gap.
2. **A constant share makes the inclusive design degenerate.** If the own share never moves, the
   inclusive regressor is constant up to floating-point dust and OLS on dust returns 3.8e13 with
   a confident t. The runner refuses below `SHARE_SD_FLOOR=1e-8` and the selftest pins both the
   refusal and the leave-one-out design staying estimable - "the coefficient exists only because
   the share moves" is the sharpest form of the mechanical argument.
3. **The regressor identity is exact, so pin it as an assertion.** d(share)_incl -
   d(share)_loo = d(log rest) - d(log agg) = -d(log(1-w)) to 0.0e+00 on every member. The two
   denominators are two restrictions of one regression, so their disagreement is never evidence
   about the market by itself.
4. **Bootstrap the paired gap, but only the point estimate.** 400 block replicates (20-session
   blocks, dates resampled jointly so the pooled cross-section stays aligned) of a lean normal-
   equation fit ran in 29s; re-running the Newey-West covariance inside each replicate would buy
   nothing and cost minutes.

Runner `scripts/e238_leave_one_out_book.py` (selftest 8/8), face
`results/state_tilt/LOOBOOK-<cutoff>.json` + `loo_by_member_<cutoff>.csv`, S6 leg
`leave_one_out_book` after `information_structure`, smoke leg requiring both denominators with
intervals, the identity as a number, and the artefact label only where the predicted gap sits
inside the measured gap interval. No charge, no adoption: E239 asks the next question the
leave-one-out book raised (in the rest-free book the cross coefficient 0.771 exceeds the own
0.596, which is either economics or the units of summing four members).

### E239 -- a sum of four members is not four counterparties: price the channel per pair (r1265 bm-b)

E238 left an ordering it could not price: in the leave-one-out book the REST coefficient
(0.771) exceeded the own coefficient (0.596). Two readings survive -- an economic statement
(a member's own turnover matters less than the rest of the book's) or a unit (the rest
aggregate is the SUM of four members, so one log point of it is four participants' worth).
Rescaling the sum to a mean cannot settle it: it multiplies the coefficient by four and leaves
every t-statistic and every interval width unchanged. The only apples-to-apples form is per
pair -- regress dy on d(log own) and on d(log other_j) for each other member separately, same
HAC lag, same fixed effects, same break bar, then compare own against the MEAN single-member
cross (13,911 pooled pair rows, 20 ordered pairs, cutoff 2026-10-29).

The answer, and it is not the one E238's ordering suggested:

- **pair fixed effects**: own 0.797 [0.748, 0.846] vs cross 0.314 [0.274, 0.353],
  difference 0.484 [0.408, 0.560] against a multiplicity bar of |t| >= 3.227 (two-sided 5%
  over 20 pairs).
- **member + date fixed effects** (denominator-invariant per E238): own 0.539, cross 0.055,
  difference 0.484 with interval [0.438, 0.530]. The difference point estimate is identical to
  three decimals across the two designs, so the ordering is not an artefact of which fixed
  effects are absorbed.
- **the sum's own coefficient is only 54% of what four counterparties imply**: mean
  single-member cross 0.356 (20 pairs, range -0.242..1.128, 15/20 positive), so 4 x 0.356 =
  1.425 against E238's published rest 0.771 -> ratio 0.541. The aggregate is not an additive
  sum of participant impact; roughly half of what a single counterparty carries does not
  survive in the sum.

Reusable pieces:

1. **"Rescale the aggregate" is not a control.** Dividing a regressor by a constant changes a
   coefficient by that constant and leaves the t-statistic, the interval width and the sign of
   the difference untouched. When the units are the objection, the fix is a different
   REGRESSOR (one counterparty, not the sum), not a different scaling.
2. **Pair fixed effects belong in the demeaned coordinates, not in 20 dummies.** Within-block
   demeaning of y, d(log own) and d(log other) is exactly the pair intercept and keeps the
   design matrix at k=3, so the HAC sandwich stays tractable (20 pairs x ~1400 rows, lag 20).
   Same estimator, different coordinates.
3. **Publish the mean of the published members.** The mean cross must be machine-derived from
   the per-pair list and the smoke leg asserts `|mean(list) - published_mean| < 1e-9`; a summary
   that prints a mean no reader can recompute is a second estimator nobody audited.
4. **Prove the machinery ran before believing any equality** (E188/r1261 family): assert pairs
   fitted == pairs total == 20, pooled rows > 5000 and both regressor sds above the dust floor
   inside the selftest, not only in the runner.
5. **A dust counterparty regressor is refused per pair, not fitted.** A member whose turnover
   never moves has no cross variation; that pair is reported with a reason, and if every block
   is dust the pooled design returns `unresolved_no_pairs` rather than a number.
6. **Read the previous face, never restate its numbers.** The 4x cross-versus-rest comparison
   machine-reads `loobook_latest.json` and declares its own absence (`available: false`) if the
   file is missing -- hand-copied constants in a follow-up face are how a family silently drifts.

Runner `scripts/e239_own_vs_cross.py` (selftest 11/11), face
`results/state_tilt/OWNCROSS-<cutoff>.json` + `owncross_pairs_<cutoff>.csv`, S6 leg
`own_vs_cross` after `leave_one_out_book`, smoke leg requiring both designs' intervals to
contain their own points, the mean cross to be the mean of the published pairs, and the frozen
verdict vocabulary to be honoured. No charge, no adoption: the family still has no bill, and
the next question is why the aggregate loses half of the single-counterparty channel.

### E240 -- a published coefficient is only comparable inside its own parametrisation (r1266 bm-b)

E239 published ratio 0.541 by dividing E238's rest coefficient (0.771) by four times the mean
single-counterparty cross slope (1.425). Both halves of that division are read in the wrong
coordinates, and the two errors do not cancel.

1. **0.771 is the book-scaling coefficient, not the rest coefficient.** E238's design is
   `dy on d(share_m), d(log agg)`; its published `loo_b_agg` is the coefficient on d(log agg).
   Writing the same regression in per-counterparty coordinates, `B = a + c` and
   `A = a - kappa*c` with `kappa = w/(1-w)`, so `c = (B - A)/(1 + kappa)`: the rest channel
   behind the published pair (0.596, 0.771) is 0.140 at the nominal w = 1/5, not 0.771.
   Reading `loo_b_agg` as "the rest channel" quotes the whole book against a quarter of one
   counterparty.
2. **Four times one counterparty is not the sum.** The right comparator is one counterparty,
   because the additive world makes the sum-of-four slope EQUAL to the per-counterparty slope:
   `y = c*S` partialled on own leaves the sum slope at exactly c, whatever rho is. So the
   reading is not "keeps 54%" but "keeps 0.138 against one counterparty's 0.183".
3. **The naive pair slope is inflated by omission, and the inflation is measurable.** A pair
   regression with own and ONE counterparty stands in for k counterparties, so its slope is
   `c*(1+(k-1)rho - k rho^2)/(1-rho^2)`: on this book rho = 0.424 and the factor is 1.89. Any
   comparison that starts from the raw pair slope inherits that factor.
4. **The identified test needs no algebra at all.** Regress on own, one counterparty, and the
   sum of the other three -- one and the sum-of-three are in the same units, no channel is
   omitted, and additivity predicts equal coefficients. Measured: one 0.183, three 0.175,
   difference +0.008 [-0.012, +0.022] -> the rest channel adds up.
5. **Two controls that cannot be built are published as refusals, not skipped.** The
   leave-one-out book move is exactly SUM/(k-1) (measured max deviation 0.0), so (own, sum,
   book) is singular; and the two-way FE form of the same design puts the total book move into
   the date effect, which makes the within-transformed sum regressor minus own (residual
   correlation -1.0). A face that quietly dropped these would look cleaner and say less.
6. **A published pair that does not reproduce is a finding.** Re-running E238's coordinates on
   this face's grid gives (A, B) = (-0.333, -0.174) against the published (0.596, 0.771). The
   gap is published with the face (`parametrisation_check.gap_A/gap_B`) instead of being
   reconciled by assertion; the rotation is next round's work, not a footnote.

Runner `scripts/e240_aggregate_attenuation.py` (selftest 12/12), face
`results/state_tilt/AGGATT-<cutoff>.json`, verdict `rest_channel_is_additive` on 1,328 common
sessions to 2026-10-29. No charge, no adoption.

### E241 -- a shared grid is not a shared row: check the y against its regressors before blaming the population (r1267 bm-b)

E238 published the leave-one-out pair (b_own 0.596, b_agg 0.771) on each member's own date grid; E240 re-ran the same coordinates on the common five-member grid and read (A -0.333, B -0.174), then published the gap as a finding about the population. E241 rebuilt it as a ladder with ONE change per rung -- r0 E238 reproduced on its own rows (0.596 / 0.771, exact), r1 the same construction on the common dates (0.891 / 0.467), r2 the inclusive denominator on those rows (0.790 / 1.344), r3 E240's routine as published (-0.333 / -0.174), r3b the same routine with its y taken from the session the regressors describe (0.791 / 1.343).

The rung that carries the whole discrepancy is the READER: `common_increments` indexes its arrays by the KEPT session (dy[i] is the move into dates[i]) while `design_share_book` re-paired the sessions from k=1 and appended dy[k-1], so the dependent variable sat one session behind the share increments -- 6,630 of 6,635 rows carried the PREVIOUS session's move and zero carried the same session's. Feeding the identical increments and the correctly paired y through the identical routine returns the E238 family again. Corrected pair: A 0.791 [0.629, 0.953], b_agg 0.552; the row restriction alone moves the rest channel 0.771 -> 0.552 and nothing flips a sign. E240's A/B pair is superseded (F-20261030-01); its dy/dx designs are untouched because they index both arrays by the same session.

How to apply: (1) when two faces on the same grid disagree, publish the ladder and let one rung change one thing -- a sign flip that survives every rung is a population statement, one that appears at a code-path rung is a defect; (2) a shared grid is a shared SET of sessions, not a shared alignment: before attributing a coefficient to the sample, assert that the y vector and each regressor carry the same session (a count, not a correlation); (3) one parametrisation can hold two meanings -- b_agg = g - b_own is the derived channel while g is the denominator's own coefficient, so a gap between one face's derived number and another face's raw coefficient is mostly two objects (measured here: 0.333 of a 0.945 naive gap); (4) a face whose reader is repaired must disclose its published numbers as superseded in the same round.

### E242 -- an omission factor is only a number against a named identified form, and it cancels in a paired difference (r1268 bm-b)

E240 priced one omission in one design (x1.89). E242 swept the family: 15 published slopes read from four faces, 7 re-priceable, four nested regressor sets on one y and one own regressor (own only / own+ONE / own+one+sum-of-rest-3 / own+each-of-four). Measured on 1,328 common sessions: the single-counterparty cross level is x1.53 of the own+four form and x1.78 of the one+sum form, against a closed form of x1.89 at rho 0.424 -- so the factor spans 1.53..1.89 depending only on which identified form you name, and the two identified forms disagree by 14% on the same grid because a sum is a constrained combination of four collinear coefficients. The own channel is inflated too (x1.22 own+one, x1.36 own-only), by less than the cross channel.

What the omission does NOT move is the paired difference. E239 rests on b_own - b_cross = 0.4837; the identified re-price is 0.6675 [0.4978, 0.8373] -- the omission moves it 0.060, while the GRID (per-pair intersections vs the common grid) moves it 0.243, four times more. The cancellation is structural: the omitted block loads on the own and the cross regressor alike, and the pooled design runs every ordered pair in both directions, so both biases cancel (pinned by two fixtures, including one with a counterparty deliberately decoupled from the common factor). Published verdicts therefore split cleanly: E239 (difference-based) survives and strengthens; E240 is already identified (its control reproduces at 0.0); E236/E233 regress on own+aggregate and omit nobody.

How to apply: (1) quote an omission factor only beside the identified coefficient it is measured against, and publish the factor for every identified form you can build -- one comparator is one number, not the factor; (2) before re-pricing a verdict ask whether it consumes a level or a difference: levels are what omission inflation moves, paired differences are spared when the omitted block loads on both retained regressors and the pairing runs both directions; (3) a grid change can dominate the omission -- recompute the published number on the analysing grid before attributing any of the gap to omitted variables; (4) a magnitude gate's exposure is an interval (the window between the published and the identified level), never a verdict.

### E243 -- a verdict carried by an aggregator, not by the data: the mean says zero, the counterparties do not (r1269 bm-b)

E240 read `rest_channel_is_additive` off one window. E243 re-ran the identical identified design (`y_m` on own, ONE counterparty, and the SUM of the other k-2) on ten cells -- the headline replay, the pre-2020 four members, three five-member eras, five leave-one-member-out -- plus one labelled k=3 degenerate cell. Not one cell supports additivity. Rollup verdict `additivity_only_in_the_mean`: in the headline cell the mean difference is +0.0079 but NINE of twenty ordered pairs have their own HAC interval clear of zero, with both signs, so the equality is the cancellation of counterparties that individually disagree. Headline replay of E240 is exact to 1e-9 (diff 0.007854), so this is the same design, not a re-cut one.

Three mechanics, each of which invalidated something E240 published:

(1) The interval was the wrong object. E240's +/-0.012/+0.022 is the across-pair spread divided by sqrt(20) -- the standard error of a mean of heterogeneously-identified quantities, which measures how well the mean is pinned and not whether the difference is identified. The same cell's conservative interval (average per-pair HAC half-width) is +/-0.32, and every cell in the grid lands at +/-0.20..0.68. A cell wider than a frozen resolution line (0.10 log points) is published `unresolved_interval_too_wide`, never additive.

(2) The pooled aggregator reports exactly zero on ANY data. Its `-4.1e-15` corroborated the verdict in E240 and is an artifact: the pooled stack replicates one member's response vector across its k-1 counterfactual "one counterparty" blocks, which annihilates the direction separating `b_one` from `b_rest3`, and the solver returns the symmetric split. Proven on every run by three fixtures whose truths differ by +0.88 / -0.55 / 0.00: pooled difference at most 3.2e-15 in all three, while the pair design reads the gap.

(3) `b_one - b_rest` is not a pair. It prices one member against the SUM of k-2 others, so its sign is a property of the sum's composition. When the rest channel is heterogeneous there is no single difference to estimate, and 9/20 mixed-sign exclusions are what that looks like from outside.

How to apply: (1) before publishing a mean across pairs/cells, count how many of those cells' own intervals exclude zero and publish the count -- a mean of heterogeneous quantities is a number that no cell reports; (2) an interval built from a spread across units is a precision statement about the mean, not an identification statement about the effect -- pair it with the units' own intervals and use the wider one; (3) when a second aggregator "confirms" a verdict, prove that it can DISAGREE first: run it on a fixture whose truth is the opposite sign (the pooled design here fails that test at 1e-15); (4) a verdict whose interval exceeds the resolution line is NOT-ASSESSED, and "failed to reject" must never be journaled as "supported".

### E244 -- two signs are not two channels: a member that pays more than the rest casts its mirror into the sum (r1270 bm-b)

E243 killed the additivity verdict and left the structure unnamed (9 of 20 ordered pairs clear of zero, both signs). E244 reads those published intervals (sha256 recorded, nothing transcribed) and asks where the spread lives. Answer: on WHO is singled out. Variance over 152 pairs: counterparty identity 50.7% / own identity 0.1% / residual 49.5%; two repeatable carriers with one sign each -- sh588000 +0.800 over 22 decisive pairs in 8 cells, sh512100 -0.498 over 9 pairs in 6 cells -- every other member mixing signs and therefore not a carrier.

The reusable law: since diff = b_one - b_rest3, a member that pays MORE than the rest reads POSITIVE when it is the counterparty and NEGATIVE for every ordinary member, because it sits INSIDE the sum they are priced against. That mirror is arithmetic, not a second channel -- the deviant has three roles (own / counterparty / inside the sum) and only the third casts it. So a sign split can never name two channels; a split needs a repeatable name on each side, and the mirror ratio must be published beside it (1.61 against a frozen floor 1.50).

Two traps: (1) a NOISELESS fixture makes every regression fit exactly, every interval zero-width and every pair 'decisive' -- the carrier test named three carriers on a one-deviant book (same family as E238's degenerate-panel refusal: put noise in the fixture); (2) 'ordinary-vs-ordinary pairs read ~0' is false -- they read the mirror too, so the structural claim has to be about the deviant's three roles, and the stragglers that appear off the deviant in small cells are counted, not denied. Overlapping cells are repeats, never replications (published as independent_observations=false).

### E245 -- "the rest channel" is a star, and the sum design is what hid it (r1271 bm-b)

E244 located the spread on counterparty identity but read it through a SUM regressor (`rest` = one member against an average of the others). E245 fits the un-summed matrix: dy_m on own turnover plus EVERY other member separately, jointly per row with one HAC per cell, six cells. Answer in the five-member window (1328 sessions, resolution 0.264): row equality is rejected in four of five rows and ONE column carries it -- sh588000's turnover is decisive for 4 of 4 members with mean slope 0.769, while every other column sits at 0.07..0.28 with 0/4..2/4 decisive. In the era cells it repeats (0.90 in 2023-24, 0.90 in 2025-now, 0.56 in 2020-22) but those cells cannot resolve (half-widths 0.42..0.51 against a 0.30 bound). Pre-2020, before sh588000 prints, there is NO star: the four columns read 0.36/0.43/0.33 with rows rejecting equality -- diffuse, not concentrated.

The reusable law: a sum regressor averages a star and its ordinary members into one coefficient, so `b_one - b_rest` reports the star POSITIVE and every ordinary member NEGATIVE (E244's "two carriers"), while the un-summed matrix shows the negative carrier is the mirror of the deviation, not a second channel: sh512100's column is decisive 0/4 with slope 0.073 in the headline cell, yet E244 published it at -0.498. Name structure from the un-summed slopes, then decide whether a single rest slope is even the right object.

Three traps: (1) a fixture keyed by ints against panels keyed "S0".."S4" reads every cross term as 0.0 -- the homogeneous leg passed on an EMPTY panel until the activity assertion (mean cross slope == truth) was added, so every equivalence leg must first prove the mechanism moved (r1261/E199 family); (2) "decisive column" is not "star": with a real effect everywhere every column is decisive, so a star needs a RATIO to the median column (2.0) or signal reads as concentration; (3) a cell whose intersection is empty (five members before sh588000 exists) silently reports zero sessions -- name the members a cell may use rather than letting the intersection choose.

How to apply: (1) never publish a mean over an average-of-others regressor without the per-member matrix; (2) any "concentration" verdict needs a relative bar, not an absolute decisiveness count; (3) publish the resolution per cell and refuse to name structure above it -- four of six cells here are honestly undecided.

### E246 -- the star leads, but the sign alternates (r1272 bm-b)

E245 named a star (sh588000 decisive for every other member, mean slope 0.769) and could not say whether that sleeve's flow moves the book or whether all five load on one driver it reads best. E246 refits every row with the counterparties' turnover at lag 1 and 2 **in the same regression as their contemporaneous turnover**, publishing both directions. Headline cell (1,328 sessions, resolution 0.264): lag0 +0.769 (5/5 decisive), lag1 **-0.527** (5/5), lag2 **+0.397** (5/5), reverse 0/4. Verdict star_flow_leads_with_sign_flip: the past does predict the book, but the lags disagree in sign in a decaying alternation -- an oscillation in a shared driver, not a one-way flow, so nothing can be billed to that sleeve. Pre-2020 four (no sh588000) reads contemporaneous_common_factor with lag1 -0.117 at 0/4, i.e. nothing leads at all; four era cells stay unresolved (half-widths 0.42..0.51 vs a 0.30 bound) and the rollup refuses to average them.

The reusable law: **a lag column without its contemporaneous twin is a proxy, not a test.** With only lagged counterparties in the row, a persistent sleeve's lag regressor is 0.87-correlated with its present value and the lone lag coefficient re-reads the present column -- a fixture whose true lead was 0.00 came back at 0.51. Partialling the past against the present is what makes the number a lead, and the placebo band (permuted series) came back at -0.019 (se 0.106) where the real member row reads -0.662.

Three traps: (1) a decisive NEGATIVE lead is still a lead -- a gate that required a positive mean would have thrown away this face's largest coefficient; the sign belongs in the verdict vocabulary (star_flow_leads_inverse, ..._with_sign_flip); (2) fixtures must accumulate the level -- E245's fixture appends the increment into the y slot so its dy is the difference of two increments, and with a lead term that reads as a sign flip (-0.584 for a +0.6 truth); (3) an answer function with no branch for a mixed rollup prints a contradiction next to its own count -- the smoke leg now fails when the one-line and the rollup disagree. Own-lag persistence (AR1 of dlog turnover -0.33..-0.44, t -11.9..-16.7) is published per member because it is exactly the collinearity the contemporaneous block has to absorb.

### E247 -- an undecided cell is not always an invitation to wait (r1273 bm-b)

E246 left four of six cells undecided (median half-widths 0.42..0.51 against a frozen 0.30 bound) and the natural next sentence is "collect more data". E247 turns that sentence into a number per cell, from the published face alone: a fixed-design CI half-width shrinks as h(n) ~ h0*sqrt(n0/n), so the sessions needed to reach a target are ceil(n0*(h0/target)**2). Reading: all_five and pre2020_four are decided; **era_2020_2022, era_2023_2024 and early_three are CLOSED windows** -- their sessions will never arrive, so no amount of patience decides them and the honest options are a longer window or a different star; only era_2025_now is open and it needs 1,158 sessions against the 408 it has (short 750, ~3 years at its observed rate). The 80%-power floor is published too (z=2.802, SE=h/1.96): a cell sitting at the 0.30 bound can only see |slope| >= 0.43.

The reusable law: **before recommending "more data", ask whether the window can produce it.** A calendar slice with a closed upper bound converts an unresolved coefficient into a permanently unresolved one, and the two cases must not share a verdict word -- unreachable_window_closed versus open_but_needs_sessions is the whole product. A session shortfall is also not a date: it is a lower bound that assumes the design and the effect's variance hold.

Three traps: (1) half-width arithmetic is only valid for a fixed design -- it says nothing about a redesign that trades history for members; (2) a "decided" cell must be read against the same bound the face froze, not a bound re-chosen after the fact; (3) a closed-window cell still reports its shortfall number, but the verdict must not let a reader act on it (the count exists, the sessions do not).

### E248 -- a coefficient read against a model is not a coefficient: spend the design's own null (r1274 bm-b)

E246 published the star lead as a lag0/lag1/lag2 sign flip (+0.769 / -0.527 / +0.397), each
coefficient against its own HAC interval. Two holes: two lags cannot separate a two-session
oscillation from a decay into noise, and an HAC interval is a MODEL -- on a persistent series the
model is the question. The fix is to spend the design's own null: shuffle the star's turnover
series in time, refit the same regression 200 times, and require the coefficient to beat what a
shuffled series produced. Extended to lags 1..5 in ONE regression (each lag a partial effect),
the row reads +0.653 / -0.586 / +0.091 / -0.204 / +0.077 / +0.027: only lag 1 crosses, at
negative sign, in five of six cells; the published "rebound" at lag 2 never crosses anywhere
(0.09 / -0.17 / 0.09 / 0.02 / 0.22 / -0.18 against lines 0.35..0.66). The shapeless verdict is
`band_without_shape` with the post-hoc label `single_lag_lead_no_rebound` beside it.

Three reusable laws. (1) **A per-lag band is not a screen**: five lags at 95% is a discovery
machine -- a pure-noise fixture crossed at lag 5 in one screen out of four -- so the decision line
must be the max |coefficient| any draw produced ANYWHERE in the screened set (family-wise, ~1/201
with 200 draws). (2) **More lags change the partials**: going from {1,2} to {0..5} moved lag 2
from +0.397 to +0.091 because that coefficient was carrying lag 3; a distributed-lag row is only
comparable with the same lag set. (3) **A frozen vocabulary must be allowed to fail**: the
observed shape (one decaying negative lead) is in none of the four pre-registered names, so the
face publishes `band_without_shape` and puts the descriptive reading next to it, labelled post-hoc
-- publishing the closest name would have been the dishonest option.

Implementation note: the numpy Newey-West fit is pinned to e235's pure-python estimator (beta and
covariance diagonal to 1e-9) and the lag-0 row is pinned to E246's published row, so the ~8s face
is the same estimator rather than a re-estimation.

### E249 -- one column read on its own is not a channel: read every column before naming one (r1275 bm-b)

E248 spent the design's own null and found exactly one crossing: lag 1, negative, -0.586 on the
star column, against a family-wise line of 0.351. That result admits two readings a single-column
row cannot separate -- that sleeve's flow is absorbed (a source with a negative sign), or a
book-wide liquidity proxy mean-reverts and the star is simply the column the fit reads cleanest.
The counterfactual is cheap and direct: refit the same lags-1..5 row with EACH member as the
column of interest (the other four are the rows), give every column its own 200-draw shuffled
band, and compare the column-to-column spread against the measured within-column band.

The answer flips the naming. All five columns are negative: -0.233 / -0.238 / -0.280 / -0.303 /
-0.586. Four of five clear their own nulls, and the spread across columns (0.352) is SMALLER than
the median within-column band width (0.384) -- spread/band 0.92. The star is the largest of five
negatives, not an outlier, so the negative one-session lead is a BOOK-WIDE number and no member
may be billed for it. The star column reproduces E248's published -0.585816805073 to twelve
digits, so the two faces are the same design and the difference is the question, not the data.

Three reusable laws. (1) **One column, one reading**: "the star is negative" and "every column is
negative" are the same number until the columns are read side by side; a channel claim needs the
rest of the columns as controls, and the cheapest control is refitting with the column of interest
moved. (2) **Specificity needs a separation test, not a crossing count**: "exactly one column
crossed" is a statement about power. Two of the six cells here are labelled `column_specific` on
the frozen vocabulary and both are low-power cells where the other columns carry the SAME sign and
sit just inside their own nulls (gap/band 0.441 and 0.509 -- well inside one band width). The face
keeps the frozen label and publishes a post-hoc separation block that reclassifies both to
`book_wide_same_sign` and names them, so the vocabulary is not rewritten after the fact.
(3) **Compare a spread to a measured band, never to a constant**: the same five readings are
`multiple_crossings_differ` against a 0.05-wide band and `book_wide_same_sign` against a 0.60-wide
one; the gate is pinned to move with the band it is handed.

Implementation note: the estimator, the shuffled band and the star rule are E248/E245/E246
verbatim (no re-estimation), the whole 6-cell five-column sweep takes ~14s, and a smoke leg
re-derives every verdict from the published per-column numbers and pins the star's lag-1 to
E248's published value to 1e-9.

## E250 — the shared lead is a book-wide quantity, and the column sits inside its own denominator

`scripts/e250_book_turnover_echo.py` (selftest 15/15) · face `results/state_tilt/BOOKECHO-2026-10-29.json`

E249 read every column of the star row and found them all negative at comparable magnitude, which
named no sleeve. E250 tests the obvious mechanism — all five columns are proxies for ONE book-level
quantity, aggregate turnover — with the aggregate in the row alone, beside each column, and as a
leave-one-out sum. Read: **c1 = −0.521** against a 200-draw shuffled-aggregate band
**[−0.206, 0.201]** on the headline cell, −0.50…−0.68 across cells, negative and outside its band
in 5 of 6 (era_2020_2022 at −0.316 sits inside, so that cell is silent, not contrary). Per-column
lag-1 shrinks toward zero once the leave-one-out aggregate is in: **mean share 0.913**, while the
star keeps ~90% of its −0.805 — the star is not merely the most readable proxy, and its residual is
the only sleeve-attributed part of the E248/E249 reading still standing (E251).

Three reusable findings:

(1) **A grid has two lists, and using the wrong one slides every lag.** `panel["dates"]` are the
INCREMENT dates; a level-difference series built from that list comes out exactly one row short
(1,327 vs 1,328), so `dy[t] on series[t−1]` is quietly a two-session lag. The first burn of this
face reported c1 = **+0.644**, positive and outside the band, on a panel whose every column is
negative. Assert the alignment (`len(series) == len(panel rows)`) inside the cell builder, not in
the caller, and put the same assertion in the selftest — the number moves the entire conclusion.

(2) **A column inside the sum is not a share.** "How much of each column's lag-1 does the aggregate
account for?" is the E238 denominator-contains-the-numerator reading whenever the column's own
turnover is a component of the aggregate: three of five columns read a share > 2 there. The
leave-one-out sum is the readable number (0.10–1.32, mean 0.91); publish both and label which is
which, or the arithmetic channel hides inside a "finding".

(3) **A fixture answers the design it was written for, not the one you published.** The synthetic
panel first wrote the effect through the *contemporaneous* aggregate while the published row reads
`t−1`; it passed only because an off-by-one in the real series happened to compensate. Three legs
went red the moment the real alignment was fixed. Generate the fixture's effect at the same lag the
face estimates, and prefer an independent truth (a written coefficient) over a reassuring pass.

## E251 — a decomposition of a different number is not a decomposition (r1277 bm-b)

`scripts/e251_star_own_component.py` (selftest 18/18) · face `results/state_tilt/STAROWN-2026-10-29.json`

E250 took the book aggregate away and left one number standing: the star kept 90% of its −0.805
lag-1 while every other column lost 60–130%. E251 decomposes that residual with the same members,
rows and estimator — own turnover (D0), plus the star's own lagged `|move|` (D1), plus member age
(D2) — and decides each addition against the band its *own time-shuffle* produces. Read:
**b1 = −0.72504 → −0.73008 (vol, share −0.0070) → −0.73005 (vol+age, share −0.0069)**;
verdict `residual_is_own_turnover` in all four cells where a star exists (era 2023–24 −0.833,
2025–now −0.911, early-three −0.494). Volatility coefficients are all small and positive
(0.027–0.044) and the drop they cause (−0.00504) sits inside the shuffled-drop band
[−0.00888, +0.01343].

Three reusable findings:

(1) **A decomposition of a different number is not a decomposition.** The residual had to be
E250's published `b1_with_loo_aggregate`, and the two faces agree to 0.0 (both
−0.7250430277727764). Pin that identity to 1e−9 in the smoke leg, and factor the star rule into one
shared function (`e250.name_star`) rather than re-typing it: a copied rule that drifts answers
about a different sleeve while every leg stays green.

(2) **An addition must clear its own null.** "b1 fell when I added X" is not evidence unless the
drop beats the drops produced by shuffling X in time. A big share without a clear band is
`partially_explained`, never `explained` — the frozen verdict table encodes exactly that.

(3) **Inside one window, a member's age is a row index.** Age correlates 0.99998 with t, so its
fitted coefficient is a trend wearing a label: publish `age_identified: false` and read age only
cross-sectionally (n = members, descriptive, `decided: false`). A face that quietly reports the
trend as an age effect reports a mechanism it never measured.

Fixture lesson (one red leg, no re-burn): write the fixture's truth in the coordinates the design
actually uses. The first volatility fixture drove the outcome with `|0.7·dx_star + noise|` — the
absolute value of a small noise-dominated series — so the proxy link was too weak, the base
coefficient collapsed to 0.005 and the face correctly answered `residual_inside_shuffled_band`.
Rebuilding it around the star's exogenous `|dy_star|` (with turnover `0.6·|dy_star| + noise`)
reproduced the intended branch on the first re-run.

## E271 A paired gap belongs to the pair, not to the sheet in your hand (2026-10-30 r1357 bm-c)

The live-width ruler is a *paired* statistic: it pairs two live faces phase by phase, so its
number is a statement about the pair, never about one of them. Once the same number is printed
on two documents a reader can hold, the document that **is** one of the measured faces must be
told which face it is, or it will read the gap as "my book vs the reference book" -- a
difference between two other books, phrased as a defect of the sheet in hand.

Derive the role, never accept it from the caller: ``live_width_ruler(sheet="card")`` returns
``sheet_role`` in {measured_card, measured_ticket, None}, and downgrades to **unverified** when
the face prices a width the live faces no longer run. An unverified sheet is not allowed to
print the clean reading -- same family as E270 (a label that outlives its numbers).

Cross-document assertions surface spelling drift that single-document greps never can: the beta
page wrote the disagreement as ``%.2f`` while the new card block wrote ``%+.2f``. Assert the same
derived numbers on both pages (smoke leg E271) rather than a sentence on either.

Two consequences worth quoting: a reader of the card sheet learns that choosing between the two
sheets is a **volatility-budget** choice (the +0.09pp median is 4.4% of the engines' own 2.045pp
disagreement), and no sheet is allowed to say "the ruler is fine because we said so".

## E272 "The gap is small" and "this ruler could not have seen a gap this small" are different sentences (2026-10-30 r1358 bm-c)

WHEN: a comparison is published as a PAIRED statistic over a small, fixed sample -- here the card
width vs the ticket width, paired across the 20 decision origins of one rebalance cycle, per-phase
differences with sd 1.255pp. Every face in this family says "the gap is inside the engine
disagreement", which reads as "the widths are equivalent".

WRONG: quote the effect-size comparison alone. At 20 pairs and sd 1.255pp the smallest paired
difference this design can detect (two-sided 5%, 80% power, z=2.802) is **0.786pp**; the measured
gap is 0.09pp, i.e. **11% of its own floor**. Two different readings output the same sentence:
"the widths do not differ" and "this pairing cannot tell them apart". Only the second one is
supported, and the first one is the sentence an operator would act on.

RIGHT: publish the resolution beside the gap, derived from the numbers the face already carries
(mde = z*sd/sqrt(n)) and re-derived at every consumer, never copied off a stored field. Every
sheet prints it, and a sheet whose face publishes no dispersion says so instead of printing 0.00.
The consequence is a quotable rule for the family: a 20-phase cycle cannot resolve anything
smaller than ~0.8pp, so the card-vs-ticket question is answerable only as a volatility-budget
choice, never as a return difference -- and any future arm has to reach ~0.79pp before this
ruler may call it separated.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.py LIVE_RULE power_z/power_target + `resolution` block
(selftest 88->91 legs) ; scripts/etf_book_order.py `_resolution_md` + re-derived
resolution_pp/gap_over_resolution/below_own_resolution on both sheets (selftest 56->59) ; smoke
leg E272 recomputes the floor from the face's own sd and requires it on BOTH order documents.


## E273 A supply floor is only green over work that can be STARTED (2026-10-30 r1360 bm-c)

The v2.4 supply floor asked one question -- "is this ready row allowed to be taken by a live
machine?" -- and the answer was yes three times over on 2026-10-30 while python sat at 0.1%:
`ready=3 / floor=3 / breach=false`. The launcher, asked the same pool, said
`ghost_ready_no_shards` for all three. Two faces, one pool, two worlds.

What the three rows actually were, measured rather than assumed:

* `E272-ATLAS-COST0` -- deliverable already in git since r1163 (commit d0cdc4e44b). Re-ran
  today: every published number reproduced bit-for-bit, the only difference being
  `audit.workers` 4 -> 8. A determinism proof, not new supply.
* `E272-ATLAS-COST20` -- deliverable also in git since r1163, but the row could not be re-run
  at all: it was stocked with `--cost=20`, and the atlas takes cost as a FRACTION per side,
  so that is 2000%/side. The equity level crosses zero, `growth ** (1/yr)` returns a complex
  number, and the run dies 280 cells deep with `TypeError: float() argument must be a string
  or a real number, not 'complex'`.
* `E243-ADDITIVITY-OUT-OF-SAMPLE` -- never deliverable here: its runner imports
  `e235_charge_granularity`, which imports `e230_hysteresis_toll`, a module present in NO
  commit (only the digest landed), so it cannot start on any clean checkout. The publishing
  machine held the file untracked, which is exactly why the pool has to check the tree and
  not the author's disk.

The rule, in three separable questions that must not be collapsed: (1) may a live machine take
it, (2) can it be claimed and can it import, (3) does its deliverable already exist. Only (1)
existed. Adding (2) without touching (1) is the point: `ready_ignitable` keeps its history,
`ready_launchable` is published beside it, and the flag `supply_unstartable` fires when
ignitable>0 and launchable==0 under low py. Params with no unit validation are a separate
defect of the same shape -- a cost in the wrong unit is not a bigger cost, it is an unusable
argument, and it must be refused where it is entered (the atlas now rejects anything outside
0..0.05 with the message naming 0.0020 vs 20).

How to apply: when a gate publishes a count as "work available", say which executor answered
and what it would refuse. A count of rows is not a count of batches; a matched set of numbers
re-run to the last digit is a determinism check, not supply. One launcher rule, two readers:
the new selftest leg imports the launcher's own `unclaimable_ready_ids` and requires it to
name exactly the ids the audit calls unclaimable.

Pos: scripts/compute_audit.py `_unsharded`/`_unresolved_dep` + `ignitable_face`
ready_unclaimable/ready_launchable + flag supply_unstartable (selftest 51->57, incl. the
launcher-parity leg) ; Tools/autofill.py `unclaimable_ready_ids` (public face of the r1353
ghost rule) ; scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas.py cost unit guard (selftest 12->13) ;
results/runnable_pool.json three rows retired with typed evidence ;
research/digests/DIGEST-20261030-r1360-supply-floor-unstartable.md

## E274 A red card that counts debt as work stays red forever (2026-10-30 r1360 bm-c)

`results/watermark_red.json` -- the face that decides whether a round opens with a P0
escalation -- was written by a fresh ad-hoc closeout script in every window
(`_rNNNbmc_closeout.py`, one per round, kept in scratch/tmp). Every copy inherited the same
defect: a pool row that declares `blocked_by` but still carries `status="ready"` was counted
as "work waiting for you".

Measured, not assumed: 738 pool rows, and after the supply floor (E273) retired the three
unstartable rows, exactly ONE row was left in that state -- `E243-ADDITIVITY-OUT-OF-SAMPLE`,
blocked on `runner-import-missing:e230_hysteresis_toll` (a module present in no commit, so it
cannot start on any clean checkout). The red card's *entire* claim of available work was
therefore the one row no machine could start: autofill refused it every tick
(`ghost_ready_no_shards`), no round could consume it, and the card stayed red for as long as
it stayed blocked.

Law: the red card's input must be work that can be CONSUMED, not rows that exist. A row that
declares itself blocked is a named debt, and debt must be printed on its own line rather than
added to the work count. A permanently red gate ends one way only -- somebody relaxes the
assertion (the r1135 trap), which is why this is fixed at the reader with the debt still
visible (`pool_blocked_ids` + `blocked_reasons`), not by quieting the alarm.

Pos: Tools/watermark_red.py (single derived producer: write/show/selftest, 12 legs; lane
`board-clear-and-pool-empty` names the legal idle and prints the debt separately) ;
scripts/py_watermark.py `_pool_blocked_claimable` + `_pool_ready_claimable` skips declared
blockers + `pool_blocked_ids` on the probe record (selftest leg "a ready row that declares a
blocker is debt, not work"). Fixture lesson caught by the new leg itself: the selftest first
reused one filename per series, so a later fixture shadowed an earlier one and the reader
looked like it disagreed when only the fixture aliased itself (r1261 family).

## E275 An ordering judged against a band must be read as a ladder in the knob that moves both (2026-10-30 r1361 bm-c)

A grid's "config A beats config B" claim is usually published against a noise floor computed
from the same grid (here: between-cell spread vs median cell band). The trap is that the
*quantity being judged* and the *quantity judging it* can both move with a third thing -- the
toll -- and a one-arm reading cannot tell "the structure decays" from "the floor rises".

Measured on the ETF atlas across 5 toll rungs (0/5/10/20/40 bp per side, same 12 cells):
spread falls 6.92 -> 3.00pp with rank corr **-1.000** while the band barely moves (6.75 ->
6.47pp), crossing at **1.045 bp/side** -- one eighth of the 8bp toll the production card is
charged. The verdict flipped at exactly one rung (cost zero). Two by-products neither a single
arm nor a band-only reader could produce: the **fast cadence inverts** (+5.54pp at zero cost,
-1.32pp at 40bp/side, argmax migrating n8/R10 -> n8/R40), and the **width knob is the only
sign-stable direction** (+0.69 -> +1.56pp, rank corr +1.000) -- the exact inverse of the
one-arm headline that dismissed width as "inside the band". A strength ladder read the same
way (7 buffer strengths at one toll) produced one non-monotone crossing out of seven: a ladder
whose only crossing is not a dose is not a rule.

Law: (1) read every bracket as a ladder and publish the crossing, not just the endpoints;
(2) a rung must be produced by the face's own `audit` block, never by its file name, and a face
that moves two knobs is excluded rather than averaged in; (3) the only defensible claim from
such a ladder is usually directional -- sign stability across rungs is a measurement, magnitude
inside the band is not a quotable number.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_atlas_toll_ladder.py (selftest 32/32; rung membership off `audit`,
two-knob faces refused; crossing interpolated; `--check` recomputes the verdict from frozen
constants) + results/etf_book_atlas_toll_ladder.json (+ .md) + smoke leg that rebuilds every
rung's spread from the source arm faces + chain leg `etf_book_atlas_toll_ladder`.

## E276 A width sign measured inside one grid is not portable until it is read against a noise score (2026-10-30 r1362 bm-c)

E275 published exactly one stable direction: wider books earn more, +0.69 -> +1.56pp median CAGR
at rank corr +1.000 across five toll rungs. It was measured inside one grid on one panel. Re-asked
on the E198 18-name cross (widths 3/5/8/12, cadence 20, costs 0 and 8bp, 5 decision phases), the
sign flips: rank corr(width, CAGR) -1.000 at BOTH cost arms, widest beats narrowest in 0 of 5
paired phases, and narrow 3 names beats wide 12 names by 6.4pp/yr at the production toll (12.76%
vs 6.34%). Vol goes UP with width there (13.8% -> 18.5%) because the composite's negative-sign
factors (vol_60, intraday_range) mean widening adds the high-vol names -- so the flip is not a
"narrow book is riskier" artefact; Sharpe falls 1.06 -> 0.44 alongside.

Then the control that makes the reading usable: a SEEDED random score on the same panel, same
engine, same widths (20 seeds x 2 phases x 4 widths = 160 runs) earns MORE as the book widens
(1.25/1.76/2.85/3.04%, rank corr +1.000) while its vol FALLS (22.8% -> 20.5%). That is
diversification arithmetic on a rising panel, and it is the same direction E275 quoted -- so the
family's width sign is the sign a signal-free book has, and cannot be credited to the signal until
a null runs on the family panel. Real-minus-null decays monotonically 11.51/7.28/5.92/3.30pp:
the edge lives in the top few names.

Law: (1) any knob direction quoted from one grid on one panel is a naming until it is re-read on an
independent panel -- and "independent" means the ranks are recomputed there, so the instrument
differs too; (2) a width sign without a null is a volatility sign: read real and noise on the SAME
panel and publish the difference, per rung; (3) when the quoted family direction equals the noise
direction, the honest output is "needs its own null", never "confirmed"; (4) publish every cost
arm's state and let a disagreement become WIDTH_SIGN_ARM_DEPENDENT rather than rounding to the
friendlier arm, and refuse a rung that never traded (turnover 0 is not a measurement).

Pos: scripts/etf_book_width_cross.py (selftest 25/25; frozen spec/verdict rule; paired-by-phase
shares; null keeps the real eligibility mask; attribution separates arithmetic from score;
`--check` recomputes every arm state and rejects a zero-turnover rung) +
results/etf_book_width_cross.json (+ .md) + digest DIGEST-20261030-r1362-width-sign-cross-panel.md
+ smoke leg that rebuilds the arm states and the real-minus-null deltas from the published columns
+ chain leg `etf_book_width_cross`.
## E277 A card whose deliverable was committed before the card was registered is supply on paper (2026-10-30 r1168 bm-a)

Three open pool cards on the board were phantom supply, and the audit tool had been saying so
for hours without anyone able to act on it: its verdict was `AMBIGUOUS` because the cards
carried approximate stamps (`2026-10-30T13:4x+08:00`). An unparsable stamp meant the
pre/post-entry question could never be answered, and an unanswerable question never closes a
card -- so `pool_ready 3` read as three units of work while every one of their deliverables was
already committed (E243's face 5.4h before its card, E272-COST0 1.6h before, E272-COST20 6.4h
before). A supply floor red over nothing fails the same way a green over nothing does: the
number cannot be believed in either direction.

Two laws. (1) **A file's mtime is not provenance.** A checkout rewrites every mtime at once
(this round's pull stamped eleven atlas faces with the same 19:04 minute), so a face that looks
"newer than the card" may only have been restored. Git commit times answer the same question
durably and survive any checkout: provenance asks git first and falls back to mtime only where
git cannot answer. (2) **A card the audit cannot adjudicate must name who can decide it, not sit
in AMBIGUOUS.** The cure was not to loosen the rule but to give it a second source stamp: when
`entered_at` is unusable, compare the deliverable's commit time against the commit that
introduced the card's id into the pool file; face-committed-earlier reads `BEFORE_ENTRY_GIT` =
duplicate supply, closable with a close_note that says nothing was burned. Fail-closed
everywhere else: a card git has never seen stays `AMBIGUOUS` (selftest L23), and a pre-existing
face with a *parsable* stamp is still never auto-closed.

By-product worth keeping: the same rule makes a zero-toll cost arm readable. Adding the 0bp rung
to the arms face published `NOT-SEPARABLE` with `CADENCE_BAND_SURVIVES_THE_TOLL`; the plan to
judge the ordering across rungs was DELETED on the spot, because bm-c r1361's
`etf_book_atlas_toll_ladder` already publishes that ladder (crossing 1.045bp) with a chain leg
and a smoke leg. A second face saying the same sentence is the r1290 defect, not a second
opinion -- the arms face now points a reader at the ladder instead of restating it.

Pos: Tools/pool_deliverable_audit.py (selftest 24/24: L21 stamps decide newer/older, L22 git
answers for a tracked face and refuses an untracked one, L23 an unregistered card stays
ambiguous, L24 a non-repo fixture cannot be closed) + `duplicate_supply` block in
results/pool_deliverable_audit.json + the three closed cards' close_note + smoke leg
"pool: a card whose deliverable already existed is not supply".

## E278 A width sign belongs to a (panel, leverage rule) pair; a null answers only the panel half (2026-10-30 r1169 bm-a)

E276 asked the right question and answered it on the E198 18-name cross, closing with the demand
that the family's width direction needs its own null on its own panel. Running that null on the
production panel (5,270 bars x 1,504 equity/commodity/convertible names, repaired prints, same
builder as the card, cadence 20, 10bp, no vol target) delivers three separable facts.

(1) **The width direction on this panel is not the noise direction.** Real rank corr(width, CAGR)
= -0.80 (narrower earns more: 15.58 / 15.73 / 15.15 / 14.21% at widths 3/5/8/12) while six seeded
random score sets on the SAME eligibility mask give +0.40 (0.77 / 0.30 / 0.48 / 1.77%). So the
decay is a property of the score, not diversification arithmetic on a rising panel -- the opposite
verdict to E276's cross-panel read, which is exactly why the null has to run where the number lives.

(2) **Publish the null's own spread, or the headline is a fiction of the estimator.** Per-seed
ranges are wide (-6.5..+3.4% at width 3) while real sits 10.5-13.5pp above the BEST draw; the gap
is decisive but the null's *trend* (rho +0.4) is not a measurement at six seeds. A face that prints
real-minus-median only makes both look equally precise.

(3) **The sign is also rule-bound, and that half survives this face.** The ticket already publishes
the same two widths under two leverage rules: beta-scaled says 12 names cost 1.53pp vs 5, vol-target
says 12 names gain 0.24pp -- a disagreement smaller than the 2.045pp engine disagreement the ticket
itself declares. So "narrow beats wide" is a statement about (panel, rule), and a width question
asked without naming the rule is not answered by any amount of panel evidence.

Law: a knob direction needs (a) its own null on the panel it was measured on and (b) the leverage
rule named, because the sign can survive (a) and still flip on (b); publish the null's per-seed range
and quote the gap to its best draw, not to its median.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_width_null_production.py (selftest 14/14: spearman/ties/constant refusal,
same-sign vs differing-sign verdicts, NOT-ASSESSED on a None or non-finite rung, gap column is
real-minus-null, the null keeps the eligibility mask and replays by seed, and the cross-rule reader
names a disagreeing ticket instead of averaging it) + results/etf_book_width_null_production.json
(+ .md) + chain leg `etf_book_width_null_production` + smoke leg that recomputes the verdict from
the published columns.


## E279 A cap printed on one face and the money printed on another are never compared by construction (2026-10-30 r1170 bm-a)

The market clock flipped to RED for the first time since the guard went `enforce`, so the CEO
page started printing "RED caps new risk at 20%" directly above six paper accounts holding
4.6%..76.8% of equity. Both numbers were correct and neither face could see the other: the cap
comes from the clock's ladder, the book comes from the paper engine, and no code path joined
them. A reader had to choose between "the guard is broken" and "the cap means something
narrower" -- and the truthful answer (the cap gates *new entries*; the accounts predate the
flip) was written nowhere.

Three laws. (1) **A published limit and a published book are two faces until something
subtracts them.** Wire the comparison as a face of its own (results/regime_cap_vs_book.json +
chain leg + smoke leg that recomputes the verdict from the published rows), never as a sentence
inside one of the two producers. (2) **The comparison must know which accounts are not governed
by that cap.** The ETF book carries its own rule (beta target x vol target, r1167/r1169);
grading it against the ladder manufactures a breach the same way mixing cost arms manufactures
an edge. It is printed in the same table as `own_rule` and excluded from the verdict.
(3) **A disagreement between cap sources is reported, not averaged** -- the ladder is imported
from the page that prints it (`ceo_live_usage.LADDER`), and a second source that differs yields
`CAP_SOURCES_DISAGREE` in the sentence instead of a silent blend.

Reading at cutoff 2026-10-29: cap 0.20 from both sources (clock ladder + engine
`risk_regime.position_cap`), 3 of 6 ladder-governed accounts above it, widest COMPOSITE-CE-01
76.8% (56.8pp over, 3.84x). Exploration only: it books no position and changes no rung.
## E281 A correction that only the artifact's own readers can see is not a disclosure (2026-10-30 r1371 bm-c; renumbered from E279 -- bm-a r1170 landed E279 on origin first)
The forward ledger has been allowed since r1366 to move a mark once, for a bar published while its
panel was still filling: it keeps the published value, the settled value, the delta and the run that
did it, in 
estatements and on each bar. That record satisfied the settle gate, satisfied the audit
face, and told the operator nothing -- the trader holds ORDER-*.md, which quoted 11.19% next to a
NAV whose history had silently moved, and the only way to learn that was to open LEDGER.json.
(1) **A correction has to be shipped by every face that quotes the corrected series.** Recording it
in the artifact is the wrong side of the boundary: the artifact is what the *producers* read. The
test is mechanical -- for each human-facing face that prints the number, is the notice printed on
that same file? Here the answer is now yes on both sheets (beta ticket + executable card ticket),
and the row prints both values so the reader can see which one the sheet now uses.
(2) **Missing input must not read as "nothing happened".** Three states, never two: unreadable,
readable-and-empty, readable-with-notices. A reader that returns [] on a missing or corrupt ledger
turns a deleted file into a clean bill of health -- the same shape as the r1288 fallback that
silently replaced the main path.
(3) **"Checked and clean" has to be a printed sentence, not a missing section.** Absence of a notice
block is indistinguishable from a face that never looked. So the sheet says it in words ("已核验：6
根 bar 中没有任何一根在定格之后被改述") and names which source it checked (top-level list vs the
per-bar 
estated_at marks), because ledgers written before the list existed carry the marks only.
Law: a correction record is disclosed when the face that quotes the series prints it, states the
three-state outcome in words, and names the source it read; a correction that lives only where the
producer reads is still a silent history change.
Pos: scripts/etf_book_order.py (_ledger_restatements + 
estatement_md, selftest 65->69/69: a
restated bar reaches the sheet with both values, a bars-only older ledger still reaches it, an
unreadable ledger prints "cannot verify" instead of "none", a clean ledger is stated as a checked
fact) + both ORDER sheets + smoke leg product: a bar restated after settling reaches the ticket's
own ledger line that recomputes the notice from LEDGER.json rather than trusting the sentence.
## E280 A staleness gate that asks the filesystem instead of the data will call a stale face hand-edited (2026-10-30 r1371 bm-c)
The arms gate (`scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.py --check`) has carried two red states since
r1346: *stale* (a source advanced, so the chain pays the declared refresh) and *inconsistent*
(sources unmoved, so a re-derive would launder a hand edit). Choosing between them used mtime --
"is any source file newer than the face". A chain leg then went red on
`.arms.cost0.audit.workers (4 vs 8)`, which is genuine staleness: the cost0 arm face was
regenerated at 14:05, while the published arms face was restored by a checkout at 20:46 -- which
handed the OLD content a NEW mtime. The gate concluded "no source moved" and reported an edit.
(1) **mtime cannot answer "did the source move".** Every checkout, clone, rebase or file restore
rewrites mtimes in arbitrary order, so "source older than face" is fully compatible with "source
content is newer". The probe measured the filesystem, not the data.
(2) **The face already carries the evidence.** `build()` copies each source's `audit` block
verbatim into the face. A differing copied leaf means the source now says something different
from what the face recorded about it -- content-level proof that the source moved, at any clock.
Every copied leaf intact while a computed number differs means no source moved and the edit is
somebody's hand. The split needs no timestamp at all, and it is *stronger* than the old test
(every copy matching is positive evidence, not merely absence of a newer mtime).
(3) **The leg has to perturb the class it claims to classify.** The first version of the new
selftest leg failed for a reason worth the line: `_diff` emits paths with a leading dot
(`.arms.cost0.audit.workers ...`), so the prefix test classified every leaf as derived and the
face read "inconsistent". A leg that only asserted the right answer on the right face would have
passed while the classifier was wrong.
Law: a staleness verdict must come from content the face records about its source (a copied field,
a digest), never from file timestamps; and the leg proving it must move exactly the field class it
distinguishes -- both directions, one at a time.
Pos: `scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.py` (`_is_copied` / `_classify_drift` + `check()`,
selftest 100->102 legs: copy-drift reads stale with the face deliberately NEWER than its sources;
copy-drift plus a moved verdict still reads inconsistent) + refreshed
`results/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.json` (one line changed -- the worker count; every published
reading identical) + digest `DIGEST-20261030-r1371-copy-drift-vs-mtime.md`.

## E282 A gate and its four siblings: sweep a defect family by the rule, not by the report (2026-10-30 r1372 bm-c)
r1371 fixed the arms gate's staleness split and left the same decision in five more places -- the
floor, width, live-width and readback gates in the same module plus the null-rule gate, all still
asking mtime "is any source newer than the face". That is the shape a family defect takes: the
report names the one site that went red, and the siblings keep passing because nothing has yet
perturbed them. This round replaced the clock in all five with the two content signals, and the
sweep immediately found a live false alarm nobody had noticed.
(1) **Two signals, both clock-free.** (a) a differing leaf that `build()` copies verbatim from the
source means the source was regenerated; (b) a source face priced on a *later bar* than this one
means it advanced. Everything a checkout can rewrite is excluded from both.
(2) **A cutoff comparison is only safe between two faces that mean the same thing by it.** The
first version compared the live-width face's cutoff (the atlas grid's bar) against
`results/etf_book_order/latest.json`, whose `as_of` is the date the ticket was *written* -- and the
gate went permanently stale against a ticket that re-derives to the same bytes. Same family as the
r1292 unit mix-up, caught only because a pre-existing selftest leg went red. Narrow the reader:
`evidence_cutoff` only, top level or inside `baseline`; nothing else is a priced bar.
(3) **A sweep needs a leg that proves the clock is not consulted, in both directions.** A hand edit
under an OLD clock must stay inconsistent (the mtime rule called it stale); a face priced on an
older bar must go stale even when it is the newest file on disk (the mtime rule called it fresh).
Proving only one direction leaves the old path reachable in the other.
Law: when a defect is found in one gate, re-read every gate of the same kind before closing the
round; and when staleness is decided by a date, only compare dates that the same face would have
written -- a written-on date and a priced-on bar are different quantities.
Pos: scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.py (_cutoff_of / _source_advanced / _source_moved, used by
check/floor_check/width_check/live_width_check/readback_check; selftest 102->109) +
scripts/etf_book_rule_null.py (same rule, its own copy of the read) + digest
DIGEST-20261030-r1372-clock-free-staleness-sweep.md. All five gates green on the 2026-10-29 bar.
## E284 A source can be regenerated on the bar it already carries, so a date comparison cannot decide "refresh me" vs "someone edited me" (2026-10-30 r1373 bm-c)

`etf_book_rule_null.check()` split two red states by one signal: is the source (the arms face)
priced on a LATER bar? The arms face is re-derived on the SAME bar several times a day (r1361
regenerated it at 14:05 and 14:20 on 2026-10-29 while the bar stayed put), and then the null
face's numbers move -- every label frequency is simulated from those rows -- while the cutoff
does not move at all. The gate reported `inconsistent` (rc=1, "hand-edited, do not re-derive"),
which under its own contract must stay red forever; the truth was `stale` (rc=2, "source moved,
refresh me"). The plausible wrong fix under pressure is to re-derive it anyway, which is exactly
the laundering the split exists to prevent.

Rule: to tell "my source moved" from "I was edited", compare **the inputs this face consumed**,
not a clock and not a date. `source_digest()` hashes the bar, every cell's band and each arm's own
rows and verdict (canonical JSON, sha256); `build()` publishes it; `check()` re-hashes the source
and answers one question. Same hash = no source moved = rc1. Different hash, absent hash, or a
missing source file = rc2, and none of those three may crash the gate. The date comparison is
demoted to a second signal and IMPORTED (`_source_advanced`) rather than re-implemented, closing
the duplicate-read debt r1372 left on the page.

Selftest 10 -> 14 legs; the load-bearing one rebuilds the null face against a perturbed band and
then points the gate back at the real source, demanding rc=2 -- the case the old gate could not
see. Published face refreshed: +4 lines, every reading byte-identical
(REJECT_IS_THE_DEFAULT, REJECT 96.48% under the null).

Pos: scripts/etf_book_rule_null.py (source_digest / check / selftest L6) + results/etf_book_rule_null.json
+ digest DIGEST-20261030-r1373-null-gate-source-digest.md.

## E285 Quote a corrected count where the reader does the counting, not where a writer is handy (2026-10-31 r1173 bm-a; renumbered from E284 -- bm-c r1373 landed E284 on origin first, r1349 law)
The bet-count face (r1172) answered a real misreading -- "five tickers look like five bets" -- but
its only consumer was the executable ticket's limitations block. The reader who forms that belief
is looking at the live-book page's order table, where five rows sit under a total, and that page
said nothing. A correction published anywhere other than the place the wrong belief is formed
keeps the belief alive, however many machines can recompute it.
(1) **Pick the consumer by where the misreading happens, not by where the plumbing is easiest.**
The ticket was the cheap wire (same script family, same run); the page was the right wire. This
round added one section to the live-book page and no new numbers -- the two faces had already
agreed for a round.
(2) **Quote through the reader, then assert the quote.** The page calls
`etf_book_bets_face.read_published()`; the smoke leg re-reads the published artifact and demands
the sentence appear in the rendered markdown, so a page that quietly stops quoting reads red. It
resolves the page path from `docs/live_book/latest.json` -- the first version rebuilt the filename
from the date and failed on that alone (262/263), which is the same class of defect it hunts.
(3) **Absent must not degrade into the myth.** The failure mode of a missing artifact here is not
a blank line, it is "5 names = 5 bets"; the page prints the honest-absence line instead of a
count, and the selftest has a branch asserting exactly that when the artifact is missing.
(4) **Counts printed on the page come from the artifact, formatted -- never recomputed.** The
selftest leg compares the rendered numbers against the artifact's own fields, so a second
computation cannot drift in.
