Pos: scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py (bets_face / render_md bet-count section / selftest 49 legs incl.
L48-L50) + smoke leg "product: ETF live-book page says how many independent bets the holdings are"
+ docs/live_book/LIVE-BOOK-20261031.md.

## E286 A date can say which bar a face is on, and nothing about whether the world it was priced against still exists (2026-10-31 r1374 bm-c)

r1372 swept the gates that asked the clock whether their source had moved; r1373 hashed the last
one. The same family has a second form, and it is the one a research face actually falls into: a
gate comparing `evidence_cutoff` with the card's cutoff. That comparison answers "was I priced
before the card", never "did the card move under me on the bar we share" -- a config edit, a
repaired print or a re-derived cell leaves both dates identical and every number the face graded
against different.

The tell is that such a face usually ALREADY records what it graded against.
`etf_book_buffer_adoption` and `etf_book_hysteresis` both store `control.published` and
`published_turnover`, so the second half of the question is answerable at check time with no engine
run; the rule now lives once, as `etf_book_production.control_moved()`, and is imported by both (a
copy of a rule is the next defect). The third state matters as much as the first two: card, cell or
recorded control absent returns `None` and the gate prints `control re-read unavailable` -- an
unreadable input is not a clean one.

The stronger instance was not a gate but a CACHE: `etf_book_beta_menu` keyed its stress replay on
the priced bar, while its own docstring asserted "the answer only moves when the panel or the ticket
does". A flip repair rewrites the worst N-day window without moving today's date, and the tier k
moves without the panel moving at all, so the replay handed back numbers for a panel that no longer
existed. `stress_inputs_digest()` now keys the replay on bar + capital + windows + ticket scale + a
hash of the panel values, and a cache file that predates the digest is a MISS rather than a hit.

Selftests 35/35 (hysteresis, +3 legs), 37/37 (buffer adoption, +4 legs), 27/27 (beta menu, 30 -> 32
chk sites) including "a same-bar panel repair is recomputed, never replayed". Both live gates remain
OK on the current bar: a correct rule finds nothing to report in an unchanged world.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_production.py (control_moved) + scripts/etf_book_buffer_adoption.py +
scripts/etf_book_hysteresis.py + scripts/etf_book_beta_menu.py (stress_inputs_digest)
+ digest DIGEST-20261031-r1374-date-only-gates.md.

## E287 A risk rule priced on a book whose own worst loss is shallower than the trigger is not a safety net, it is an untested branch (2026-10-31 r1174 bm-a)

The live book published what it expects to earn and how deep it has fallen, and nothing about what
the operator should do when it falls. The obvious answer -- a pre-committed stop at 8%/10%/15% --
turns out to be three empty branches: over 1,652 bars and four decision calendars the book's own
worst drawdown is -6.8..-6.9%, because the beta overlay (k = 0.20/beta) already de-levers. A
threshold above the book's realised risk envelope never fires, so it costs nothing and protects
nothing, and any test of it returns exactly zero. The levels that DO fire sit below the envelope: at
5% the switch cuts CAGR by 5.3pp for 0.8pp of shallower drawdown, and proportional de-leverage buys
1.1-2.1pp of drawdown for 1.0-3.1pp of return at every threshold tested.
(1) **Print the envelope beside the rule.** A drawdown-rule face that does not state its baseline's
own max drawdown cannot distinguish "the rule is good" from "the rule never fired"; INERT must be a
first-class verdict, not a silent pass.
(2) **Two de-risking mechanisms on the same losses pay the protection twice.** The book already
scales exposure continuously; a second mechanism reacting to the same drawdown charges fees on every
re-arm to buy risk it is already rid of.
(3) **Path test before the verdict, identity control before the cells.** Four schedules started
0/5/10/15 bars into history each re-price the rule on its own decision sequence, and the no-guard arm
must reproduce the published overlay bit for bit (max abs diff 0.0) or the face is measuring its own
arithmetic.
How: when a risk overlay is proposed, first ask where the instrument's worst loss sits relative to
the trigger, then charge it for the trades it moves, then check whether another rule already handles
the same risk.
Pos: scripts/etf_book_drawdown_guard.py (selftest 12/12, run/check modes) +
results/etf_book_drawdown_guard.json + research/ETF_BOOK_DRAWDOWN_GUARD.md. Verdict REJECT
(8 arms x 4 calendars, 0 adopted; 3 arms INERT at 8/10/15%).

## E288 A return statistic that no market move can explain is a data defect, and the arithmetic the face already prints is the detector (2026-10-31 r1175 bm-a)

The book published a worst day of -4.70% (2025-07-07) while 510300 moved -0.02% that day.
A book built to a 0.20 beta cannot lose 4.7% on a flat index day -- the magnitude was
impossible, not merely surprising. The cause was a unit-change print (512800 halves from
1.777 to 0.894 and never returns) read as a return.

Two transferable rules:

(1) **A face that only sees returns cannot see a print.** Nothing downstream of a price
panel can tell a -50% print from a -50% crash, so the defect has to be caught by a
magnitude argument made against the thing the number is supposed to track. Cheap form:
multiply the beta the rule already trades to by the worst move of the market it is
measured against, and compare. If the book's worst loss is deeper than that, stop and
look at the prices before publishing.

(2) **The cheapest sound repair measurement is the same rule twice on the same dates.**
One call to the published 
epair_flips, one re-run of capture(), and the delta is the
contamination: +1.17pp of annual return, +1.13pp of drawdown, +0.286 of Sharpe on the full
history, and a worst day that more than halves (-4.70% → -2.88%). Freeze the materiality
band before the run (0.50pp CAGR / 0.50pp drawdown / 0.10 Sharpe) and let the gate
recompute the verdict, or the finding becomes an opinion about how much is too much.

(3) **Measure the contamination, do not absorb it.** Repairing the production panel moves
every published number of the book at once; doing it in the same round that discovered
the size would hide which face changed. Publish the delta, name the consumers that still
read raw prices (the CEO page's stress block replays today's weights over history), and
let the repair be one deliberate, all-at-once decision.

How: when a published risk statistic looks large, bound it from above with the exposure
the rule claims, then compare the same rule on repaired prices. A magnitude that cannot
be produced by the market is a property of the data, never of the strategy.
Pos: scripts/etf_book_shock_day.py (selftest 19/19) + scripts/etf_book_flip_contamination.py
(selftest 15/15) + results/etf_book_shock_day.json + results/etf_book_flip_contamination.json
+ research/ETF_BOOK_SHOCK_DAY.md + research/ETF_BOOK_FLIP_CONTAMINATION.md. Verdict: shock
beta MATCHED (0.12 full / 0.21 recent); contamination MATERIAL (168 prints).

## E289 A defect fixed in one copy is still live in the other: the CEO page kept its own replay cache keyed on the date (2026-10-31 r1375 bm-c)

r1374 fixed the per-tier stress replay cache and wrote the rule down as `stress_inputs_digest`. The
same pattern lived one file away: `etf_book_ceo_page.build_stress` replays today's weights over the
panel and caches the answer in its OWN directory keyed on the priced bar, guarding the hit with a
capital equality check. That guard answers "was this priced with the same money", never "with the
same book, on the same panel". Both inputs move while the date does not: a flip repair inside the
panel rewrites the worst N-day window on an unchanged bar, and a re-priced ticket rescales the
weights on the very same bar. A reader hitting either would be quoted last world's drawdown with a
today date beside it.

The fix is the same shape, and it is deliberately NOT a second implementation: `panel_fingerprint()`
now hashes the panel's values (shape + hash_pandas_object) in the CEO page, and
`etf_book_beta_menu.stress_inputs_digest` imports it instead of hashing the panel itself. One rule,
two call sites -- the copy is what the next defect grows from.

Two smaller lessons rode along. First, a display string must never be able to abort a run:
`build_stress` now resolves the panel BEFORE the cache probe (so the probe can hash what it would
use), and its error path still returns an honest error dict rather than raising. Second, the
selftest itself was red for weeks in a way no one had harvested: `cash_yield` formatted its panel
path with a bare os.path.relpath, which raises ValueError across drives -- and the selftest pins
fixtures under %TEMP% (C:) while the repo sits on K:, so `etf_book_ceo_page.py selftest` died at
L1473 before printing a count. `_rel()` now falls back to the absolute path (same family as
pool_deliverable_audit._under in r1371). The suite reads 49/49 PASS after the fix, 52/52 with the
three new cache legs.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py (panel_fingerprint / stress_cache_digest / build_stress /
_rel, selftest L51-L53) + scripts/etf_book_beta_menu.py (panel_fingerprint import)
+ digest DIGEST-20261031-r1375-second-copy-cache.md.

## E290 Two sheets of paper, two price panels: the repair has to be declared where the reader trades (2026-10-31 r1176 bm-a)

E288 measured what the flip prints do to the book and closed with a disclosure naming the CEO page's
stress block as "the next consumer face to fix". That sentence was an inference, not a measurement,
and it is wrong: `etf_book_forward.build_inputs` -- the loader both the CEO page and the forward
ledger use -- already calls `repair_flips` before it returns anything. Measured on the live panel:
1504 columns, 5270 bars, **zero** prints beyond 35% (max |r| 0.3447), so the page's own worst window
(-3.60% on 2025-04-07) is a market day and always was. The face now prints that proof instead of
asserting it, and it will print the opposite the moment a flip enters the panel.

The measurement did find the real hole one layer sideways. `etf_book_order.run()` builds its panel
with `F.build_panel()` and never repairs it: **164 flip prints on 155 names** inside its own
1503-name universe. So the production card (`etf_book_production`, repairs at line 688) and the
executable ticket measure the same book on two different price panels, and the ticket is the one the
operator trades from. No single number can be compared across them, and nobody noticed because both
faces print a CAGR in the same format.

Three transferable rules. (1) "Which panel did this number come from" is a first-class disclosure,
not a footnote: the sheet now carries `price_basis` (panel kind, flip threshold, flip count, symbols)
and prints it in its honesty section, so the reader learns it where the trade happens. (2) A repair
applied in one consumer and not another is worse than no repair, because it manufactures an apparent
disagreement between two faces of one book. (3) Correcting a prior round's disclosure costs one cheap
measurement and buys the right to keep trusting the rest of it -- the stress block needed twelve
lines and four selftest legs to answer a question a sentence had already answered wrongly.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py (clean_panel / contamination_block / book_returns, selftest
L27b-L27e) + scripts/etf_book_order.py (price_basis, selftest leg46a-c, payload + sheet line)
+ results/etf_book_order/ORDER-2026-10-30.md + docs/live_book/LIVE-BOOK-20261031.md.


## E291 A sweep you keep deferring is not a sweep until it has a count, and the count depends on three judgements (2026-10-31 r1376 bm-c)

r1374 and r1375 each closed with the same sentence: the rule was applied inside the etf_book family,
the rest of the repo was never asked. Twice-deferred work is work nobody owns, so it was replaced
with a number: 698 files scanned, 16 places that actually decide whether to reuse a live value,
13 of them serving on a key with no digest in it, 1 fully digested, 2 that raise on mismatch and so
cannot serve a stale number at all.

Three judgements moved that number and each one is a reusable rule. (1) NAMES ONLY: matching the word
"cache" in docstrings/comments turned 21 real decisions into 194 rows of prose, and the row count is
what a reader uses to decide whether the audit is worth reading. (2) IMPORT TABLE IS NOT AN AST Name:
`import hashlib` creates no Name node, so a module that hashes something ELSE was reported as
"no digest anywhere" -- the exact wrong sentence about a wrong file; the half-covered case is now its
own verdict with its own leg, because it is precisely r1375's defect shape (a digest existed, one
cache used it). (3) ASK WHAT HAPPENS ON MISMATCH: a gate that raises cannot serve a stale value while
a face that returns the cached payload is the whole family -- collapsing both into one count produces
a list where most rows are harmless.

One pit was worth more than the report: six working files were silently outside the sweep because
they start with a UTF-8 BOM and `ast.parse` rejects U+FEFF at line 1. Reading utf-8-sig fixed it. The
general rule is the r1375 relpath lesson in a new axis -- a scanner that cannot read a file must never
report it as clean, and here it is only findable because the row said `unreadable` instead of being
dropped.

Pos: Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py (selftest 14/14) + results/live_value_cache_audit.json + smoke
leg "audit: which caches serve a live value on a key with no digest" + digest
DIGEST-20261031-r1376-cache-sweep.md.

## E292 A checkpoint that asks "same rule" is not asking "same world", and a scanner that reads keys
cannot see a digest passed as an argument (2026-10-31 r1377 bm-c)

r1376's sweep ended with one name: `alloc_backtest.run_cell_faces` — the only row in the list that both
serves a value someone acts on (the ALLOC-P1..P6 cell faces) and already carries digest machinery
elsewhere in its module. Read one function and the answer is immediate: `config_sha` hashed
`(weights, mode, p2, capital, start, cutoff)` and the panel the cell was computed from — prices,
amounts, the repo-rate cash leg — never entered the key. Every way a panel moves while the rule stands
still (a quote-flip repair inside the window, a late bar, a repaired rate print) left the old cell
serving. Rule identity and data identity are two questions; a checkpoint that answers only the first is
a cache that cannot notice the world under it changing.

Two reusable rules came out of fixing it.

(1) **A cell must publish the digest it was keyed on, and a pre-digest record is a MISS.** Not "resume,
this is probably fine" — unreadable/absent evidence is never clean. The cost is a one-time recompute of
the live cells on their lane owner, which is what having been wrong costs; the alternative is a cache
whose only failure mode is silence.

(2) **When you make a cache safe by passing a digest into the reader, a keys-only scanner will still
call it unsafe.** r1376's audit re-ran after the change and the row did not move, because it looked for
`cached.get("something_sha")` and this face writes `cached = load_valid_cell(name, sha, data_sha)`. A
scanner that under-reports is worse than no scanner: it regenerates the same work every round. The fix
is a third evidence class (`digest_argument_to_cache_read`) that is *published with the names it saw*
and deliberately kept OUT of the "digested" bucket — a name in a call is a clue, not a proof, and the
whole point of the sweep was that the copy-in-name evidence is what lies. Its headline keeps the
conservative total and prints the split.

The general form: a sweep that produces a reading list must also be able to see the fix, or the next
round will re-open a closed row.

Pos: scripts/alloc_backtest.py (panel_sha / config_sha(data_sha=) / load_valid_cell, selftest 21 -> 26,
F9-a..e) + Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py (digest_argument_to_cache_read, selftest 14 -> 19, L15-L19) +
results/live_value_cache_audit.json + 2 smoke legs + digest DIGEST-20261031-r1377-panel-digest-checkpoint.md.
## E293 One book, two flip counts: a census total without its denominator reads as drift, and the face that feeds the identity check can still be ranking on raw prices (2026-10-31 r1177 bm-a)

The r1177 arrival fixed the right defect -- the executable sheet now repairs its panel like the card
already did -- and then printed the two faces side by side: the ticket says **164 flip prints on 155
names**, the card says **168 on 158**. Same rule, same library, same day, two different totals. Both
are truthful: the card repairs the whole 1,613-column panel before it classifies, the ticket censuses
only the 1,503 columns it ranks. Nothing on either sheet said which universe its total was counted on,
so a reader comparing them can only conclude that the arithmetic drifted. Adding the panel width to
the shared sentence (`price_basis_line(..., columns=)`) turns an apparent contradiction into a
visible universe difference -- and the smoke leg now fails when the two totals differ on the *same*
width, which is the only case where it really is a drift.

The bigger find was one layer upstream, and it was already red: the carry-split identity leg
("base cell == the ticket's own headline") had started failing at 11.19% vs 12.78%. The cause was not
the split arithmetic but `etf_book_return_regime.build()` -- the third face of this book -- still
calling `F.build_panel()` and ranking on unrepaired prints, so its base cell priced a different world
than the ticket it is supposed to reproduce. Repairing it moved the cell to 12.78% and
`identity matched=True`. Two rules transfer: (1) **when two faces print the same statistic, print the
denominator too** -- a total without its universe cannot be compared, and the reader will read the
difference as sloppiness; (2) **a contradiction between two published numbers is a lead, not a
tie-break**: ask which panel each sits on before assuming either is wrong, and fix the face that is
still on the old panel rather than the one it is being compared against.

Pos: `scripts/etf_equity_alpha.py` `price_basis_line(columns=)` / `scripts/etf_book_return_regime.py`
`build()` / `smoke_test.py` (r1177 leg: width on both faces + same-panel-different-total is red).

## E294 A fingerprint that covers what a face PRINTS is not a certificate for what it CHOSE (2026-10-31 r1179 bm-a)

r1178 made the three faces of this book publish a panel digest, and the digest was built from
close/open only. The book's universe is not chosen on close: it is filtered on 20-day turnover.
So two faces could agree to the bit on every price and still rank different names off different
amount panels -- "we priced one world" would stay a claim one field away from being false. The fix
is not a bigger hash but a NAMED one: `panel_digest(close, openp, amt)` labels each block in the
stream and reports `blocks`, and every face now says which blocks it covered (ticket/card/ledger
all read `close/open/amount`, digest `a1a42aee64453839` over 5,270 bars x 1,613 symbols).
The atlas arm face carries the same stamp for the same reason: the arms audit (E256/E259) compares
faces produced hours apart, and without a stamp a band measured on one panel can sit beside a band
measured on another and read as a rule effect -- the E293 class one level up.
Two negative tests make it falsifiable rather than decorative: an amount-only perturbation must
move the digest, and a two-block digest must never equal a three-block one.

Pos: scripts/etf_equity_alpha.py `panel_digest(amt=)` / scripts/etf_book_{production,order,forward}.py
/ scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas.py `payload["panel"]` / smoke_test.py r1179 leg (blocks named, amount
moves the digest, two-block != three-block).


## E295 A stamp that nobody consumes is still a claim: make the consumer withhold the label (2026-10-31 r1180 bm-a)

E294 taught every atlas arm face to say which panel it priced. It did not teach the AUDIT that
compares them to do anything with the answer -- the stamp was published and read by nobody, and
the family kept publishing ten per-cell verdicts whose arithmetic (arm minus baseline, same cell)
is only a measurement inside one panel. Measured here: all eleven faces on disk carry no stamp at
all (they predate r1179), so the honest reading of the whole family is not "buffer k=0.05 REJECT"
but "one world unproven". The cure belongs at the consumer, and it distinguishes three states
rather than two: MIXED_WORLDS (a delta across panels is a world difference wearing a rule-effect
label -> verdict withheld), UNSTAMPED (predates the stamp -> unproven, not disproven, also
withheld), ONE_WORLD (labels publish). A withheld label is not deleted -- it travels as
`verdict_if_coherent` beside its reason, so the reading stays on the record and returns as a
measurement the moment the arms are rebuilt onto one panel.
The second half of the same lesson: a downstream face that asks "what does this RULE say under a
null" must keep reading the rule's own answer (`verdict_if_coherent`), or every arm silently
scores 0.0 against the null and the null looks decisive. Publication status is a separate column
from the label, never a replacement for it.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_phase_atlas_arms.py `world_of`/`coherence`/`withhold_verdicts` (+7 selftest
legs, 109 -> 117) / scripts/etf_book_rule_null.py `label_of` / pool unit
ATLAS-ARMS-ONEWORLD-20261031-ALL (the rebuild that returns the labels).

## E296 A gate that pins the STAMP of a panel is not pinning the panel, and a memo is not a cache (2026-10-31 r1378 bm-c)

Two questions from one round of reading a list.

First. `cn_mkneutral_p1` gates its panel on the asset meta stamp, the last bar, the shape and a
universe re-derive -- four facts about the FILE. A repaired print inside the window moves none of
them (the E289 flip class rewrites a price and keeps the date), so the eleven cell checkpoints and
the null/sobol shard pools were still resumable against a world that no longer existed. The fix is
the r1377 rule one lane over: `panel_digest(P)` over the numbers `simulate()`/`null_draw()`
consume (joint calendar, close, amount, the IC close/open pair, the SSE bridge, EW passive, the IC
bar mask), carried in the cell JSON and in one `_panel.json` sidecar per shard directory. A
checkpoint written before this change carries no digest and reads as MISS -- unreadable is never
clean, and the cost of having been wrong is one recompute.

Second, and the reusable half. The sweep r1376 built to answer "which faces cache a live value"
produced sixteen rows and never shrank, because most rows were not caches: `cache = {}` filled and
read inside one call is the function's own scratch (five rows), and `stress_cache_digest` is the
RULER, not the thing measured (one row). Two syntactic rules -- a name bound to an empty literal
is a memo, a name that also carries a digest word is a ruler -- drop 45 memo names and 6 rulers
and leave twelve decisions. The remaining twelve were then READ, one line each, and published with
a class (`fixed` / `guarded` / `refuses` / `not_a_cache`) plus the sentence that justifies it; a
row nobody has read is published as `unread`, so the work still owed is its own number instead of
hiding inside a total. A face that leaves the list is named in `closed_by_reading` -- otherwise
"fixed" and "the scanner stopped seeing it" look identical. Two of the twelve were real:
`member_reinforce_p1` is `guarded` because G-ANCHOR re-runs the anchor backtest on the live panel
and refuses BEFORE the cell cache is read, and `p1e`/`t73` are input panels whose recon gate
raises. Reading the list is the work; the count was never the deliverable.

Pos: scripts/cn_mkneutral_p1.py `panel_digest()` / `load_cell_ck()` / `_shard_stamp_ok()`
(selftest 51/51) / Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py `_memo_names()` / `_ruler_names()` / `TRIAGE`
(selftest 24/24) / results/live_value_cache_audit.json (`closed_by_reading`).

### E297 -- a unit being burned is not supply: read the claim face the claimer writes (r1181 bm-a)

Two paths claim pool work and each writes a different file. The fleet autofill path sets
`shards[].owner` inside results/runnable_pool.json; the pool_worker path (O-20260928-2210) writes
results/pool_claims/<entry>/<shard>.<machine>.json and never touches the pool row. Every reader
that consults only the pool JSON therefore sees a unit being burned as `status=ready, unclaimed`.

2026-10-31 live: the ATLAS-ARMS-ONEWORLD unit burned from 04:42 at 8 workers while the next audit
published pool_ready_unclaimed=1, ready_ignitable=1 and a supply-floor breach over it -- the board
said a machine could start work that was already running.

Cure: claim truth is the UNION of both faces, read through the claimer's own parser
(Tools/pool_worker._shard_claim_age_min, one implementation, no second copy) with ONE staleness
line (20 min = the claimer's STALE_MIN, and a smoke leg asserts the two constants are equal). A
stale claim is not liveness; a failed claim is not occupancy.

The trap inside the cure: subtracting claimed rows from `ready_ignitable` alone leaves the
owner-silence test reading `ready_total - runner_missing > 0` and fires a false red of exactly the
class just healed. A burning unit must leave every denominator, not one.

Sibling lesson, same window: proving a family priced one panel is not the same as republishing the
comparison built FROM those panels. The rebuild ran only `--check` and got rc 2 ("face is stale,
cutoff 2026-10-29") while all eleven faces were already uniform -- the arms face that holds the ten
labels is a separate artifact and had to be rebuilt. A unit whose deliverable is a reader's face
must run its producer, not only its verifier.

Pos: scripts/compute_audit.py (`claim_by_file_face`/`POOL_CLAIM_STALE_MIN`, selftest 64/64) /
scripts/etf_book_atlas_arms_rebuild.py (`audit_steps`/`success`, selftest 16 legs) / smoke leg
"the compute audit reads the claim face, not just the pool row".

## E298 A gate that moves into a helper leaves the census reading a row that says the opposite of the truth (2026-10-31 r1379 bm-c)

r1378 made a face safer by moving its disk read AND its provenance gate into `_chain_base()`. The
census that watches such faces (Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py) enters a function through a cache-ish
NAME plus a literal-key read. Once both went into the helper, `finalize` contained no comparison, only
a call -- so the scanner published `reads_a_stored_value: false, evidence_class:
no_stored_value_read` about a face whose ledger head comes straight off disk. The live row was not a
miss; it was a false sentence. And the leg written for it (L35, "a gate that lives in a helper is
still a gate on the face that uses it") could never pass, because its fixture was ignored for the same
reason; meanwhile the sibling leg L34 passed for the wrong reason -- the value had vanished from the
table, not been gated.

Three edges have to be in any "who reads disk" census: the delegation edge (admit a caller only when
the helper it calls actually checks identity keys, not merely because the helper exists), the binding
(the call's targets include tuple unpacking -- reading only `ast.Name` made `prev_total, src =
_chain_base(...)` look like a call returning nothing), and the product (a value served by being written
into the JSON payload counts as served; a return-only taint rule says "serves nothing").

Effect on the published face: cache decisions 16 -> 31, identity-checked 0 -> 19, rows that serve a
stored value with no digest anywhere 2 -> 1 (alloc_backtest.run_cell_faces, whose digest is handed in
as an argument and whose F9-d leg already forces the recompute). Six faces left the headline each with
the helper name and its identity keys published beside it; nothing was downgraded -- the memo rows
stayed memos.

Sibling lesson from the same estate: a worktree rebuilt from an older base had deleted two legs bm-a
added upstream (`etf_book_flip_contamination`, `etf_book_shock_day`); only the pre-push ownership claw
would have caught it. An inherited tree is a subject, not a summary -- every leg was re-run here before
anything was committed.

Pos: Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py (`helper_facts`/`_call_targets`/`_json_payload_names`/`_taint`,
selftest 37/37) + results/live_value_cache_audit.json + scripts/member_reinforce_p1.py (`_chain_base`)
+ research/digests/DIGEST-20261031-r1379-delegation-blind-spot.md

## E299 A license that lives on one face is not a license the operator holds (2026-10-31 r1184 bm-a)

E293 taught the production CARD to print the panel fingerprint; E295 made the consumer withhold a
label whose stamp nobody read. Then the card learned which world its WIDTH came from (r1183: "Family
coherence: ONE_WORLD -- all 10 width arms priced one panel"). Two other faces quote the same width
family and carried none of it: the executable sheet an operator actually holds, and the CEO one-page.
A reader on either could not tell a measured width from a configured one, and nothing on those pages
was wrong -- they simply inherited a licence that was only written down somewhere else.

Publishing a verdict is not propagating it. Count the faces a reader can hold, and make each carry
the same sentence from ONE reader (`etf_book_production.family_coherence`), never a second derivation.
Three states must be distinguishable in the text of every face -- licensed / withheld / absent -- or
"unproven" reads as "fine". The two failure directions are not symmetric: an affirmative-only leg is
satisfied by a page that prints the good word unconditionally, so the leg has to (a) recompute the
verdict from the artifact, (b) require the arm count beside the word, and (c) assert that a withheld or
absent family never prints the affirmative token. A fixture that swaps the field on the LIVE page
(rather than a hand-built dict) keeps the leg failing only for the reason it is about.

Pos: scripts/etf_book_production.py::family_coherence (the one implementation) +
scripts/etf_book_order.py (ORDER sheet + card-config sheet, selftest 77/77, legs 46g/46h) +
scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py (L54-L56, selftest 59/59) + smoke_test.py leg "every face a reader
holds says whether the width's family was one world" + research/digests/DIGEST-20261031-r1184-width-license.md

## E300 A provenance pin whose two sides are one literal is a spelling check (2026-10-31 r1380 bm-c)

`if d.get("evidence_cutoff") == CUTOFF` where the writer wrote `"evidence_cutoff": CUTOFF`
cannot distinguish a product of this rule from a product of the previous rule: both sides
are the same constant. It catches a hand-edited file and nothing else -- a moved reject
line, a moved tolerance, an added pair, a repaired panel bar all leave it green. A date at
least moves with the calendar; a constant moves with nothing.

Cure: digest the rule block and the panel bytes (`inputs_sha256`), and make an unreadable
panel a MISS rather than a cache hit. Limit that must be published with it: the digest
covers the inputs, not the conclusion.

Detector (`Tools/live_value_cache_audit.py`, selftest 41/41): a row carries
`identity_pins` and `identity_pin_is_self_written` when the compared constant is also baked
into a payload the module writes. Two refinements keep the count honest -- the pin may live
in a helper the face merely calls (r1379's delegation shape, one layer down), and a pin
stops being tautological once the same stored value is ALSO compared against something
computable (`guarded_by_computed`), so fixing a face moves the number that measured it.
Live 2026-10-31, first reading: 19 identity-checked rows, 5 self-written, 2 of them live
(`alphagen_rg_census.cmd_run`, `member_reinforce_p1.finalize`). Republished after the
`alphagen_rg_census` cure landed in the same round: 18 identity-checked, 4 self-written,
exactly ONE live face left -- because that face now keys its panel cache on the member
bytes it consumed plus the rule block (selftest 37/37, legs s9a-s9d: an appended bar must
NOT move the digest, a repaired bar MUST, a moved constant must, a pre-digest cache is a
named MISS). The count fell because the face changed, which is the point of
`guarded_by_computed`; a detector that cannot see its own fix reports it forever.

## E301 A shared face's sentence about "本机" is a sentence about nobody until it names the observer (2026-10-31 r1380 bm-c)

`results/data_lane_freshness.json` is one shared file, rewritten each round by whichever
machine ran the chain, and its disk block counts lanes relative to that machine. The daily
report copies the sentence verbatim (single implementation, r1162) -- so the CEO page printed
"9 条在役车道里有 9 条的数字不在**本机**盘上" with no way to learn whose disk "本机" was. On
the morning two machines took turns, the bm-c-built report (9 off-host) sat beside the
bm-a-refreshed board (6 off-host) and a smoke leg demanding `n_offhost` equality read a
host difference as a contradiction.

Three rules, and each one is a leg now. **A face that aggregates a per-observer statistic
must publish the observer**: `disk_face()` returns `observer` and prefixes its sentence with
"以本机 <id> 的盘为基准", so the same lanes read from bm-a and bm-c give different sentences
and neither is lying (L16). **The face that copies it must carry the name through**:
`_lane_authority_face()` publishes `observer` next to the sentence -- a copy is the next
defect, so the name travels with the number. **A gate comparing two faces of one shared
artifact must first ask whether they were computed by the same observer**: same observer ->
the counters must be equal; different observers -> both sides must name themselves, and the
reason line says `observers differ (report=bm-a board=bm-c)` instead of printing a fake
inequality. Demanding numeric equality across hosts does not test agreement, it demands that
the other machines stop writing their own boards.

Falsifiability kept: the sentence check runs on every path, so a report that drops the
sentence still reds. The limit, stated: a report written before the tag existed passes with
the word `report-predates-observer-tag` and its counts are NOT judged -- an old artifact is
un-attributable, not a contradiction. The pit is the shape -- this leg was green for weeks
because every board it had seen had one author; a per-host statistic agrees with itself
until it is read across machines (r1262 "compare like with like" one level up: the
mismatching input was the observer, not a parameter).

## E303 A paper that says "same" is not a paper that says WHICH day (2026-10-31 r1186 bm-a)

E299 made every face carry the family licence; the priced-day face was still published by ONE paper.
The card and the executable sheet printed the bare boolean `same_priced_bar`, so an operator holding
those two -- the two a person actually acts on -- was told "different" without being told WHICH paper
had fallen behind. Publication on the page did not reach the readers who never open the page.

Two rules, both cheap and both missed on the first attempt:

1. A three-state answer has to be printed by every paper that quotes the number, each from its own
   artifact, and a face that is building itself must hand in its OWN bar rather than read back
   yesterday's copy of itself (the card is written after the comparison runs).
2. The smoke leg has to re-derive the verdict from THAT paper's artifact and then look for it in THAT
   paper's text. Reading it off a neighbouring paper (or asserting only the page) lets either paper
   drop the sentence and still pass. Negative control: strip the line from copies of the live CARD.md
   and ORDER-*.md and the leg must flag both by name.

Also: absence of a day is never agreement. A missing artifact reads UNKNOWN, and the word for
"unknown" must be distinguishable from the word for "same" in the text (SAME_DAY / DIFFERENT_DAYS /
UNKNOWN on the card; 同一天 / 不是同一天 / 无法判断 on the executable sheet).

Pos: scripts/etf_book_production.py::priced_faces(+_page_priced_bar) is the one implementation;
scripts/etf_book_order.py::priced_face_check quotes it (selftest 79/79, legs 26b/26c); both the card
(`Priced day: ...`) and ORDER-*.md (`两张生产面是不是同一天`, three days + verdict) print it from their
own block; smoke_test.py leg "the three papers print the day each was priced on, and can disagree"
now checks all three papers. (Card number: bm-c took E300 at r1380, so this one moved to E301 per the
collision rule -- card ids live only in this ledger, so the references moved with it.)

**Renumbered E301 -> E303 (r1187):** the move to E301 was itself a collision -- bm-c's r1380 batch
landed TWO cards (E300 and E301) while this window only checked against the one id it knew about, so
the ledger now carried E301 twice and bm-c's next window took E302. See E304 for the rule.

## E302 - 供给阶梯把「已关单的家族」读成「缺一个 schema 字段」

**症状**：Tools/fill_ladder.py 的供给阶梯在池空时打印唯一一行阻断：
「INNOVATION-QUOTA-SLOT-3 (consumer_plan missing ...)」。这一行的字面意思是
「补一个 consumer_plan 就有活了」——而该条目的 prereg 自己的状态行写着
**已判决关单（judged-negative 0/3）**。一个读者照着这行做，就会把同一个
zoo #95 族、同一套冻结参数、同一组格子再烧一遍，产出第二次同名判决。

**根因**：prereg_frozen 闸只问了两件事——文件在不在、头部有没有**草稿**标记。
关单与撤回不是草稿标记：它们是**结论**。于是「冻结」被当成「可烧」，能拦住它
的只剩 consumer_plan 这个跟科学无关的 schema 字段。这正是本仓反复记录的形态：
**闸通过的理由不是它想测的那个理由**。

**修法**：状态行（r189 权威面）出现 已判决/关单/RETRACTED/撤回/判负终局 -> 拒，
理由写作 prereg closed (已判决)。叙述里提到「曾撤回」而状态是 FROZEN 的照旧通过
——历史不是状态。

**可证伪**：自检四条腿。已判决状态拒 / RETRACTED 状态拒 / FROZEN 状态但叙述含
撤回仍过 / 真实的 W3 prereg 被**以关单为理由**拒（不是以 schema 为理由）。现场跑
一次阶梯，阻断行从「缺字段」变成「已关单」。

**局限（写明）**：闸只认这五个字面，别族的关单词汇仍可能漏；真正的重开通道是写
新 prereg，阶梯不做语义推断。


## E304 A new id checked against the id you remember is not checked against the ledger (2026-10-31 r1187 bm-a)

Two machines assign ledger ids by reading the highest one they know and adding one. The r1349
collision rule ("later card yields numbers") is prose, so the only thing it constrains is a writer
who happens to know every id the peers just wrote. r1186 checked against `E300` -- the id bm-c's
window had named -- and took `E301`. bm-c's SAME batch had landed two cards, `E300` and `E301`, so
the ledger carried `E301` twice, and bm-c's next window then took `E302`. Nothing complained: ids
are cited from the ledger, and the ledger is append-only text nobody re-reads.

The rule, mechanical: an id assignment must be validated against the **set** of ids present at
origin, not against the maximum you remember, and the validation has to run at write time
(`git show origin/main:<ledger> | grep -oE '^## E[0-9]+'`), not at plan time. A duplicate id is
not a cosmetic problem -- E301 now names two unrelated lessons, so a citation of E301 is
ambiguous forever, and the only repair is renumbering one of them and disclosing it in place
(done here: bm-a's card moved E301 -> E303).

Pos: knowledge/METHODOLOGY_ASSETS.md (E303 renumber + this card); the check is `git show
origin/main:knowledge/METHODOLOGY_ASSETS.md` id sweep before every assignment.

## E305 - 唯一的记号闸装在提交上，而手术推送从不走提交

**症状**：`knowledge/METHODOLOGY_ASSETS.md:7316` 带着一行孤立的
`||||||| parent of 552f00ce84` 进了 r1381 的提交（09:42 上链），一直到
10:14 一个已经死掉的窗口的 smoke 才把它报红——它在一个每轮都被读的账本里
躺了 44 分钟。而 pre-commit claw 当天是好的（探针：stage 一个含 base 记号的
临时文件，claw 直接 rc=1）。

**根因**：闸只挂在 commit 上。手术推送路径是「cherry-pick 到 origin 顶端
再 push」，而 `git rebase` / `git cherry-pick` / `commit-tree` 都不跑
pre-commit（钩子文件自己的头注就写着 Rebase replays are not affected）。于是
「所有路径都会经过的那一步」是 push，而 push 上只有属主爪，没有记号爪。
两次实弹（r1304、r1381）走的是同一条路。

**修法**：`Tools/git_claw.py` 新增 `marker_fragments()` / `marker_violations()`，
接进 `check_push()` —— pre-push 钩子直接继承，零新装、零名单。作用域与 smoke
腿一致：只扫本次推送 AMCR 的 blob，`results/_*` 抓原始 diff 的捕获件除外；
整块平衡 hunk（文档引用一次完整冲突）合法，孤立 `|||||||`/`=======`/`>>>>>>>`
与未闭合 hunk 一律拒。规则只有一份：`smoke_test._hunk_state` 改为委托
`git_claw.marker_fragments()`（抄一份规则就是下一个缺陷）。

**验证脚**：`Tools/git_claw.py selftest` 38→**44/44**（孤立 base 拒 / 整块引用
通过 / results/_ 捕获不扫 / 未闭合 hunk 拒 / 拒绝信息带文件名与行号 / 纯函数
判定本身）。实弹：`check_push(origin/main, r1381)` **rc=1** 点名
`knowledge/METHODOLOGY_ASSETS.md:7316 |||||||` —— 这道闸今天就会在 r1381 的
推送口上把它挡住。

**局限**：闸在本机钩子上，`--no-verify` 仍是逃生口（须在轮报留痕）；`results/_*`
不扫意味着抓原始 diff 的捕获件里可以合法躺记号（与 smoke 同 scope）；
已经进历史的记号不会被这道闸回溯清理（r1381 的靠行级删除修）。

## E306 Attribute a compounding book by booking each factor as a LOG, not by splitting the percentage (2026-10-31 r1188 bm-a)

A book's nav is a product of factors, so a split of the *level* does not add up: 8.21pp/yr
split as percentages leaves a residual that grows with sample length, while the same split
done in logs closes exactly (measured residual -5.6e-17 over 1653 bars, four buckets).
Two consequences worth remembering:

* the four buckets are not "shares of the return" -- they are the log contributions of the
  four places the machine multiplies (overnight while the order is pending / toll at the
  fill open / intraday after the fill / quiet bar close-to-close). Booth-armed ordering
  matters: `overnight` belongs to the OLD book and `intraday` to the NEW one, which is the
  difference between a close-auction strategy and a market-on-open strategy;
* the walker must be pinned to the engine by an identity leg, because a copied loop is a
  second implementation. Here L1 recomputes Forward's nav curve and demands a byte-level
  equal series, so a drift in either walker turns the suite red instead of publishing a
  plausible attribution of a different book. Same family as E289 (one implementation,
  imported) -- the exception is allowed only when the copy is *measured* against the source
  on every run.

Readings that came out of it: production 8.21pp/yr = plain +9.83 in 1569 quiet bars, fill
drag -1.62 over 83 fill bars (toll -1.145, fill-bar intraday -0.636, overnight +0.163) --
i.e. the book is paid on the quiet bars and the fill bars are a cost, which is what a
20-day rebalance with 0.93 turnover per decision should look like and had never been said.

## E307 - 账本里的控制字符是「没有读者的腐蚀类」：记录过不等于修过

**触发**：r1380 在卡片账本里查明 7 个控制字符，写下「本轮不修」，然后**没有任何东西去读它**——一条已经登记的腐蚀类就这么躺了整夜。r1383 实测 `knowledge/METHODOLOGY_ASSETS.md` 仍有 7 处（0x08 / 0x07 / 0x1b），其中五处**吃掉了后面那个 token 的首字母**：`reak_guard`（应为 break_guard）、`bs(ser.iloc[0]`（应为 abs()）、`tf_breadth_sweep.py`、`tf_book_production.run()`、`urncontrol`（应为 burncontrol）；另两处是把转义符插在完整 token 之前（`book_decided_at`）。Markdown 渲染里这些字符不可见，所以账本可以长期「说着一句没人看见过的话」——而这句话恰好是代码里的标识符，读者照着抄会抄到一个不存在的函数。

**正法**：`Tools/card_text_hygiene.py` 三件。①`scan` 只读扫 corpus（`knowledge/*.md` + `research/digests/*.md`；tab/CR/LF 合法），有命中 exit 1 并打印 文件:行:字节 + 上下文。②`fix` 走**冻结表**：每行 damaged → restored 都带 **proof**（磁盘上的文件名，或代码里真实使用的标识符——`scripts/e39_panel_census.py` 里确有 `break_guard`，`scripts/e110_burncontrol_close.py` 里确有 `burncontrol`），且每处必须**恰好出现一次**：0 次或 2 次一律 exit 2 且零写，绝不靠猜。③修完做**行级并集断言**：行数不变、变动行集合必须恰好等于修复行集合、且把 restored 换回 damaged 必须逐字复原该行；任一条不成立 exit 3 且零写。treasure_guard 对本面 rc3 硬拒是设计如此（登记簿的合法通路只有行级并集或摘录存旁），本工具走的就是行级并集并落回执。

**验证脚**：`Tools/card_text_hygiene.py selftest` **11/11**（清洁文本通过 / 控制字符带行号 / digest 在射程 / 七处全部修复 / 每处一行 / 修后零控制字符 / 站点缺失拒且零写 / 重复出现拒 / 干跑可复原原字节 / 每行带 proof）。实弹：scan 411 文件 **7 处** → `fix` rc0 → scan **0 处**；`git diff --numstat` **7/7**（只有这 7 行动，其余 726KB 逐字节不动）。smoke 新增腿 `config: no card or digest carries a control character`，与工具**共用**同一个 corpus 与同一个控制字符集（抄一份规则就是下一个缺陷）。

**首版自己的坑**：行级校验最初是把 restored 字符串在全文件里 `find()` 回来——可 `book_decided_at` 这类标识符在账本里本就出现多次，于是「复原」找错位置，健康文件被自己的断言判红。正法=**按行复原**：只在记录下来的那一行内部做逆向替换。同一个词在多处出现、却用全文件搜索当身份，是 E300「拿记忆核对 id」的同族错误。

**局限**：本条只治**已知形态**（吃掉 token 首字母的转义残留）。被吃掉的是中间字母、或整段被截断的，scan 看得见但冻结表里没有对应行——那是人判范围，工具一律拒而不猜；corpus 也尚未覆盖 `research/*.md` 与 `docs/`，扩面须同窗核一遍假阳性。

## E308 - 一条「按时间拆钱」的答案只能有一份实现，且算不平的拆分必须被拒绝而不是加个注 (2026-10-31 r1189 bm-a)

本窗把 r1188 的四桶归因（隔夜/成交 bar 内/安静 bar/手续费）从 `results/etf_book_legs/` 搬到 CEO 一页纸。
缺陷不是「没印」，而是「印法有两种都像对的」：

1. **第二个实现 = 第二个答案**（E289 族）。页面若自己从净值曲线重算四桶，两处第一次任一被改就静默分叉，
   而两处分叉时页面上的四个数依然加得出一个总数——只是不同的总数。正法：`etf_book_leg_attribution.money_split_face()`
   是唯一读者，页面 import 它，缺件读 `absent`/`unreadable` 而不是空表。
2. **算不平的拆分读起来和真数据一模一样**。reader 里 `check_face()` 对「四桶之和不等于自身总对数收益」
   直接拒否（`broken`），不打印四舍五入后的近似值、也不加注「可能有偏差」——一个不自洽的拆分要按
   机制故障处理，因为读者没有第二个参照物能发现它。
3. 页面的三种状态必须互不相同：读不到 / 已核验（并印出四桶与成交 bar 数）/ 自相矛盾被拒。
   本窗 smoke 腿（ceo_page L59-L62）分别用「无定价日」「缺件」「坏件」三组夹具钉住，其中坏件腿
   断言的是**页面上不出现任何桶数字**，而不是出现一句警告。

Why：读者手上每一个数都是一个 CAGR；把 CAGR 拆成时间桶之后，读者第一次能问「这笔钱是不是台抽水机」。
一条会给错答案但格式漂亮的拆分，比没有这条面更糟——它把「没测」伪装成「测过了，是 −0.47 个百分点」。

Pos: `scripts/etf_book_leg_attribution.py::money_split_face/check_face` · `scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py`（leg-split 段 + L59-L62）
· `docs/live_book/LIVE-BOOK-20261031.md`
## E309 A module nobody committed is a dependency nobody can install -- and the face it published is the only witness that can prove the restoration (2026-10-31 r1384 bm-c)

**Shape.** `scripts/e230_hysteresis_toll.py` was imported by seven scripts and present in no
commit (`git log --all -- <path>` empty). Every one of them was therefore un-startable on a
clean checkout, and nothing in the tree could see it: the importers compile, the file simply
is not there at run time. Same family as E302 (a supply row closed as "missing a schema
field" that its own prereg calls judged-negative) and the r1360 finding (`ready_dep_broken`).

**Two rules.**

1. **A published face is an executable acceptance test for code that no longer exists.** The
   missing module's OUTPUT was committed (`HYSTERESIS-2026-10-29.json` + the per-rung CSV),
   so the reconstruction could be held to `0 numeric diffs` over 2 panels x 3 arms x 6 rungs
   x 2 cost levels rather than to a plausible story: `reproduce()` truncates the live panel
   back to the face's own session counts and diffs every number, then the verdict counts
   (1/5/24) fall out as a consequence. Any future "we rewrote X from its digest" claim has a
   template now -- publish the face first, then the code can always be rebuilt and checked.
2. **Text is not a number: release the prose separately.** The r1256 face prints
   `toll +226.8` beside a published delta of `+113.4` -- twice the number it measured. The
   restoration reproduces every numeric field and DISAGREES with that sentence on purpose,
   so the gate has two outputs (`numeric_identical`, `prose_diffs`) and only the numeric one
   is a leg. A gate that demanded byte equality would have made the bug load-bearing.

**The second layer.** `e229.channel_rows` called `e228.rows_for`, which no longer existed
either -- so the family was broken twice over and the first fix alone would have looked like
a fix. The cheapest test that sees this is not the importer's: it is the DOWNSTREAM
selftest (`e232_cost_curvature.py selftest` crashed on `e228.rows_for` in its gamma=1
crosscheck). `rows_for` is restored once, `e230` delegates to it instead of carrying a copy,
and a leg pins it to `excess_series` -- the function the published E228 face actually uses --
so the two definitions of the same excess cannot drift.

**Verify.** e230 selftest 16/16 incl. `reproduces_the_published_face_numbers` and
`frozen_acceptance_face_still_exists`; e228 22/22 (+3); e231/e233/e234/e235/e236/e237
18/13/14/18/18/18 all green on a clean checkout; e232 10/10 (was a crash).

### E298 -- "is the book concentrated?" is two questions, and a top-k leave-out answers neither well (r1190 bm-a)

The allocator's question after a CAGR is *whose* CAGR. Two traps, both hit and both now pinned
by tests on `scripts/etf_book_concentration.py` (selftest 12/12, `--check` recomputes):

1. **A contribution split must first be the engine, then a split.** Splitting P&L bar by bar is a
   second implementation of the equity path; if it drifts, the face measures its own arithmetic
   and every share inherits the error silently. The replica therefore rebuilds the curve from the
   engine's own `diag` weights and REFUSES TO PUBLISH when `max|replica-engine| > 1e-10` (live:
   3.1e-15). Same discipline as the drawdown guard's identity arm (E287).
2. **A top-k leave-out of names that share an asset class is a redundancy test, not a sleeve
   test.** The book's four biggest contributors were four gold ETFs held in 43-47% of decisions;
   deleting the top three cost only -1.99pp CAGR because the fourth simply took the slot. The
   question that matters needed a second arm: delete the whole commodity sleeve (41 ETFs) ->
   9.60% -> 5.89% CAGR, Sharpe 0.99 -> 0.64, maxDD -11.8% -> **-22.3%**. The same sleeve that
   earns is the sleeve that cushions -- a single dependency invisible in a top-k table.
3. **Report the denominator with the share.** Shares are of the SUM of daily arithmetic returns
   (gross 0.7444), not of log growth and not of the net curve, which also carries the fee toll
   (0.0775). Mixing the two made a first draft of the selftest read shares summing to 1.18.
   The face publishes `shares_sum_all_names` so a gate can check 100% over ALL names -- a
   truncated top-10 table sums past 100% whenever any contributor is negative (69 of 189 were).

Live reading (2026-10-31): top1 7.5%, top3 22.1%, HHI 0.0241 -> verdict BROAD|SURVIVES_WITHOUT_TOP3,
with the commodity caveat quoted above. Pos: scripts/etf_book_concentration.py (selftest 12/12) /
results/etf_book_concentration.json / research/ETF_BOOK_CONCENTRATION.md / chain leg
etf_book_concentration (before etf_book_order) / smoke leg re-deriving the verdict from the rows.

## E310 A selftest cannot testify to its own deletion -- the witness has to be a consumer (2026-10-31 r1385 bm-c)

**Shape.** Two files in the working tree were one commit behind: `scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py`
and `scripts/etf_book_leg_attribution.py` no longer contained the `leg_split` reader or the page
section it feeds. Their own selftest still printed `Summary: 62/62 PASS` -- because the three legs
that guarded the reader and the section (L60-L62) had gone away WITH the feature, and one of them
was the only place that counted legs. Nothing else noticed: the regenerated live page simply lost
the section (19 md lines / 66 json lines), which is how a user-facing guarantee disappears while
every gate stays green.

**Three rules.**

1. **A test inside the thing it tests cannot notice its own removal.** `N/N PASS` is measured over
   the legs that still exist, so deleting a leg is always a silent success. The only durable
   witness is an assertion OUTSIDE, on the consumer's output: render the page with a fixture face
   and require the promised tokens, then hand the reader a face whose buckets miss its own total
   and require refusal. Delete either half and the leg turns red although its own body is untouched.
2. **Stale is not intent: measure the tree against the commit, not against your expectations.**
   `git hash-object <path>` vs `git rev-parse <commit>:<path>` matched `HEAD~5`'s blob exactly, so
   the deletion was a restore artefact, not a decision -- restore, do not "review the change". The
   r609 law applies to the closing window: `git add -A` on that tree would have shipped the revert.
3. **The incident is the fixture.** Loading the actual stale blobs (`git show 1b0728461c:scripts/...`
   into `scripts/_r1385_stale_*.py`, then `importlib`) gives `money_split_face absent` and tokens
   `[False, False, False, False]` while their own selftest passes 62/62 -- so the new leg is proved
   against the real regression rather than against a hand-made one.

**Verify.** `smoke_test.py` leg `product: the CEO page still prints where in the day the money is
earned`; `scripts/etf_book_leg_attribution.py selftest` 13/13; `scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py
selftest` 65/65; the reprised files are byte-identical to `HEAD` and the published page restored.

## E311 A measurement is not delivered until it reaches the face the reader opens -- and a verdict string carrying a pipe breaks its own table (2026-10-31 r1191 bm-a)

**Shape.** r1190 built the concentration reading (top-3 share of the gross, delete-top-3,
delete-the-whole-sleeve) and printed it on the ORDER ticket. The next round's own state file
recorded the gap in one line: *"the concentration face has not reached the CEO page (only the
ticket)"*. Two faces, one book: the ticket is what an executor holds, the live-book page is
what the CEO actually opens. A reading that lands only on the first is a measurement delivered
to an audience of one script.

**Two rules.**

1. **Name the reader before calling a face published.** The question is not "does a face exist"
   but "which human face quotes it". Wiring ticket-only feels complete because something printed
   it; it is not complete for the person the book is reported to. The r1290 family (producer
   behind consumer) has a mirror-image failure: a producer *in front of* the wrong consumer.
2. **A machine verdict embedded in prose needs escaping at the render site.** `BROAD|SURVIVES_WITHOUT_TOP3`
   was written verbatim into a markdown table cell, and the pipe split the row into a fourth
   column: the page still rendered, the number still read, and the table silently stopped being
   the table it claimed to be. Cure: the renderer substitutes the separator (`BROAD · SURVIVES...`)
   and the smoke leg asserts the *rendered* form, so a future consumer that re-adds the raw verdict
   reads red instead of producing a legal-looking broken table.

**Falsifiability.** ceo_page L63-L65: the four numbers and the verdict must reach the page from
the published face's own values; a face whose identity gate failed (`no_identity`) or is absent
must print "cannot verify" with **no numbers at all**, never "broad" by default. The smoke leg
re-reads `results/etf_book_concentration.json` and demands the page print those exact strings
(equal, not merely present, r1130 law).

Live reading (2026-10-31): top3 22.1%, delete-top-3 -1.99pp, delete commodity sleeve -3.71pp
(maxdd -11.8% -> -22.3%). Pos: scripts/etf_book_ceo_page.py::concentration_face + the page
section + L63-L65 / smoke_test.py leg r1191 / docs/live_book/LIVE-BOOK-20261031.md.

### E312 -- a sleeve gate that is not better than "hold less sleeve" is not a signal (r1192 bm-a)

The next question after "the commodity sleeve is the cushion" is not "keep it?" (yes: deleting
it costs 3.71pp of CAGR and takes the drawdown from -11.8% to -22.3%) but "does it need a gate of
its own?" -- three gates priced on the production engine through the same mechanism the
concentration face uses (commodity columns' score -> NaN on the gated decision dates, so the
engine substitutes on its own grid; nothing here re-implements selection, sizing or costs):

| gate | sleeve off | CAGR vs card | max drawdown vs card | vs its own placebo |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sleeve below its own 200d MA | 20/83 (24.1%) | -0.32pp | -2.77pp | loses |
| sleeve vol > its own trailing p80 | 25/83 (30.1%) | +0.02pp | **+0.86pp** | wins by 5.00pp |
| sleeve 10% below trailing peak | 13/83 (15.7%) | -0.18pp | -1.27pp | wins by 2.43pp |

*(r1193 correction: this table's drawdown row said "loses" and piece 1 said two of three gates
lose to their own placebo; the artifact says the drawdown gate beats its placebo by +2.43pp dd /
+2.00pp CAGR and only the trend gate loses. Hand-typed prose drifted from the numbers it quotes;
the card is now generated (`etf_book_sleeve_gate.write_md`) so the column cannot drift again.)*

## The three reusable pieces

1. **Dose-matched placebo, or the arm measures nothing.** Switching a sleeve off a fixed share
   of decisions changes two things at once: the exposure level and the timing. A placebo with the
   *same number* of off-decisions spread evenly over the grid holds the exposure change fixed and
   destroys only the timing, so "beats its own placebo" is the only form of the claim that can be
   a signal. Read the two columns together: **a signal can be real and still not worth trading** --
   the vol and drawdown gates both beat their own placebos, and neither improves the drawdown
   against simply holding the sleeve (E188/E199 path-test family).
2. **Freeze the bar before the run, then quote the near miss instead of moving it.** The vol gate
   cut the drawdown 0.86pp against a pre-registered 1.0pp bar and beat its placebo by 5.00pp:
   the honest publication is "named as the re-test candidate on new data, NOT adopted", never
   "bar 1.0 -> 0.8". The card prints that sentence from the rows (`judge_gate` + `--check`
   recompute every verdict), so the retelling cannot drift from the numbers.
3. **A new face must reproduce the old face's deletion arm.** The reference arm (delete the whole
   sleeve) has to land on the concentration face's published -3.71pp to 0.01pp on the same
   cutoff, or the two faces are quoting different books (live: gap 0.0pp, same cutoff). Same
   discipline as the page==face equality leg (r1191); an all-on gate must also be bit-identical
   to the baseline (live: max abs 0.0), which is what proves the gate mechanism adds nothing by
   itself.

## Answer to the question

NO_GATE. The sleeve's risk is already carried by the book-level vol target (it only de-levers);
across all three shapes a sleeve gate did not make the drawdown shallower than simply holding the
sleeve (two of them did beat their own placebo, so the lesson is not "the signals are noise" but
"a real signal can still be worse than doing nothing here"). The one shape that cleared its
placebo by the widest margin (sleeve-vol cutoff) missed its frozen drawdown bar and stays a
candidate, not a component.

Pos: `scripts/etf_book_sleeve_gate.py` (selftest 23/23, `--check` recomputes verdicts and the
answer string) + `results/etf_book_sleeve_gate.json` + `research/ETF_BOOK_SLEEVE_GATE.md`
(generated at r1193 — `OUT_MD` had been declared and never written) + CARD.md section + the CEO
page section "要不要给商品袖单独立个风险门" (r1193) + `research/digests/DIGEST-20261031-r1192-bma-sleeve-gate.md`.

Also r1192: the "whose CAGR" block moved down into `etf_book_concentration.reading()` so the
card, the ticket and the CEO page format one implementation instead of three copies (E289
family; the page's `concentration_face` is now a delegate).

### E313 A sentence that explains a number must be generated by the same function that reads it (r1193)

The sleeve-gate reading shipped at r1192 with a hand-typed "vs its own placebo" column, and the
column said the drawdown gate loses. Its own artifact says it wins by +2.43pp of drawdown and
+2.00pp of CAGR -- two gates beat their placebos, only one loses. Nobody caught it for four
rounds because no gate can check prose: `--check` recomputes verdicts, the ledger guard checks
ids, the smoke leg checks equality of *numbers* that pages print. Three fixes make the class
impossible rather than fixing the instance: (a) `gate_rows()` now classifies every arm
(`adopted` / `no_gain_vs_baseline` / `short_of_bar` / `gives_up_cagr` / `loses_to_placebo`) from
that arm's own two numbers, and every face prints the classification instead of typing it;
(b) `write_md()` generates the research card from the payload, so the "wins/loses" column cannot
drift (`OUT_MD` had been declared at r1192 and never written -- a dead constant is how a face
ends up with no generated record at all); (c) the CEO page reads that same block and a smoke leg
re-reads the page and refuses a sentence claiming a gate lost to a placebo the face says it beat.
Generalisation: when a face prints a *verdict for a reader*, the verdict must be a field on the
artifact, not a phrase in the renderer. And a near miss is worth publishing as a near miss: two
gates beating their placebos while still being worse than the baseline is a stronger statement
than "no significant gain".

### E314 The cost assumption is also a number nobody measured (r1195)

The production card charges every leg a flat 10 bp per side and every downstream number is
priced net of it -- but no face had ever asked what these ETFs actually quote. Estimating each
name's effective spread from its own daily high/low (Corwin-Schultz 2012; 20-day rolling median,
lagged one bar so a decision cannot see t+1) and then weighting the spreads by the book's own
turnover gives a measured one-way cost of **5.9 bp/side** against the 10 bp assumption, i.e. the
card over-charges by ~41%: re-pricing the identical engine at the measured cost earns 10.1%
CAGR / 1.04 Sharpe vs the published 9.6% / 0.99, and even the pessimistic arm (every traded name
at its own 75th percentile -> 9.0 bp/side) leaves 9.7% / 1.00. Two reusable moves: (a) an
assumption that enters every published number deserves its own estimator with a control arm --
the control here is the same engine at the published 10 bp reproducing the card's CAGR, so the
comparison is measured rather than asserted; (b) convert the assumption into the engine's own
unit (equivalent scalar cost = sum|dw|*halfspread / sum|dw|) instead of re-implementing the
cost path, which keeps the engine the single source. Honest limits printed on the face: the
estimator proxies the quote from the day's range (a wide-range day absorbs volatility into the
implied spread), so its p75 arm is the pessimistic bound, not a forecast; and it is an estimate
of half-spread only -- impact beyond the quote is not in it.


### E315 A de-levered book is not a timed book (r1196)

The card has always called the 8% vol target "a drawdown tool, not an edge", and the slot census
agrees that 87.3% of decisions were de-levered -- but nothing had measured whether the exposure
PATH earns anything over simply holding the same book at the same average size. Three arms that
differ in exactly one number (the scale applied at each decision): production (engine scale),
full (exposure 1.0), constant (the book's own mean scale every bar); the control is 20 placebo
paths built by permuting the engine's own scale series across its own decision bars, which keeps
the exposure distribution and destroys the timing information. Live on the 2026-10-30 panel (1653
bars, 83 decisions): production 9.12% CAGR vs constant 10.03% -- timing **-0.91 pp/yr** inside a
placebo band of [-1.90, +0.88] -> AVERAGE_EXPOSURE_ONLY; and constant vs full is **-3.18 pp/yr**,
so what the overlay actually buys is a smaller book, paid for in return rather than earned back
by timing. Two reusable moves: (a) when a rule changes the *size* of a position, the honest
control is the same book at the same average size, and a permutation of the rule's own series is
the placebo that separates "timing" from "less risk"; (b) an arm that replaces one number must be
built on the engine's own state machine with an identity arm that reproduces the engine's nav bit
for bit -- otherwise the comparison is between two books, not between two exposures.

### E316 An identity arm that reads back the engine's *published* number is measuring the publication (r1197)

The exposure-timing face (scripts/etf_book_exposure_timing.py) builds its arms on the engine's own
state machine and hands scale_fn the scale for that decision. The first version read it off
last_decision["scale"] -- the value the engine *publishes*, rounded to 6 decimals. On the fixture the
identity arm reproduced the engine exactly (gap 0.00e+00); on the real panel it was off by
**1.29e-07** of nav. Small, invisible in every printed number, and wrong: the "identity" arm held
marginally different weights, so every other arm was being compared against a book that was not the
book. The 6dp publication is part of the *reporting* surface, not the *decision* surface; feeding it
back is a round trip through a lossy channel.

Fix: the subclass mirrors the decision (selection -> base weights -> P.scale_for -> scale_fn) so
the identity arm consumes the same unrounded scale the engine did, and gap becomes exactly 0.0 on the
real panel. Two reusable moves:

- **(a) The artifact publishes its own identity evidence.** 
un() re-runs the untouched engine and
  writes identity = {gap, tol, passes, pick_drift}; 
ead_published() refuses to hand back arms when
  passes is false, so the page reads "cannot verify" instead of quoting a verdict off an unverified
  book. A selftest-only identity leg cannot do this: the fixture is where the bug hides.
- **(b) A structurally-zero counter cannot testify.** selection_drift counts "the arm changed the
  engine's picks", but every arm derives picks from the same rule, so it is 0 by construction and a
  smoke leg asserting it guards nothing. The real check is a cross-book comparison -- pick_drift
  counts bars where the two books hold different names -- and that is what the gate asserts.

Live: gap 0.00e+00, pick_drift 0, verdict unchanged (AVERAGE_EXPOSURE_ONLY, -0.91pp), published
reading identical to r1196 -- i.e. the defect was in the *witness*, not in the finding, which is the
cheapest possible place to find it.

### E317 A measurement with no scheduled producer goes stale in silence, and a reader must carry the control (r1198)

r1195 measured the cost assumption that sits under *every* published number (10 bp per side; the real
tape says 5.9 bp) and filed it in a research note. Twelve hours later the card, the ticket and the CEO
page were still quoting 10 bp -- not as a mistake, but because the face had **no producer on the
chain**: nothing would ever re-price it, and nothing would notice if the panel moved. This is the
r1290 two-face class one layer down: a face with no scheduled producer is indistinguishable from a
face that agrees.

Two moves, both cheap:

- **(a) An assumption that enters every published number is a measurement target, and it belongs on
  the chain ahead of its readers.** `etf_book_cost_tape` now runs (check + run) immediately before
  `etf_book_ceo_page`, and the page formats it through `read_published()` -- one reader, no second
  implementation (r1290/r1371 family).
- **(b) The reader carries the control, not just the numbers.** The artifact's control arm re-prices
  the same engine *at the assumption* and must reproduce the published CAGR (0.5pp tolerance). If it
  does not, the face is a different book and `read_published()` returns `no_control` with no sentence,
  so the page prints "cannot verify" rather than "the card is conservative". Without that gate the
  claim "our headline is conservative" is unfalsifiable: any mis-specified re-price would still be
  read as evidence about the cost.

Third, smaller: a verdict string containing `|` ("CONSERVATIVE | SURVIVES") is a markdown table
column separator. The page collapses it to `CONSERVATIVE · SURVIVES` and the smoke leg compares
whitespace-normalised, because a raw pipe splits the row *silently* -- the table renders, the number
reads, and it is no longer a table (r1193 recurrence, now pinned at the reader-facing form).

### E318 A red that measures one half of a two-cause state cannot be acted on, so it stays red (r1199)

The compute_audit `supply_floor` flag had been red for 1,258 minutes: pool ready 0, floor 3. That
number has exactly two causes and they need **opposite** responses -- *work exists and nobody put it
in the pool* (enqueue it: a mechanism fault) versus *no startable work exists at all* (generate a
topic: an agenda debt). Because the reader measured only the pool, both printed the same word, and a
red nobody can act on is a red that stays red. Twenty-one hours of it were not a mystery; they were a
missing second half.

The cheap fix is the wrong one: lowering the floor (or adding an "idle is fine" whitelist) is the
cheapest way to lose the signal, and it was already rejected once (r1135 family). The fix used here
adds the other half **beside** the reading, never instead of it:

- **count candidates from the sources that own them**, reusing the one pool/ticket reader
  (`compute_audit.pool_ready_entries` / `fleet_open_tasks`) rather than writing a second parser;
- **separate `startable_now` from everything that merely looks like work**: queue drafts each still
  need a prereg freeze, and `fleet/backlog.md` is cross-company with the owner named in the row --
  counting either as supply would manufacture a green;
- **print the shortfall**, so the verdict carries an amount and not just a mood
  (`AGENDA_DEBT`, shortfall 3, startable 0 on the live face);
- **an unreadable pool stays `UNKNOWN`** -- r98's law again: unknown is not empty.

Gate: the smoke leg re-derives the verdict from the published rows (`--check`), so a "supply is fine"
line has to follow from the numbers that were published, not from whoever typed it.

Read across: any standing flag that has been red for more than a day is a candidate for this defect --
ask what the flag measures and whether the state it names has more than one cause. If it does, publish
the split; do not publish a looser threshold.
### E319 · A status column that carries a suffix turns consumed rows into inventory (r1200 bm-a)

`supply_candidate_face` counted a queue row as a live draft when its status cell did not equal
`done`. Real rows read `done（r873 bm-b）`, `done（r1077 bm-b 全链落地…）`, `closed`, and one
row carried a measurement (`0.455 > null family p95 0.077`) where a status belongs. Exact
equality therefore reported **tech 9 / explore 10 = 19 drafts** on a queue that held **zero**
-- and that number sat next to the face's verdict (`AGENDA_DEBT`, shortfall 3), where it reads
as the opposite: "there are 19 things waiting to be frozen".

The fix is a three-state classifier, never a fourth guess:

- `done*` / `closed*` (prefix, case-insensitive) -> **consumed**;
- `open` / `pending` / `draft` / `queued` (prefix) -> **live**;
- anything else, including a number where a status belongs -> **unreadable**, printed **by row
  id** rather than counted either way.

Live face after the fix: live drafts **0/0**, consumed **50**, unreadable **1** (E16, whose
status cell holds a census result). That is the point: the shortfall of 3 is now provably topic
debt -- there is no hidden inventory to enqueue -- so the only correct action is to open a
topic, and the face stops offering the excuse of "maybe 19 rows are waiting".

Two reusable rules: (1) a status/enum reader must match a **prefix family** and name what it
could not read, because consumed rows accumulate commit notes and dates as they age; (2) when a
count feeds a verdict, publish the count the verdict acts on (live drafts) and keep the raw
bucket (`not_marked_done`) beside it under its own name. Read across: any `== "done"`-style
exact match on a column humans append to has this defect latent.

### E320 · An arm calibrated from the answer it tests, and a full-window verdict its own note reversed (r1202 bm-a)

r1196 measured the book's 8% vol target against "the same book held at a constant exposure", and the
constant was set to **the production path's own mean exposure (0.708)**. That arm won by +0.91 pp/yr
and the reading went into the research file. It cannot answer the question it is named after: 0.708 is
a number you only know after you have run the thing you are comparing against, so the arm is calibrated
from the answer. `etf_book_exposure_ladder` fixes the grid before the run (0.40 .. 1.00, seven fixed
fractions) and prices every one on four decision phases.

What that bought, on the same panel and the same identity leg:

| fixed fraction | full-window CAGR vs production | phases not worse | out-of-sample CAGR vs production |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.70 | +0.29 pp | 3/4 | -1.98 pp |
| 0.80 | +1.25 pp | 2/4 | -0.36 pp |
| 1.00 | +3.13 pp | 0/4 | +2.86 pp |

So the ladder reproduces r1196's headline on the full window (0.70 is no worse, in three of four
phases) and **the out-of-sample window reverses it**: production's own out-of-sample Sharpe (1.698)
is the highest of the eight arms, above the average-size match (1.598). The de-lever rule is not
"just a smaller book" once you ask the question where the answer can be wrong.

Two reusable rules.

1. **A control arm may not be calibrated from the thing it is controlling for.** If the comparator is
   derived from the treatment's realised path (mean exposure, mean turnover, realised bandwidth), it
   answers "is a book of that size equal to this book of that size", not "is the rule worth having".
   Fix the comparator's grid in advance and report the whole ladder -- the ladder is also the only way
   to see whether the champion is a plateau or a knife-edge.
2. **A pre-stated standard may not be dropped when it bites, and a frozen verdict may not be retuned
   when it flatters.** This face's own notes, written before the first run, said "a fixed fraction
   that only wins in sample is not an adoption". The first run is exactly that case. The honest
   resolution is not to re-tune `decide()` (that would be editing the criteria after seeing the
   answer) but to publish **both**: the frozen full-window verdict keeps its v1.0 criteria, and the
   adoption verdict applies the pre-stated rule, with the difference named on the page. A reader can
   then see that the two verdicts disagree and why, instead of receiving whichever one the author
   preferred.

Read across: any "constant / matched / average" control arm (matched exposure, matched turnover,
matched risk), and any face whose verdict block was frozen before the run -- ask whether its own
notes contain a test that the first run already failed.

### E321 · A window axis priced on one decision calendar is not a result (r1203 bm-a)

`results/etf_vol_target_sweep.json` swept window (30..120) × target and printed
"window=LOAD-BEARING" off a Sharpe spread -- with every cell priced on the **same** 20-bar
decision grid. r1325 had already shown what that costs: a buffer rule that won on one phase
lost 0.24 pp/yr on the phase one week later. The window axis therefore had never been tested;
the face that said it was load-bearing had measured one calendar.

`etf_book_vol_window_phase` re-prices the ladder on four decision phases (shift the panel
start inside one 20-bar cycle) and keeps the shipped-window arm honest by reproducing the
production card's own n12_vt08 metrics (0.096 / 0.99 / -0.1177 / 0.1017) to its printing
precision in the phase that prices the shipped span.

| window | shipped-window-relative Sharpe, by phase | phases earning +0.02 | OOS not worse |
|---|---|---|---|
| 30d | +0.067 / +0.143 / +0.103 / +0.050 | 4/4 | 4/4 |
| 45d | +0.062 / +0.040 / +0.059 / -0.037 | 3/4 | 3/4 |
| 90d | -0.139 / -0.053 / -0.111 / -0.104 | 0/4 | 0/4 |
| 120d | -0.161 / -0.066 / -0.138 / -0.129 | 0/4 | 0/4 |

So the earlier "LOAD-BEARING" word was right about the axis and wrong about the direction:
the gradient is monotone toward the **shortest** tested window in every phase and out of
sample, and the shipped 60d is nobody's optimum on any of the four calendars. The exposure
column keeps it honest -- a faster estimate also holds more of the book (mean scale
0.708→0.724 .. 0.735→0.740, delivered vol 10.2%→10.3%), so part of the return is just more
risk; the Sharpe column is the part that is not.

Three reusable rules.

1. **Any axis already sold as "swept" must be re-read for how many calendars it was swept
   on.** A sweep that varies one knob across a single decision grid confounds the knob with
   the grid's phase; publish the phase dimension or the sweep is a single observation with a
   table drawn around it.
2. **A monotone gradient is not an adoption at its edge.** The best cell here is the last
   cell tested, so the correct deliverable is "the shipped value is not load-bearing, and
   shorter deserves its own frozen test", not "set it to 30". Extrapolating past the edge of
   a frozen grid is how the next round fits the sample it just measured.
3. **The identity leg must claim only the span it can prove.** A shifted phase prices a
   different set of bars, so asserting the published card on every phase would have failed
   for the right reason and taught the wrong lesson; assert the shipped span, and label the
   rest "different priced span, not asserted". (A gate that fails on a definitional
   difference gets loosened, and then it stops working.)

Read across: any knob whose published value came from a one-grid sweep (vol target, vol
window, hysteresis buffer, momentum lookback), and any verdict block of the form "X is
load-bearing" -- ask which calendars, and whether the gradient points at the edge of the grid.
#### E321 addendum (same round, r1203 bm-a): two frozen rulers on one axis, and the one that matches the comparison

The first run of the face above passed its own frozen bar (a +0.02 Sharpe gain in 4 of 4
phases, no worse out of sample in 4 of 4) and printed `ADOPT_WINDOW`. Then it was read
against the older face on the same axis (`results/etf_book_voltarget_phase.json`), which
calls the same shorter-window win *within noise* -- because it compares a **paired** gain of
+1.50pp to the **control's own CAGR spread across phases** of 1.81pp. That is a
between-phase variance applied to a paired effect: inside a phase the two books hold the
same names on the same grid, so the calendar cancels.

So the difference of the two books' own return paths was bootstrapped (circular blocks of
one rebalance cycle, 1,000 draws, seeded by draw index):

| phase | paired mean | 5%-95% band | share > 0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | +0.83 pp/yr | [-0.10, +1.92] | 92.5% |
| 1 | +1.82 pp/yr | [+0.75, +3.12] | 99.9% |
| 2 | +1.19 pp/yr | [+0.10, +2.45] | 96.3% |
| 3 | +0.42 pp/yr | [-0.33, +1.21] | 83.1% |

`MIXED_ACROSS_PHASES`: positive everywhere, but the band still contains zero in two of four
phases. The direction is robust; the size is not separable, on either ruler. The shipped 60d
window therefore **stays**, and the face now says so in its own block instead of implying a
change it cannot prove.

Rule 4: **when two published faces answer the same question with different rulers, do not
delete either and do not let the friendlier one act.** Print both, name the ruler each one
uses, and settle the action on the ruler that matches the comparison being made (a paired
effect needs a paired band, not a cross-group spread). Rule 5, the corollary that matters
here: **a face may pass its own frozen bar and still not be allowed to change production.**
Frozen criteria protect against retuning after the fact; they do not make the criterion the
right measurement. Adoption takes both -- the pre-stated bar *and* a band that clears zero --
and a face that reports only the first is a wish with a checksum.

---

### E322 · A CAGR cannot tell a book from a jackpot, and two faces can publish different books

**Anchor**: `scripts/etf_book_tail_dependence.py` (selftest 10/10, `--check` recomputes the
verdict from the stored bar returns) · `results/etf_book_tail_dependence.json` ·
`research/ETF_BOOK_TAIL_DEPENDENCE.md` · chain leg `etf_book_tail_dependence` (r1204 bm-a).

**Reading 1 -- spread vs jackpot.** The production book (1,652 bars to 2026-10-30, 9.12%
cagr / 0.955 sharpe / -13.26% maxdd) has its 10 best bars carry only **6.3%** of the gross
up-move; removing them leaves **5.20%** cagr (57% of the headline), removing the best 5
leaves 6.86%. Against a seeded random-drop placebo the same k=10 removal typically costs
0.19pp (p50 9.45%), i.e. the best-ten cost 3.92pp -- real, but nowhere near wiping the book.
Best month (2025-08) out: 8.10% (-1.02pp). Verdict `ROBUST_TO_TAILS`.

**Three traps this face had to walk through, all of which had produced a wrong reading first:**
1. **Share of NET log growth is meaningless** -- a driftless random walk puts essentially all
   of its net growth in a handful of bars by construction, so the first fixture (and any
   ordinary book) reads "dependent". Divide by the **gross up-move** instead.
2. **Drop-best is always the worst drop**, so "dropping the best k sits at the 0th percentile
   of random drops" is arithmetic, not evidence. The placebo is there to price the magnitude
   ("best-ten cost 3.92pp, a typical ten cost 0.19pp"), never as a pass/fail band for the
